**As built (issue 47).** Notes and topics are `apps/helm/users/{uid}/notes/{id}` and `topics/{id}` (owner rule via the collection list), sealed with `NoteSealed` and `TopicSealed` as above; the hand-made ones are `source: 'typed'`, `createdBy: 'person'`, `projectIds: []`, and a topic is `docState: 'empty'`, `version: 0` with no sections (the document, versions and the assistant's filing come with later issues). Two whole-collection listeners (notes 1,000 ordered by `createdAt` descending, topics 100) open the sealed parts, skip a document that will not open and default every field a legacy document lacks; the store keeps `notes` and `topics` and `knowledgeSelectors.ts` derives the tree (A to Z at each level), each topic's notes and the loose ones on the device. `utils/topicTree.ts` is the one place depth, cycles and caps are decided (`moveRefusal`, `legalParents`, `createRefusal`, `depthAfterMove`, `liftChildren`, from the tree's shape and the parent's stored depth; `check:helm-topic-tree`). `moveTopic` is one transaction that re-reads the topic, its subtopics and the new parent, refuses with "changed" when any differs from what the caller saw, and writes the parent and every depth; its Undo is the same call back. `deleteTopic` removes the topic id from its notes (transaction chunks of 400, notes first), then in one transaction lifts each subtopic to the deleted topic's parent with depth minus one and deletes the topic, so a failure never deletes a topic notes still name (the cut notes are re-filed); its Undo sets the topic back under its id, moves the subtopics back only where each is still as the delete left it and re-files the notes that still exist and hold fewer than ten topics (a topic whose parent is gone comes back at the top level, and a step that fails after it is back says so). `editNote` writes only the fields the person changed into a re-read of the note (title and body in the live sealed payload, topics as a diff against the live list) and sets `checkedAt`; its Undo restores a field only while it is still as the edit left it. A move or a subtopic create re-checks depth only against the subtree this device has loaded, and the 20-subtopic cap is not re-checked inside the transaction. A topic or note made offline is a plain `setDoc` handed back unawaited; a subtopic, a rename, a move, a delete and an edit need a connection. The new `notes` and `topics` collections join the owner rule's list (`check:helm-rules`); both queries are single-field, so no index. `LinkedText` (shared) draws a note's web links as `ExternalLinkText`. The server's `sanitizeScreen` knows the `knowledge` and `topic` views, so a turn asked from them names its page (no note or topic ids travel yet).

### `Procedure`, `ProcedureVersion`, `ProcedureRun` [MVP]

Replace round 5's checklist `Artifact`. Briefings, research and other drafted documents (round 5's other artifact types) become topic documents the assistant drafts (**[Next]**, `source: 'assistantDraft'`), so the `artifacts` collection is not built.

```ts
interface Procedure {
  stepState: Record<string, { order: number; groupId: string | null }>; // readable order
  groupOrder: string[];                // ungrouped steps first (one pure helper orders every surface)
  projectIds: string[];                // a work procedure filed under a project ("Starting a new initiative")
  version: number;
  runCount: number; lastRunAt: number | null;
  createdBy: 'person' | 'nomos'; executionId: string | null;
  createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null; archivedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                      // ProcedureSealed
}
interface ProcedureSealed {
  title: string;                       // "Packing for a trip"
  steps: Record<string, { text: string; detail: string | null }>; // same keys as stepState; text ≤200, detail ≤500
  groups: Record<string, { name: string }>;
  sources: SourceRef[];                // notes or topics it was drafted from
}
interface ProcedureVersion {           // procedures/{id}/versions/{n}; like NoteVersion
  version: number; restoredFromVersion: number | null; createdBy: 'person' | 'nomos';
  reason: 'created' | 'editedByHand' | 'revisedByAsking' | 'restored' | 'fromRun';
  sourceCaptureId: string | null; executionId: string | null; createdAt: number;
  sealed: Sealed;                      // { title, steps, groups, changeSummary }
}

type RunAttachment =
  | { kind: 'project'; id: string }
  | { kind: 'task'; id: string }
  | { kind: 'synced'; id: string }     // a Waypoint trip or a calendar or app event
  | null;

interface ProcedureRun {
  procedureId: string;
  procedureVersion: number;            // the version it started from
  attachedTo: RunAttachment;
  dueOn: number | null;                // date-only: the trip's or event's first day, else null
  stepState: Record<string, { order: number; groupId: string | null; doneAt: number | null; isExtra: boolean }>;
  groupOrder: string[];
  startedAt: number; finishedAt: number | null;
  createdBy: 'person' | 'nomos'; executionId: string | null; lastEditedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                      // { title, steps: Record<id, { text }>, groups, attachedLabel ("Lisbon trip, May 3", kept if the trip goes) }
}
```

A run copies the procedure's steps when it starts, so editing the procedure never changes a run in progress or a past one. A tick is `stepState.<id>.doneAt` alone (no version, offline-friendly). A step added "just this time" is `isExtra` on the run; "Add it to the procedure too" makes a new procedure version (`reason: 'fromRun'`). Section 25 has the flows.

**As built (issue 54).** Procedures only; runs come later. `Procedure` is `{ stepState, groupOrder, version, createdBy, sourceCaptureId, sourceTaskId, executionId, createdAt, lastEditedAt, sealed }`: `projectIds`, `runCount`, `lastRunAt` and `archivedAt` wait for runs and filing, and `sources` is not kept. `sourceTaskId` names the task whose steps it was made from (null otherwise); a task with one offers "Open {title}" instead of making another, and the server refuses a second create from the same task. `ProcedureVersion` carries its own `stepState` and `groupOrder`, `reason` is `'created' | 'personEdit' | 'revisedOnRequest' | 'restored'` (`fromRun` comes with runs), and its sealed part adds `requestQuote` (the turn's words when it was revised by asking). Caps in the rules: 60 steps, 12 groups, `version` an integer ≥1, `createdBy` `person` or `nomos`. Steps are ordered by `groupSteps` (`utils/stepGroups.ts`, a server copy guarded by `check:server-copies`).

**As built (issue 55).** `ProcedureRun` at `users/{uid}/procedureRuns/{runId}` is `{ procedureId, procedureVersion, attachedTo, dueOn, stepState, groupOrder, startedAt, finishedAt, createdBy, executionId, lastEditedAt, sealed }`. `attachedTo` is `{ kind: 'project' | 'synced', id }` or null (a task is not offered); `dueOn` is date-only (a trip's or event's first day). `stepState` copies the procedure's step and group ids with `doneAt` and `isExtra`; the sealed part carries the steps' text and detail, the group names and the label of what it was for, so a run reads the same after its procedure or trip changes. Caps in the rules: 80 steps, 12 groups, `attachedTo` only those two kinds; the device and the server also hold extras to 20. The procedure keeps no run count; runs are listed by query (indexes on `procedureId` and `attachedTo.id`, each with `startedAt` desc), and open runs (`finishedAt == null`, up to 50) are listened to eagerly. Deleting a procedure deletes its runs that were on their own (`attachedTo` null) with it, and Undo brings them back; runs on a project or trip stay there. Open runs whose procedure is gone are kept out of the model's context. `ProcedureVersion.reason` adds `'fromRun'`.

### `Media` [MVP]

```ts
type MediaKind = 'photo' | 'pdf' | 'voiceNote' | 'markdown';   // [Next] 'video'
type MediaStatus = 'uploading' | 'received' | 'kept' | 'discarded';
type MediaRole = 'attachment' | 'photo';                       // photo: a who's or place's picture, picked by hand or kept from a turn

interface Media {
  kind: MediaKind;
  role: MediaRole;
  status: MediaStatus;
  mimeType: string;                    // of the original, before sealing ("image/jpeg", "application/pdf", "audio/webm", "text/markdown")
  byteSize: number;                    // sealed size
  width: number | null; height: number | null; durationMs: number | null; pageCount: number | null;
  hasThumb: boolean;
  attachedTo: RecordRef[];             // the records it shows on; [] while only in the conversation
  sourceCaptureId: string | null;
  executionId: string | null;
  decidedBy: 'nomos' | 'person' | null;
  takenAt: number | null;              // a photo's own date when the device can read it; feeds the life timeline later
  createdAt: number; decidedAt: number | null;
  deleteAfter: number | null;          // discarded: decidedAt + 24 h; uploading: createdAt + 24 h
  sealed: Sealed;                      // MediaSealed
}
interface MediaSealed {
  fileName: string | null;             // ≤120
  caption: string | null;              // the assistant's one line of what it is (≤200): "Receipt from Sweet Layer, $1,800"
  text: string | null;                 // a Markdown file's text (≤20,000) or a voice note's transcript (≤5,000, kept even when the audio is let go); a PDF's text is never stored
  bytesNonce: string | null; thumbNonce: string | null; // nonces for the sealed Storage objects
  keyId: string;                       // the data key that sealed the bytes
}
```

A voice note's transcript becomes its capture's `text`, so it outlives the audio; the audio itself is `discarded` by code (never by the model) unless the person taps Keep the recording (PRODUCT.md light call, round 6).

Bytes are sealed on the device with `encryptBytes` (`@lib/security`, Runway's receipt path), so Storage only ever holds opaque bytes. Section 23 has the flows, caps and deletion.

**As built (issue 37).** Photos only, so `Media` carries just the fields photos use: `kind` (`'photo'`), `role`, `status`, `mimeType`, `hasThumb`, `attachedTo` (`{ kind: 'who' | 'place'; id }[]`, not the screen context's `RecordRef`), `decidedBy`, `createdAt`, `decidedAt`, `deleteAfter`, and `sealed` holding `bytesNonce`, `thumbNonce` and `keyId` (a photo sealed under another key is refused, not shown). `byteSize`, `width`, `height`, `durationMs`, `pageCount`, `takenAt`, `sourceCaptureId`, `executionId` and the sealed `fileName`, `caption` and `text` arrive with the issue that reads them. A who's `mediaIds` holds every photo it keeps (at most 12) and `photoMediaId` names the main one among them; a place's `photoMediaIds` is the ordered list and its first is the cover. The rule for `…/media/{mediaId}` is its own match (owner-only, with the readable shape checked), not the wildcard's list.

**As built (issue 53).** PDFs, voice notes and Markdown files. `Media.durationMs` (a recording's length as the device measured it) and `Media.pageCount` (a PDF's pages as the device counted them; the server counts again) are new, null on a photo. `MediaSealed.bytesNonce` is now nullable: a Markdown file has no Storage object, its text is sealed in the document as `text` (≤20,000) with its `fileName`; a voice note's `text` is its transcript, sealed by the server (≤5,000); a PDF's text is never stored and is read again on each turn that opens it. `MediaAttachment.kind` adds `'note'`: an imported Markdown file is kept on the note made from it, so deleting the note releases it for 24 hours like a task's photo. The listener reads a missing `kind` as `'photo'` and missing new fields as null. The media rule now accepts the four kinds, each with its own content type (`image/*`, `application/pdf`, `audio/*`, `text/markdown` or `text/plain`).

**As built (issue 52).** Photos sent in a turn. New fields: `Capture.attachmentIds` (the turn's photos in pick order, at most 5, `[]` on a turn made before), `Media.sourceCaptureId` (the turn that sent it; null on a who's or place's photo), `Media.executionId` (the execution that kept it, so Undo finds it; null until one did) and `MediaSealed.caption` (the assistant's one line about a kept photo, at most 200; null on any other). `MediaAttachment.kind` widens to `'who' | 'place' | 'task' | 'fact'`; `PhotoOwner` (`who | place`) stays the type of a who's or place's picture. Keep targets are tasks and facts only: projects, a who's own photo ("this is Dana") and facts noted in the same turn come later, as do MCP and file uploads into notes or topics (`asNote`/`asTopic` are not built).

| Moment | `status` | `deleteAfter` |
|---|---|---|
| Upload started on the device | `uploading` (then `received`) | now + 24 h, so an upload that never finished is swept |
| Sent with a turn | `received` | `null`; `sourceCaptureId` set |
| Kept (by the assistant, or "Keep it") | `kept` | `null` |
| Read and let go | `discarded` | now + 24 h, never sooner |
| Removed from its record, or its keep taken back by Undo | `discarded` | now + 24 h |
| A held or failed turn's photos, when it is made a task as written | `discarded` | now + 24 h |

The server reads a photo only for the capture it was sent with (`sourceCaptureId`) and only while `received`. Let-go photos are not Undo effects; a keep is (its effect names the medium, and Undo makes it `discarded`). The index `media status ASC, deleteAfter ASC` (collection scope, in `firestore.indexes.json`) serves the sweep.

### `StyleProfile` [MVP]

```ts
type StyleScope = 'general' | 'occasion' | 'audience';

interface StyleProfile {
  scope: StyleScope;
  audienceWhoId: string | null;        // audience: the who it is about (texts to Dana); never made from mail alone
  learnedFrom: ('helmText' | 'sentMail')[];
  sampleCount: number;                 // how many pieces of writing it rests on
  personEditedAt: number | null;
  createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                      // StyleSealed
}
interface StyleSealed {
  label: string;                       // "General", "Work email", "Texts to Dana" (≤40)
  traits: Record<string, { text: string; isPersonSet: boolean; exampleCount: number }>; // ≤12, ≤120 chars: "Short sentences", "Signs off 'Cheers,'"; exampleCount: how many pieces of writing showed it (0 when the person wrote it)
  avoid: string[];                     // ≤6, ≤80 chars: "No exclamation marks"
}
```

No sample of the person's writing is stored: only traits written as descriptions, each ≤120 characters, and a sign-off of at most 30 characters may be quoted. Section 26 has the learner.

**As built (issue 56).** Only `style/general` is written (`learnedFrom: ['helmText']`); the rules let the owner alone read and write it, with the eight keys above and `scope` one of the three. A trait's key is the learner's short id, or `you-<uuid>` for one the person adds. `HelmProfile.styleLearnedThroughAt` marks the newest capture the last learning read, so the device counts only typed captures after it. The learner reads typed captures through the index `inputMode ASC, createdAt DESC`.

### `Execution` (Activity) [MVP]

As round 5, with `HelmCollection` now `'captures' | 'tasks' | 'projects' | 'who' | 'keyDates' | 'places' | 'facts' | 'notes' | 'topics' | 'procedures' | 'procedureRuns' | 'media' | 'style' | 'planDays'`.

```ts
type ExecutionState = 'proposed' | 'succeeded' | 'failed' | 'rejected' | 'executing' | 'verificationRequired';
type ActionCategory = 'organize' | 'remember' | 'plan' | 'draft' | 'answer' | 'undo' | 'outbound'; // outbound: [Next]

interface Execution {
  captureId: string | null; actor: 'nomos' | 'person'; category: ActionCategory;
  autonomyLevel: AutonomyLevel; state: ExecutionState;
  effectRefs: { op: 'create' | 'update' | 'delete'; collection: HelmCollection; docId: string }[]; // capped 40; 'delete' only in an undo's own execution
  undoesExecutionId: string | null; undoneByExecutionId: string | null;
  questionRef: { captureId: string; questionId: string } | null; // round 6c: the question this answer's effect came from
  errorCode: string | null; createdAt: number; decidedAt: number | null; finishedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                      // { summary, commands, effects: { written, previous }[], undoReport }
}
```

`written` and `previous` hold plain values keyed by field path (`sealed.<field>` for sealed ones). Every task effect (create, update, complete) carries `sealed.title` with the task's title at that moment, so a result row can name its task; Undo compares the opened current value with what was written. A topic change a turn makes (added, revised, moved, grouped) is an `update` whose `written`/`previous` hold the version numbers (or a placement's `parentId` and `depth`); Undo restores that version as a new one (section 24). A `writeHelmTopic` batch write carries no effects; its Undo is a restore in History. A procedure's change is an `update` whose previous value is the version number. A kept medium's effect is its `status` and `attachedTo`; Undo sets it back to `discarded` with `deleteAfter`.

### `PlanDay` [MVP]

```ts
interface PlanDay {                    // id = "YYYY-MM-DD" in profile.timeZone
  date: number;                        // date-only, UTC midnight
  slots: Record<string, PlanSlot>;     // readable: Keep / Another time / Not today stay dotted-path writes
  isRestDay: boolean;
  restDayOverriddenAt: number | null;  // "Plan my day anyway"
  generatedAt: number | null;
  calendarReadAt: number | null;      // round 5: the Calendar category's lastOkAt the plan was made from
  sealed: Sealed;                      // PlanDaySealed
}
interface PlanSlot {
  taskId: string;
  startAt: number | null;              // instant; null = "Any time today" (no calendar)
  endAt: number | null;
  status: 'suggested' | 'kept' | 'notToday';
  source: 'nomos' | 'person';
  decidedAt: number | null;
}
interface PlanDaySealed {
  reasons: Record<string, string>;     // slot id → rule-built chip ("20 min · fits before the tasting")
  calendarSnapshot: { eventRef: string; title: string; startAt: number; endAt: number; isAllDay: boolean }[];
}
```

The snapshot is rewritten whenever that day's plan is generated or re-run and whenever Today shows the day with a newer calendar read, and never once the day is past. Done-ness is not stored on a slot: a past day shows a task done when its `completedAt` falls inside that day.

### `DayAims` (the day's aims and its wind-down) [Next]

```ts
interface DayAims {                    // id = "YYYY-MM-DD" in profile.timeZone, same key as PlanDay
  date: number;                        // date-only, UTC midnight
  state: 'open' | 'answered' | 'dismissed';          // the morning card
  windDownState: 'waiting' | 'ready' | 'done' | 'dismissed';  // 'ready' is set by the server
  lookAt: number | null;               // the next instant the server must act on this day; null when nothing waits
  rolledAt: number | null;             // set by the rollover, so it runs once
  sealed: Sealed;                      // DayAimsSealed
}
interface DayAimsSealed {
  aims: { aimId: string; taskId: string; order: number; lengthIsGuess: boolean; broughtFrom: string | null }[];
  windDown: Record<string, { result: 'done' | 'tonight' | 'wontHappen'; choice: 'tomorrow' | 'later' | 'dropped' | null; reason: StruggleSignal | null; saidSpanId: string | null }>;
}
```

- **An aim is a task** ("Every fact has one home"): the document holds the task's id, the aim's place in the day and the day's own result, never the task's title, length, steps or done-ness. The length shown is the task's `durationMinutes` (a fix writes it there); `lengthIsGuess` is only whether it was guessed. Done is the task's `completedAt`, read, never copied; `windDown` keeps the result the person saw and the choice made. The slot (time) is the day's `PlanDay` slot for that `taskId`, so the planner, Keep, Another time and the calendar sync keep one slot per aim.
- **One document per day, not per aim**, like `HabitDay`: Today and the strip read the same 7 to 14 days by id range; the card reads one. Readable fields are only what the server needs without the key: the day key, `state`, `windDownState`, `lookAt` and `rolledAt`. The aims, their order, the results and the reasons are one sealed payload, rewritten whole by the device in a transaction when online; the server opens it only in the cases below. `reason` is one of the fixed struggle signals (section 28, "Learning from what is said"); a said reason keeps its verbatim span id only, never a copy of the words.
- **Readers default what an older document lacks**: `windDownState` `'waiting'`, `rolledAt` and `lookAt` null, `windDown` `{}`. A day with no aims says nothing and gets no document.

### `Habit` and `HabitDay` (habits and their check-ins) [Next]


A habit is something the person (or someone they care for) does again and again at times of their day, where a missed time simply passes: "pills, morning and evening", "water, through the day", "practise piano four times a week". It is not a task: it is never overdue, never piles up, never enters the planner's ranking or the open-task counts, and has no `status: 'done'`. Section 28 draws the line against Issue 70's repeating tasks, and describes a habit's life, its reminders and importance.

```ts
type Weekday = 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6;     // 0 = Sunday, as getUTCDay
type Cadence =                                 // src/utils/cadenceUtils.ts (general, no app words)
  | { kind: 'everyDay' }
  | { kind: 'onWeekdays'; weekdays: Weekday[] }              // [1, 3, 5]
  | { kind: 'timesPerWeek'; count: number }                  // 1–6, any days of the week (Monday to Sunday)
  | { kind: 'everyNDays'; days: number; anchor: number };    // 2–30; anchor date-only, UTC midnight
type HabitStatus = 'active' | 'paused' | 'ended' | 'retired';  // 'retired' = let go once Formed (never a care habit); 'ended' = stopped for any other reason
type HabitStage = 'building' | 'holding' | 'formed' | 'buildingAgain'; // round 3: product's thresholds, design's words (section 28); buildingAgain counts as building
type DayPart = 'morning' | 'midday' | 'afternoon' | 'evening' | 'bedtime';
type ReminderPace = 'adaptive' | 'start' | 'late';             // round 3, replaces round 2's ReminderLevel
type HabitEventKind = 'started' | 'reminderOn' | 'reminderOff' | 'reminderPace' | 'stage' | 'paused' | 'resumed'
  | 'retired' | 'broughtBack' | 'ended' | 'restarted' | 'nameShown' | 'nameHidden';

interface HabitReminder {                       // readable: the server's planner reads all of it
  isOn: boolean;
  pace: ReminderPace;                           // adaptive: follows the stage (start → late → none); start / late: pinned there
                                                // ("Keep reminding at the start", "Keep reminding me" on a Formed habit); a care habit is never adaptive
  anyTimeAt: string | null;                     // "HH:mm" for a once-a-day habit with no window; null = the middle of Your day
  allowQuietHours: boolean;                     // the person asked for a time inside their quiet hours anyway
  turnedOnAt: number;
  easedAt: number | null;                       // a care habit's "Fewer reminders?" accepted (pace start → late); null otherwise
}

interface Habit {                               // habits/{habitId}
  status: HabitStatus;
  cadence: Cadence;
  windowState: Record<string, {                 // two-map shape; {} = any time of the day ("through the day")
    start: string; end: string;                 // floating "HH:mm" in the profile's zone; end may pass midnight, never the 03:00 rollover
    part: DayPart | null;                       // the named part it came from (null for a said time); a reminder's words use it
    order: number;
    editedAt: number | null;                    // evidence about this window counts only after it (section 28)
  }>;
  timesPerDay: number | null;                   // a tally across the day ("8 glasses", ≤20); null = once per window
  projectId: string | null;                     // the goal it is the regular step of (a project with kind 'goal')
  forWhoId: string | null;                      // whose habit it is when not the person's own (Mom's doses); null = the person
  isCare: boolean;                              // health or care: set by code, learning only asks, never retired (section 28)
  startsOn: number;                             // date-only
  endsOn: number | null;                        // date-only, inclusive ("for 10 days")
  pausedUntil: number | null;                   // date-only; a pause with an end resumes on that day, on the server or the first open, whichever comes first
  reminder: HabitReminder | null;               // null = never turned on
  lockScreenName: string | null;                // round 3: PLAIN TEXT, never sealed: the habit's name as the lock screen may show it (≤60),
                                                // set only while "Show its name on the lock screen" is on; null = fixed words (the default)
  stage: HabitStage | null;                     // round 3: the last weekly judgement; 'building' on create; null for a carer's habit (forWhoId set: no stage);
                                                // a health habit (isCare, the person's own) stops at 'holding', never 'formed'
  stageAt: number;
  stageWeek: string | null;                     // round 3: Monday day key of the last week judged, so the device and the server judge a week once
  rejudgeFrom: string | null;                   // Monday key of the earliest judged week a late check-in landed in; cleared by the re-judge
  careAskedAt: number | null;                   // "Is this a medicine?" asked (once, ever)
  untoldSince: number | null;                   // round 3: oldest server-made change (stage, pace, a pause ended) the device hasn't yet written to Activity
  formedAt: number | null;                      // when it first reached Formed (kept through a bring-back, for the history)
  formedSummary: { doneCount: number; dueCount: number; since: number } | null; // the count it leaves behind, frozen at retirement
  history: { kind: HabitEventKind; at: number; by: 'person' | 'nomos'; pace: ReminderPace | null;
             stage: HabitStage | null }[];      // newest 30; enums only, so it stays readable
  createdBy: 'person' | 'nomos';
  sourceCaptureId: string | null; executionId: string | null;
  createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null; pausedAt: number | null; endedAt: number | null;
  retiredAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                               // HabitSealed
}
interface HabitSealed { name: string; emoji: string | null; notes: string | null;
  medicine: { name: string; dose: string | null } | null;   // a care habit's medicine (≤60) and dose as said (≤40, a DOSE_PATTERN span: "25 mg", "2 tablets"); null when not care or not said; readers default null
  windows: Record<string, { label: string }>;   // "Morning", "With dinner" (≤30); same keys as windowState
  importance: Importance | null; }              // round 3: section 28, "Importance"; null = nothing said

interface Importance {                          // round 3: sealed inside a habit's or a project's payload, never readable
  rank: number;                                 // > 0 said to matter (higher comes first); < 0 said it can wait; equal ranks = said to matter the same
  words: string | null;                         // the person's own words, copied by code from the turn (≤120): "the wedding matters most"
  saidAt: number;                               // instant
  until: number | null;                         // date-only, inclusive: "matters most until June" (Jun 30); past it the entry reads as unplaced
  captureId: string | null;                     // the turn it came from; null when set on a sheet
}

interface HabitDay {                            // habitDays/{dayKey}: one per local day, every habit in it
  date: number;                                 // date-only, UTC midnight of dayKey
  isInUse: boolean;                             // round 3: the device set it at the day's first capture, task tick or check (the "Helm was in use" rule, readable for the server)
  entries: Record<string, {                     // habitId →
    checks: Record<string, HabitCheck>;         // windowId, or 'day' for a habit with no windows
    count: number;                              // timesPerDay habits: the day's tally; 0 otherwise
    lastCountAt: number | null;                 // instant of the newest tally
    reminded: Record<string, number>;           // round 3: windowId (or 'day') → instant a reminder for it actually went out; written only by the sender's Helm hook
    followUpAt: number | null;                  // when "How was it?" was asked about this habit today (at most once a day); null = not asked
    followUpState: 'asked' | 'answered' | 'skipped' | null;   // a skipped question is never asked again that day
    noteKeys: string[];                         // windowIds (or 'day') with a note in the sealed payload, so the line shows a note mark without the key
  }>;
  sealed: Sealed | null;                        // HabitDaySealed; null = no note that day
}
interface HabitDaySealed {                      // the person's check-in notes, word for word
  notes: Record<string, {                       // "habitId/windowId" →
    text: string;                               // verbatim (≤500): typed on the sheet, or the span said with the check-in
    at: number;                                 // instant
    captureId: string | null;                   // the turn it was said in; null = typed on the sheet
  }>;
}
interface HabitCheck {
  status: 'done' | 'skipped';                   // skipped = the person said "not today"; a time that just passes writes nothing
  at: number;                                   // instant it happened (a said time, read by code), not when it was typed
  by: 'person' | 'nomos';
  executionId: string | null;                   // set when the assistant wrote it, for Undo
}

interface HelmReminderQueue {                   // round 3: apps/helm/reminderQueue/{uid}; Admin SDK only, written only by planHelmReminders
  lookAt: number | null;                        // the next instant a run must plan this person; null = no habit reminds
  plannedAt: number;
  plannedFor: number | null;                    // the profile's habitRemindersChangedAt this plan answered (equality skip, section 28)
  leaseUntil: number | null;                    // a run holds the person for 2 minutes so two runs never plan them at once
  dayKey: string;                               // the habit day the slots belong to
  stageWeek: string | null;                     // Monday key of the last week the server judged for this person
  slots: Record<string, {                       // slot key = `${dayKey}_${HHmm}` of the fire time → (≤6)
    reminderId: string;                         // reminders/{id}, a random id, never derived
    fireAt: number;
    refs: string[];                             // "habitId/windowId" it covers (windowId 'day' with no window); ≤30
    part: DayPart | null;
  }>;
}
```

- **Readers default what an older document lacks**: `reminder` null, `stage` `'building'` (null when `forWhoId` is set), `stageAt` `createdAt`, `stageWeek`, `untoldSince`, `lockScreenName`, `formedAt`, `formedSummary`, `pausedUntil` and `retiredAt` null, `history` `[]`, a window's `part` null, a day's `isInUse` read as "has any check", `reminded` `{}`, a sealed `importance` null. An unknown `status` reads as `'active'`, an unknown `stage` as `'building'`, an unknown or round-2 pace as `'adaptive'` (`'start'` for a care habit).
- **Check-in notes are the day's one sealed payload** (replacing round 1's "a note on one check-in is Later"). A note typed on the habit's sheet ("half a dose") or said with a check-in ("took my pills, felt dizzy": the `checkIn` command's `noteSpanId`, a verbatim span copied by code) is kept word for word in `HabitDaySealed.notes`, with `noteKeys` readable so the line can show a note mark without the key. Ticks, tallies, `reminded` and `isInUse` stay readable one-field writes that work offline and that the server's reminder code reads; it never opens the payload. A note reseals the payload from the slice's opened copy of that day, in a transaction when online; two devices adding a note to the same day while both offline keep the later one (stated, rare). A day with no note keeps `sealed: null`. Removing a note deletes the observations that cite it (section 28, "Where things live").
- **One document per day, not per check-in.** Today and "this week" read 7 to 14 small documents by id range, the habit page 12 weeks (84), a weekly stage judgement up to 16 weeks (112). A check-in is `setDoc(ref, { date, isInUse: true, entries: { [habitId]: { checks: { [windowId]: check } } } }, { merge: true })`, which creates the day if it is missing and deep-merges, so it works offline and two devices ticking different windows both land; un-ticking is `updateDoc` with `deleteField()` on `entries.<habitId>.checks.<windowId>`. A tally is `increment(1)` on `entries.<habitId>.count` beside `lastCountAt`. The first capture or task tick of a day merges `isInUse: true` once (remembered in memory for the day). No execution for the person's own ticks (as with task ticks); a quick action that locks nothing.
- **Why so much of a habit is readable** (round 3, owner: reminders and stages keep going from the server). The server's planner and the sender's check read the windows, cadence, status, pause, reminder setting, stage, `isCare`, and each day's checks, counts, `reminded` and `isInUse`, and nothing else; none of them says what the habit is. The words (name, emoji, notes, window labels, importance) stay sealed. **The one exception is `lockScreenName`**, a plain copy of the name the person chose to show on the lock screen; it exists only while that switch is on, is written in the same update as any rename while on, and is set to null (the field kept, the words gone) the moment the switch goes off, the habit ends, retires or is deleted (section 14).
- **A formed habit's moment**: retiring a Formed habit writes one `KeyDate` moment on the self who (`kind: 'custom'`, precision `day`, the sealed label from a template), and `KeyDate` gains a readable `sourceHabitId: string | null` (null on every other date) so Undo and a bring-back can find it. A care habit is never retired, so it writes no moment on anyone's page.
- **Caps** (device and server, outside the rules): 30 active or paused habits, 4 windows each, a name of 60 characters (so `lockScreenName` too), a window label of 30, `timesPerDay` ≤ 20, `history` 30, `reminded` one entry per window; retired and ended habits don't count toward the 30 and are kept (up to 200, oldest ended first past it). At most 6 habit reminders a day reach the phone (section 28).

### `Pattern` (what Helm has noticed, guardrails, habit-life offers, and what the person said about each) [Next]


One record shape carries every offer Helm makes from what it has seen, so every one shares the states, the back-off, the signature and Undo (section 28). Round 2 adds the habit-life kinds and the guardrail kinds; no second collection.

```ts
type PatternKind =
  // noticing (round 1)
  | 'windowMissed'           // a habit's window rarely kept on one kind of day ("evening pills, Tuesdays")
  | 'doneOutsideWindow'      // a habit kept, but nearly always outside its window, on the same side (suggests moving it)
  | 'plannedSlotMissed'      // Helm's plan slots in one kind of day-part rarely done, moved or Not today'd
  | 'offerDeclined'          // Issue 69: a Not now on an offer (a plan suggestion, a run, a link, a role, a grouping)
  // a habit's life (round 2)
  | 'habitFormed'            // a week after it reached Formed: offer to let it go from Today (retire it)
  | 'reminderEase'           // a care habit that would be Holding: ask before its reminders step back (care never fades on its own)
                             // round 3: 'habitSlipping' is gone: slipping is the automatic Building again, and adaptive reminders step back up with it
  // learning from what is said
  | 'saidStruggling'         // owner review 2 (was saidReluctance): a struggling signal about one habit on 3+ different days in 2 weeks (found on the device)
  | 'saidWorking'            // a working signal about one habit on 3+ different days in 2 weeks: keep this time, maybe grow it
  | 'saidLink'               // a nightly-pass candidate: what was said lines up with when a habit or goal slips ("piano slips on days you said work ran late")
  // guardrails (round 2)
  | 'tooManyGoals'           // more goals running than the person keeps up with
  | 'deadlineWontFit'        // the open work before a date is more than the free time left before it
  | 'starved'                // something that matters more has had no time while something else took it
  | 'busyStretch';           // the calendar ahead is full; hold off starting, or pause a goal through it
type PatternFamily = 'noticed' | 'habitLife' | 'guardrail'; // derived from kind in constants.ts, never stored
type PatternBucket =
  | { kind: 'weekdayPart'; weekday: Weekday; part: DayPart }
  | { kind: 'shift'; relation: 'beforeShift' | 'afterShift'; part: DayPart }  // only once shift calendars are marked
  | { kind: 'stretch'; from: number; to: number }                             // round 2: date-only days of a busy stretch
  | { kind: 'none' };
type PatternState = 'candidate' | 'suggested' | 'accepted' | 'declined' | 'undone' | 'retired'; // 'candidate' = found, waiting for its turn under pacing
type PatternHandling = 'actAndTell' | 'suggest' | 'askOnly';     // set by code from the kind and subject (section 28); guardrails and habit-life offers are never actAndTell
type PatternMoment = 'daily' | 'onStart';                        // round 2: found by the daily check, or at the moment something was started
type PatternEffect =
  | { kind: 'plannerAvoid'; bucket: PatternBucket }                // Helm's own planning stops using that day-part
  | { kind: 'moveWindow'; habitId: string; windowId: string; start: string; end: string }
  | { kind: 'retireHabit'; habitId: string }                       // habitFormed's "Let it go" (round 3: "Keep counting" is a no, so keepFormed and remindAgain are gone)
  | { kind: 'easeReminders'; habitId: string }                     // reminderEase's "Fewer": a care habit's pace start → late, easedAt set
  | { kind: 'pause'; collection: 'projects' | 'habits'; id: string; until: number | null } // round 2: a guardrail's "Pause it" (until a date-only day, or open-ended)
  | { kind: 'lowerCadence'; habitId: string; cadence: Cadence }    // "a smaller version": one fewer time a week or weekday, computed by code
  | { kind: 'talkItThrough'; start: 'differentTime' | 'smaller' | 'somethingNew' } // opens the conversation about the habit with that start; writes nothing itself
  | { kind: 'keepTime'; habitId: string; windowId: string }        // saidWorking's "Keep it here": moving suggestions for that window need twice the evidence while accepted
  | { kind: 'raiseCadence'; habitId: string; cadence: Cadence }    // saidWorking's "A bit more": one more time a week, computed by code; never on care, never past daily
  | { kind: 'none' };                                              // ask-only, or a guardrail answered "Keep going"
type PatternEvidence =                                             // round 2: a union; round 1's shape is 'occurrences'
  | { kind: 'occurrences'; from: number; to: number; matched: number; total: number; dayKeys: string[] } // dayKeys ≤10
  | { kind: 'load'; measured: number; limit: number; unit: 'goals' | 'minutes' | 'days'; asOf: number;
      refs: string[] }                                             // ids it weighed (≤10): the goals, the tasks, the stretch's day keys
  | { kind: 'said'; from: number; to: number; matched: number; dayKeys: string[];
      observationIds: string[] };                                  // ≤10 each; the quotes stay on the observations, never copied here

interface Pattern {                             // patterns/{patternId}; id = 'pt_' + first 24 hex of sha256(signature)
  kind: PatternKind;
  subject: { collection: 'habits' | 'tasks' | 'projects' | 'procedures' | 'topics' | 'who' | 'planner';
             id: string | null; windowId: string | null;
             otherId: string | null };          // round 2: starved's "what took the time" / deadlineWontFit's new thing that pushed it over; null otherwise
  bucket: PatternBucket;
  signature: string;                            // kind|collection|id|windowId|otherId|bucket, ids and enums only: the same thing is always one record
  moment: PatternMoment;                        // round 2; 'daily' on a record from before
  state: PatternState;
  handling: PatternHandling;
  acceptedBy: 'person' | 'auto' | null;
  effect: PatternEffect;
  evidence: PatternEvidence;                    // as last shown; a record from before reads as 'occurrences'
  reasons: { kind: PatternKind; evidence: PatternEvidence }[]; // round 3, guardrails only: every reason that met the bar, the record's own kind first (≤3); [] otherwise
  evidenceSince: number;                        // instant: only signals after it count (first shown, last no, retire, reopen)
  sourceKeys: string[];                         // 'observations/{id}', 'reflections/{dayKey}' it rests on (≤20), for the delete cascade; [] for tick-only kinds
  declineCount: number;                         // Not now and Undo each add one; reopen sets 0
  nextEligibleOn: number | null;                // date-only: the first day it may be shown again; null = no wait
  offReason: 'thirdNo' | 'askedToStop' | null;  // set = never shown again unless the person reopens it
  history: { to: PatternState | 'off' | 'reopened'; at: number; by: 'person' | 'nomos' }[]; // newest 20
  seenAt: number | null;                        // when "What I've noticed" last showed it, for a new-since dot
  executionId: string | null;                   // the act-and-tell or accept execution, for Undo
  createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null; stateAt: number;
  sealed: Sealed;                               // PatternSealed
}
interface PatternSealed {                       // every line code-built from templates in constants.ts, sealed as shown
  statement: string;                            // "Evening pills don't tend to happen on Tuesdays" / "You have 4 goals going"
  evidenceLine: string;                         // "Not checked on 4 of the last 5 Tuesdays" / "About 9 h of tasks, about 6 h free before Jun 3"
  reasonLine: string | null;                    // only from records, never mood or health: "Those were days after a night shift"
}
```

**Owner review 2** (replacing "candidates are never stored"): a candidate that met its bar but is waiting under pacing is written `candidate`, sealed lines included, so another device or a new phone shows the same next thing and nothing found is lost; the daily check re-measures it and deletes it when its evidence no longer meets the bar. A quiet Helm still writes nothing. Caps: 500 records (an off one is kept, since it is what keeps the "never again"; past the cap the oldest `retired` go first, then the oldest `accepted` guardrails whose subject is gone).

`HelmProfile` gains (Next; each reads its default on a profile from before): `habitRemindersChangedAt: number | null` (round 3: stamped by the device, in the same write, whenever anything a reminder depends on changes; the server's planner picks the person up within 5 minutes; section 28), `habitRemindersIdleAt: number | null` (round 3, written by the planner under the idle rule, cleared by the device's next check or capture; section 28 "Reminders"), `patternsCheckedOn: string | null` (the day key of the last daily check, so a second device that day skips it), `isLearningOn: boolean` (round 2, default `true`: noticing and guardrails; off stops both and shows nothing new, while records already answered stay listed), `quietHours: { from: string; until: string } | null` (round 2: floating "HH:mm"; null = outside Your day, from `dayEndTime` to `dayStartTime`) and `remindersPausedAt: number | null` (round 2: "Pause all habit reminders", one switch for a holiday). `PlanSlot.status` gains `'moved'` with `movedToDayKey: string | null` (Issue 69).

### `Project` as a goal [Next]


A goal is a project about becoming ("get back into piano"): `Project` gains `kind: 'project' | 'goal'` (readable; a project from before reads `'project'`) and `ProjectSealed` gains `why: GoalWhy | null` (three versions, below; round 1's single string is gone, never built) and, round 3, `importance: Importance | null` (the same sealed shape as a habit's, for any project, goal or not; section 28, "Importance"). Its milestones are the project's two-map milestones as built in issue 46; its regular step is a habit with `projectId` set or a repeating task (Issue 70) in the project. **Paused (lead, round 1):** `status` gains `'paused'` for any project, goal or not, with `pausedAt: number | null` and `pausedUntil: number | null` (date-only; null = until resumed), so a guardrail's "Pause it until Dec 1" is one shape for both; readers default an unknown status to `'active'`. A paused project's tasks stay open but leave the planner's ranking and Today's suggestions (they still show in Tasks, folded under "Paused"), and pausing a goal pauses its habits (section 28). Nothing else changes shape: roles, media, Finish and delete are a project's. Progress is derived, never stored and never a percentage (section 28, "Goals").

**The why, in three versions.** The person's words are kept word for word; Helm offers a tidied version; Helm writes a short summary with tags for matching. All three are sealed.

```ts
interface GoalWhy {                     // inside ProjectSealed
  verbatim: string;                     // the person's words (≤1,000): copied by code from the turn's span, or typed on the goal's sheet; Helm never rewrites it
  cleaned: string | null;               // Helm's tidied version (≤400), offered once; null = none, or dropped because the words changed
  cleanedState: 'offered' | 'accepted' | 'declined' | null;
  summary: string | null;               // one line (≤120) for tags, search, research and the nightly pass; never shown as the why
  tags: string[];                       // ≤5 short lowercase tags ("music", "calm")
  basedOn: string;                      // first 16 hex of sha256(verbatim) the cleaned version and summary were made from
  saidAt: number; editedAt: number | null; captureId: string | null;
}
```

- **What is shown** (`readShownWhy`, pure): the cleaned version when `cleanedState == 'accepted'` and `basedOn` still matches the words, else the verbatim. "Your words" on the goal's sheet always opens the verbatim.
- **Who writes what.** The verbatim: `createProject` or `updateProject` with `whySpanId` in a turn, or the goal sheet's edit (`projectActions.editWhy`), both through `goalWhyWrites`. The cleaned version, summary and tags: the model, in the same turn that brings the words (`whyCleaned`, `whySummary`, `whyTags`, cut by code), written `offered`. Accept or keep mine: `projectActions.setWhyChoice` (or `setPatternState`-style words in a turn: "use the tidy one").
- **When the words change** (an edit on the sheet, no turn): the cleaned version is dropped (`cleaned: null`, state null; the person's new words win, and a tidy-up is offered again only when asked); the summary is stale (`basedOn` differs) and the nightly pass rewrites it and its tags (section 28), so nothing else reads a summary of words that no longer exist. A turn that touches the goal before then rewrites it at once.

### `Observation`, `Reflection`, `Research` and `ScheduledAction` [Next]


Owner review 1. An observation and a reflection are **derived** (section 28, "Where things live"): each names what it rests on in `sourceKeys`, is never edited by hand and can be rebuilt or dropped. A research record and a scheduled action are the person's own (their home is here).

```ts
type ObservationTag =                   // a bounded set both ways (OBSERVATION_TAGS in constants.ts): what was said, never a diagnosis or a guessed mood
  // struggling (STRUGGLING_TAGS)
  | 'tired' | 'reluctant'               // reluctant: "don't feel like it", "it's too hard", "not in the mood"
  | 'tooBusy' | 'unwell'                // unwell: ill or in pain ("my back hurts")
  | 'bored' | 'stressed'
  // working (WORKING_TAGS)
  | 'enjoyed' | 'easy' | 'proud' | 'energised' | 'madeProgress'
  // neither
  | 'bodyEffect';                       // a body sensation said with a care item ("felt dizzy"); kept as said, never a signal, never used to suggest a change
type ObservationSource = 'turn' | 'checkInNote' | 'followUp';

interface Observation {                 // observations/{obsId}; written only by sortHelmCapture (in the turn) and runHelmReflection (from notes)
  subject: { collection: 'habits' | 'projects' | 'tasks' | 'day'; id: string | null };  // 'day' = about the day, no record
  dayKey: string;                       // the habit day it is about (03:00 rollover, profile zone)
  at: number;                           // instant it was said or noted
  source: ObservationSource;
  sourceKeys: string[];                 // 'captures/{id}', or 'habitDays/{dayKey}#{habitId}/{windowId}' for a note; the cascade's array-contains key
  isCare: boolean;                      // copied from the subject when written: never feeds a change to a care item
  writtenBy: 'turn' | 'reflection';
  createdAt: number;
  sealed: Sealed;                       // ObservationSealed
}
interface ObservationSealed {
  kind: 'feeling' | 'reason' | 'outcome' | 'answer';
  tags: ObservationTag[];               // 1–3: something said that fits no tag is not an observation (it stays in the capture)
  quote: string | null;                 // verbatim from its source (≤200), found there by code: null when it named another person's health (masked by code)
}

interface Reflection {                  // reflections/{dayKey}; written only by runHelmReflection
  dayKey: string;
  reflectionId: string;                 // the request's random id; a retried write with the same id changes nothing
  candidateCount: number;
  seenAt: number | null;                // the device's daily check weighed it
  sourceKeys: string[];                 // every observation and note it read (≤120)
  createdAt: number;
  sealed: Sealed;                       // ReflectionSealed
}
interface ReflectionSealed {
  candidates: {                         // ≤3, every one checked by code before it is written
    subject: { collection: 'habits' | 'projects'; id: string };
    observationIds: string[];           // ≥3, on ≥3 different days within 14
    tags: ObservationTag[];
    linkedTo: 'shift' | 'busyDay' | 'lateDay' | 'weekday' | 'other' | null;  // what the slips line up with, from records the request carried
    suggestion: 'differentTime' | 'smaller' | 'somethingNew' | 'none';         // 'none' forced for a care subject
  }[];
  whyRefresh: { projectId: string; summary: string; tags: string[]; basedOn: string }[];  // ≤5 stale goal summaries rewritten
}

type ResearchMode = 'learnLater' | 'leadTime';
type ResearchStatus = 'planned' | 'asking' | 'preparing' | 'ready' | 'offered' | 'done' | 'dismissed';
interface Research {                    // research/{researchId}
  mode: ResearchMode;
  status: ResearchStatus;               // learnLater: preparing → ready → offered → done | dismissed; leadTime: planned → (offerOn) offered → asking → preparing → ready → done
  projectId: string | null;             // leadTime: the dated project it prepares for
  leadDays: number | null;              // leadTime: 7–180, how far ahead of the project's date to offer it
  offerOn: number | null;               // date-only, by code: the project's date minus leadDays (rewritten when the date moves); null for learnLater
  questionIds: string[];                // the up-front questions, on the capture that asked them
  sourceKeys: string[];                 // the captures it came from and the answer turns it uses
  depth: 'light' | 'thorough' | null;  // null until asked or defaulted; see "Research depth and live web search"
  searchesUsed: number;                // live web searches this item spent (0 before)
  costCents: number;                   // what preparing it cost, searches and tokens (for the monthly cap)
  rerunOn: number | null;               // date-only; set only by the person's yes to "Look on <reset date>" after a capped Quick look
  preparedAt: number | null;
  videosFetchedAt: number | null;       // past 30 days the videos are fetched again or dropped (YouTube's storage terms)
  resultTopicId: string | null;         // where the person kept the result (a topic, or a section in the project's topic)
  offeredAt: number | null; createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                       // ResearchSealed
}
interface ResearchSealed {
  question: string;                     // the person's words (≤300), a verbatim span
  subject: string;                      // a short label (≤60) the merge matches same-subject learn-later items on
  moreAsked: string[];                  // later words merged into this item (≤5 × 300)
  // readable: notNowStreak: number (Not nows in a row, reset by Show me); declineReason: 'timing' | 'lostInterest' | 'changedMind' | null
  answers: { prompt: string; answer: string }[];  // the up-front questions and what was said (≤8)
  overview: {                           // general knowledge, always labelled "Not from your records"
    summary: string;                    // ≤600
    sections: { heading: string; points: string[] }[];   // ≤5 sections of ≤5 points
    comparison: { columns: string[]; rows: string[][] } | null;  // ≤4 columns, ≤8 rows ("AWD | 4WD")
  } | null;
  videos: { videoId: string; title: string; channel: string; durationSec: number }[];  // ≤2
  searchQuery: string | null;           // the video search the model wrote (≤100)
  sources: { title: string; url: string; site: string }[]; // the web pages a live search cited (≤10), shown under the overview
}

type ScheduledActionKind = 'commands' | 'prompt';
// the repeat is an RFC 5545 rule (the standard Google Calendar uses), read and written only through
// src/utils/repeatRule.ts (general, no app words; server copy functions/src/vendor/repeatRule.ts under check:server-copies)
interface ScheduledAction {             // scheduledActions/{actionId}
  status: 'active' | 'paused' | 'ended';
  endedBy: 'person' | 'finished' | null;  // 'finished' = its rule's end came (Ends on / After N), set by the run that was its last
  repeat: string | null;                // RRULE body in the closed subset parseRepeatRule accepts ("FREQ=WEEKLY;INTERVAL=2;BYDAY=TU,TH;UNTIL=20270301"); null = does not repeat
  startsOn: string;                     // the rule's DTSTART, a day key "YYYY-MM-DD" (date-only); every occurrence is a day, the time is applied after
  time: string;                         // floating "HH:mm"
  timeZone: string | null;              // null = the profile's home zone, followed when it changes; an IANA zone only when the person named one ("9 AM London time"); never a zoneSpans zone
  skipped: string[];                    // EXDATE, the original day keys of future runs skipped (≤100; past ones pruned by each run)
  moved: Record<string, { day: string; time: string }>;  // original day key → this run's day and time, once (≤20 future; pruned like skipped)
  nextRunAt: number | null;             // instant: the next occurrence (moves and skips applied) through zonedDateTimeToEpoch; null while paused with no end or ended
  nextRunKey: string | null;            // that occurrence's original day key: the run's id and the key Skip, Move and "Just this one" name
  splitFromId: string | null;           // set on the newer half when "This and following" was chosen on a later run (the older half ends the day before)
  kind: ScheduledActionKind;            // 'commands': held commands run by the executor, no model; 'prompt': a model turn with a narrowed policy
  allowed: string[];                    // the command kinds a run may write (closed list per action, checked at create and at each run)
  needsApproval: boolean;               // true while any allowed kind is outbound and Issue 93 hasn't granted acting without asking for it
  lastRunAt: number | null; lastRunCaptureId: string | null;
  lastRunDueAt: number | null;          // the set time of the last run, which its outcome line shows (not when the function got to it)
  lastOutcome: 'done' | 'needsYou' | 'failed' | 'skipped' | 'waiting' | null;  // 'waiting' = over the person's limits, runs on the next open
  lastFailReason: 'notConnected' | 'grantExpired' | 'notAllowed' | 'runFailed' | 'ruleInvalid' | null;  // by code, before any model call: 'ruleInvalid' = the stored rule no longer parses or passes its caps
  pausedFrom: number | null; pausedUntil: number | null;  // date-only, "Pause what runs at home" for an away span
  createdBy: 'person' | 'nomos'; sourceCaptureId: string | null; createdAt: number; lastEditedAt: number | null;
  sealed: Sealed;                       // { title, instruction (the person's words), heldCommands (kind 'commands') }
}
// round 1's `cadence: Cadence` is replaced, not deprecated: scheduled actions are unbuilt, so no stored document carries it
// (data-contracts.md's "deprecate, don't delete" applies to fields already in production).
```

`HelmProfile` gains (Next, owner review 1; each null on a profile from before): `reflectDueAt: number | null` (the next 03:00 rollover after something new arrived for the nightly pass; set by the turn's transaction or the device, cleared by the pass) and `scheduledActionsChangedAt: number | null` (stamped with any change to a scheduled action, like `habitRemindersChangedAt`).

`HelmProfile` gains (Next, owner review 2; each reads its default on a profile from before):

```ts
type PersonalityTrait =                 // closed, PERSONALITY_TRAITS in constants.ts, each with a prompt line and a sample sentence
  | 'blunt' | 'gentle' | 'kind' | 'playful' | 'dry' | 'brief' | 'detailed'
  | 'warm' | 'formal' | 'casual' | 'encouraging' | 'curious';
type HonestyLevel = 'tactful' | 'straight' | 'unvarnished';   // how plainly it says what the person may not want to hear
interface Personality {
  traits: PersonalityTrait[];           // ≤5
  honesty: HonestyLevel;                // default 'straight'
  clashWinner: Record<string, PersonalityTrait>;  // 'blunt|gentle' → the one that wins when they pull apart; set from the clash warning
  previewHash: string | null;           // first 16 hex of sha256 of the traits, honesty and winners the stored preview was made for
}
// on HelmProfile:
personality: Personality | null;        // null = the tone's defaults (assistantTone stays the preset; picking a tone fills traits, traits win)
phoneLocation: 'off' | 'city' | 'precise'; // owner: per person, default 'off'; only the chosen precision is ever stored (see "Where the person is")
researchDepth: 'light' | 'thorough' | null; // owner: the default depth for research; null = ask on each request ("Quick look or thorough?"), a skipped ask is light
prefersNaturalVoice: boolean;           // was a per-browser choice; now synced, and each device downloads the voice when it is true
zoneSpans: { zone: string; from: number; to: number; awayId: string }[]; // an away span's zone the person chose (date-only, inclusive, ≤10; `awayId` is the span it came from, so the offer is never made twice); [] by default
abilityOffers: { ability: 'location'; level: 'city' | 'precise'; declinedAt: number }[]; // Not now stamps, readable, dates only; kept offers wait 28 days; [] by default
forgottenAt: number | null;             // the last "Forget what you've noticed"; a nightly result submitted before it is dropped
// captures gain (readable): touchesPrivate: boolean (the turn filed or touched a private thing);
// presence: { isHidden: boolean; isUntouched: boolean } | null (sent by the device; unattended wake-phrase turns mask private names)
```

Readable, like the tone and name: settings, not the person's content, and the server's prompt builder reads them before any key is unwrapped. The preview's sample replies are sealed in `style/general` as `personalityPreview` (≤3 × 240 characters) so they don't burn a call on every device.

### `SeenAsLine` (the profile behind "How Nomos sees you") [Next]


Owner review 2. Derived, like an observation: each line cites what it rests on and can be rebuilt, except that the person's correction or removal is theirs and survives every rebuild (like a pattern's answer).

```ts
type SeenAsKind = 'tendency' | 'whatWorks' | 'whatDoesnt' | 'rhythm' | 'howYouTalk';
interface SeenAsLine {                  // seenAs/{lineId}; id = 'sa_' + first 24 hex of sha256(kind + normalised claim), so a re-derived line lands on its record
  kind: SeenAsKind;
  state: 'shown' | 'corrected' | 'removed';
  sourceKeys: string[];                 // ≥2 (a line resting on one thing is not a tendency): 'patterns/{id}', 'observations/{id}', 'style/{scope}#{traitKey}', 'habits/{id}' (≤20)
  subjectRefs: string[];                // the habits, goals or who it is about, for the cascade and the page's grouping
  writtenBy: 'reflection' | 'person';
  dontDrawFrom: string[];              // owner review 2 (design): on Remove, the line's sourceKeys; no new line of the same kind may cite any of them (≤20)
  derivedAt: number; correctedAt: number | null; removedAt: number | null;
  createdAt: number;
  sealed: Sealed;                       // { line: string (≤140, "You get piano done on mornings you've slept well"), basis: string (≤120, code-built: "From 6 things you said and 2 patterns"), corrected: string | null (≤140, the person's words) }
}
```

- **Written** by the nightly pass (a `seeYou` part of the `reflect` request, weekly per person, and on any night with three or more new observations): the model gets the person's shown patterns and their answers, 30 days of observations as aliases with tags (quotes only for the ones it cites), habits' stages and the style notes, and returns at most 8 lines, each with ≥2 source aliases; code checks the aliases resolve and differ, drops a line about a care habit's body ("you get dizzy"), a diagnosis or a guessed motive (`SEEN_AS_REFUSED`, a word list plus the model's own `isAboutHealth` flag), and drops any line whose id is `removed`.
- **Correct** writes `corrected` with the person's words (`seenAsActions.correct`, or a turn: "that's not right, I practise in the evenings now"); a rebuild never replaces it; its sources stay, so the person can still see why Helm thought otherwise. **Remove** writes `removed` and keeps the record with its line cut to the normalised claim, which is what keeps it from coming back; Undo restores it. **A correction outranks a derived line** (design): a corrected line is shown and sent first, and the derivation drops any new line on the same subject and kind that contradicts it (the model gets corrected lines as "the person says" and is told not to argue with them; code drops a returned line on a corrected line's subject and kind unless it cites a source newer than the correction). **A removal marks its sources "don't draw this"**: its `sourceKeys` are copied to `dontDrawFrom` on the removed record (the sources themselves are not written, so each keeps its one writer), and code refuses any new line of that kind citing one of them; those sources still feed patterns and other kinds. Undo clears it. Cap 40 records (shown and corrected first; the oldest removed past it keep only the id).
- **The cascade.** A source deleted: the line loses that key; under 2 left, a `shown` line is deleted and a `corrected` one keeps the person's words with `basis` "From what you told me".
- **To the model**: shown and corrected lines (≤20, corrected first) as `<how_you_seem>` in the full and quick prompts, for tone and suggestions only; never as a fact to state back unasked.

### `SyncedRecord` and server-only documents [MVP]

```ts
interface SyncedRecord {               // synced/{syncedId}: client reads; only the platform's Helm sink writes (section 19)
  connectorId: string;                 // 'app:pinch', 'google:calendar'
  accountId: string;                   // 'site' for a site app; a Google account's id (section 19)
  grantId: string;
  category: string;                    // the adapter's category id ('plannedMeals')
  kind: SharedKind;                    // 'event' | 'task' | 'place' | 'project' | 'person' | 'knowledge' | 'trip' | 'pet' | 'moneySummary' | 'countSummary'
  sourceRef: string;                   // the source's own id, opaque; syncedId = hash(grantId, category, sourceRef)
  startAt: number | null;              // events: instant; date-only values are UTC midnight with isAllDay
  endAt: number | null;
  isAllDay: boolean;
  dueAt: number | null;                // tasks (date-only, UTC midnight)
  calendarKey: string | null;          // calendar events: "{accountId}/{calendarId}", for shifts and busy
  isBusy: boolean | null;              // calendar events: declined dropped, tentative busy
  sourceUpdatedAt: number | null;      // the source's own change time when it has one
  contentHash: string;                 // HMAC under a subkey of the person's data key: skip unchanged writes without opening
  syncedAt: number;                    // when this copy was last confirmed by a sync
  transformVersion: number;            // the adapter's version when it wrote this shape
  connectionEndedAt: number | null;    // set when the grant ends and the copy is kept ("no longer updated")
  deleteAfter: number | null;          // set for a kept copy: connectionEndedAt + 90 days; null while connected
  sealed: Sealed;                      // { title, subtitle, detail (≤500), placeLabel, amountCents, currency, deepLink, extra }
}
```

`SharedKind` and each kind's sealed fields are the platform contract's (section 19), not Helm's; Helm's type re-exports them. A record sealed by an older `transformVersion` is rewritten at its next sync, never migrated.

```ts
interface DropBoxKeyDoc {              // server/{uid}/keys/dropBox
  publicJwk: JsonWebKey;               // P-256 ECDH public key, handed to the device
  wrappedPrivateKey: string; nonce: string; masterKeyId: string;
  createdAt: number;
}
interface AiUsageDoc {                 // server/{uid}/aiUsage/{dayKey}
  full: { day: number; recent: number[] };   // the full (Sonnet-class) route: calls today, and the start times inside the last 10 minutes (≤20)
  quick: { day: number; recent: number[] };  // round 6: the quick route, counted apart (issue 11)
}                                      // the day key is the UTC day; spend lives in aiBudget/{dayKey}.hundredthsOfCent
interface VoiceUsageDoc {              // round 6: voiceUsage/{uid}/days/{dayKey}, site level, Admin only (section 20)
  appId: string;                       // the app the last charge came from; per-app split is a later field
  listenMs: number;                    // cloud listening, as reported at stream close, capped per mint
  mints: number;                       // listening tokens minted today
  spokenChars: number;                 // cloud speech, after toSpokenText
  transcribeMs: number;                // voice notes
  costMicros: number;                  // worst-case estimate the budget is checked against
}
interface VoiceBudgetDoc {             // round 6: voiceBudget/{dayKey}, site level, Admin only
  costMicros: number; isOver: boolean;
}
```

## 3. Encryption

The pattern is Runway's (`src/apps/runway/TECHNICAL.md` "Sealed documents", "The key", "Master secret management"; code in `functions/src/apps/runway/planKey.ts` and `getRunwayKey.ts`), reused rather than rebuilt.

### Readable versus sealed

| Readable (queries, rules, ordering, the planner's arithmetic) | Sealed (anything the person would recognise) |
|---|---|
| ids and opaque id lists (`projectId`, `whoIds`, `subjectIds`, `topicIds`, `waitsOnTaskIds`, `effectRefs`) | capture text, its reading, answers, question options and held commands |
| statuses and enums (`status`, `importance`, `energy`, `context`, `stated`, `source`, `category`, `state`, `heldReason`) | task titles and notes; project names, emoji, objectives, milestone titles |
| flags (`isUrgent`, `isHome`, `isRestDay`, `isFromGeneralAnswer`, `hasAnswer`, `isSelf`) | who records' names, aliases, relations, phones, emails, notes; places' names and addresses |
| timestamps and date-only values (`createdAt`, `due`, `notUntil`, milestone dates, slot times) | fact statements and quotes; note titles and bodies; topic names |
| map state (`stepState`, `milestoneState`, `whoRoles`, `slots`, `questionState`) | step texts, procedure titles and groups, topic documents and overviews, versions' change summaries, event snapshots |
| round 6: kinds, tiers, media kind, status, size, dimensions, page count, duration, the route and voice providers | round 6: media file names, captions and extracted text; media bytes (sealed in Storage); spoken lines; style traits; fact topics and values |
| profile settings, name and tone; a synced record's connector, account, category, kind, times and freshness | execution summaries, commands, written/previous values, undo reports; slot reasons; the calendar snapshot; a synced record's words and deep link; answer quotes and what was searched |

 Key dates add no readable date: Today and Coming up read the one sealed yearly index (section 2 "KeyDate"), never a query on a day. What readable fields still say: when the person captures, how many tasks, facts and notes they have, their due dates and slot times, which records link to which, which browsers they trust. Rules protect them; sealing doesn't.

**Habits (Next, round 3).** Readable so the server can remind and judge stages without the key: a habit's status, cadence, window times and parts, tally size, goal and who ids, `isCare`, dates and pauses, reminder setting, stage and its history; each day's checks (status and time), tallies, `reminded` instants and `isInUse`. Sealed: the habit's name, emoji, notes, window labels and its importance (a project's importance is sealed too). What the readable part says about a person: at what times of day they do things, how reliably, and when a reminder reached them; rules protect it. **`lockScreenName` is the one plain-text copy of a person's words in Helm**, present only for a habit whose "Show its name on the lock screen" switch is on, because a push can't open a sealed field and the site's push service and the lock screen see the text anyway (section 14).

**Observations, notes, reflections, research and scheduled actions (Next).** Readable: ids, the subject's collection and id, day keys, instants, the source, `isCare`, statuses, `offerOn`, cadence, time and `nextRunAt`. Sealed: every quote, tag, note, candidate, why version, overview, video and instruction. A readable observation says only that the person said something about a habit on a day, never what.

**Private.** `privacy` (`'ordinary' | 'private'`, the class in effect) is readable on every private-capable record, so a job without the key can honour it; the record's own mark (`privateMark`: health, care or the person) and `isPrivacyCleared` are sealed. A readable `private` says that something is private, never why (section 28, "Private is a class").

### The key

- **Data key per person.** Callable `getHelmKey()` (`request.auth` required, App Check enforced): in one transaction reads `apps/helm/server/{uid}/keys/data`; absent, creates a random 256-bit key and wraps it (AES-256-GCM, the uid as authenticated data) under the keyring's `current` id, and creates the drop-box pair (below); present, unwraps it with the id the document names and re-wraps under `current` when that differs. An unwrap failure answers `failed-precondition` ("Helm can't open your things right now") and never writes: nothing replaces a stored key. Returns `{ appId: 'helm', keyId, keyVersion, key, dropBoxKey }`, the shape `createTrustedKeyStore` expects (section 4); never stored by the server or logged. Whether this browser is still trusted is the shared `checkInTrustedBrowser`'s answer, not the key call's.
- **Client.** `queries/helmKeyQueries.ts` (`helmKeyQueryOptions(uid)`: `staleTime: Infinity`, `retry: false`, no `meta.persist`), cleared on user switch. The query is the shared `createTrustedKeyStore` over `getHelmKey`: server first, a trusted browser's kept copy only when offline (section 4). `src/apps/helm/security.ts` is the one place that seals and opens: `sealJson` / `openJson` (validates `v`, throws `UnsupportedSealedVersion` for a newer one) over `encryptValue` / `decryptValue`, with the plain-HTTP `@noble/ciphers` fallback `@lib/security` already has. `openJson` also refuses a payload whose `keyId` isn't the current key's, so a document sealed by another device's stale key reads as "Couldn't open this", never as garbage.
- **Server.** `functions/src/apps/helm/helmKey.ts` holds `unwrapHelmKey(uid)`, `sealForHelm(key, fields)` and `openForHelm(key, sealed)`, the only Helm code that handles a raw key. The callables unwrap once per request and drop the key when it ends.
- **The general parts move to `functions/src/appKeys/`** in the issue that adds `getHelmKey`: keyring parsing (fails closed), `gcmEncrypt`/`gcmDecrypt`, `wrapDataKey`/`unwrapDataKey`, `sealWithKey`/`openWithKey`, the canary (`makeKeyCheck`, `keyCheckOpens`), `loadKeyring(secret)` with the emulator keyring and `deriveSubkey(keyring, masterKeyId, label)` (HKDF-SHA256). No app words; Runway's `planKey.ts` keeps only its path and key-id prefix and imports the rest, proven unchanged by `check:runway-keys`.
- **OAuth tokens are not Helm's**: the platform seals them under its own keyring (section 19, "Tokens"), so a second reader app never needs Helm's keys and Helm's keyring never holds a Google token.
- **Seeds and emulator**: the development keyring when `isRunningInEmulator()` and the secret is empty, never in production; seeds seal fixtures with a key derived per uid and write the matching wrapped key; the drop-box pair is created on the person's first open, like any account's.

### The drop box (captures made with no key)

A capture made on an untrusted device opened with no connection can't be sealed with the data key, which isn't there. Round 2 kept it as plain text; the form-draft rule for encrypting apps (security-privacy "One device, several accounts") rules that out. Instead:

- Each person has a P-256 ECDH key pair. `getHelmKey` creates it with the data key, wraps the private key under the keyring (`server/{uid}/keys/dropBox`) and returns the public JWK, which the device keeps as the `helmDropBoxKey` device secret on every online open.
- **Sealing offline** (`utils/dropBox.ts`, WebCrypto only): an ephemeral ECDH pair, HKDF-SHA256 over the shared secret with the uid as info, AES-256-GCM over `{ captureId, text, reading, inputMode, createdAt }`. The envelope `{ id, epk, nonce, ciphertext, createdAt }` goes into the `helmOutbox` device secret. The device can't open it again; the capture sheet shows the words only for the session it was typed in, and the Inbox shows "1 thing saved on this device" with its time.
- **Flushing** (`useCaptureOutbox`, once online): callable `importHelmOutbox({ items })` (≤20 per call, ≤12 KB each) opens each envelope with the private key, checks the shape and caps, seals it with the data key and creates `captures/{captureId}` as `received` with `arrivedVia: 'dropBox'` only if absent (a retry is a no-op), and returns the ids it holds. The device removes those envelopes, then the sweep sorts them like any capture (it runs again whenever a received turn lands after open).
- **As built (issue 60).** The envelope is `{ id, epk, nonce, ciphertext, createdAt }`, all base64 except `id` (the capture's `generateUuid` id, which becomes the document id) and `createdAt` (ms): `epk` is the ephemeral public key as a raw 65-byte uncompressed P-256 point, the AES-256-GCM key is `HKDF-SHA256(ECDH secret, salt: empty, info: uid bytes)`, the nonce is 12 random bytes and the envelope's `id` is the GCM additional data, so an envelope moved to another id doesn't open. The sealed JSON holds `{ captureId, text, reading, inputMode: 'typed' | 'voice', createdAt }`; the server drops an envelope whose `captureId` or `createdAt` disagrees with the envelope's, whose words are empty or over 4,000 characters; a time ahead of the server's clock is stored as now. The pair is created by `getHelmKey` (public JWK `{ kty, crv, x, y }` returned as `dropBoxKey` and read by the helper in `helmKeyQueries.ts`, which keeps it as the `helmDropBoxKey` device secret; the private key is stored as `server/{uid}/keys/dropBox` and re-wrapped lazily like the data key; a person who had a data key and no pair gets one on their next open, and a pair is generated only when the key document lacks one; a drop-box key that won't unwrap never fails the data key). `importHelmOutbox({ items })` answers `{ held, rejected }`: `held` is every id the person's Helm now has (created now or already there, so a retry creates nothing twice), `rejected` is every one that can never import and is never also in `held` (wrong person, changed, malformed), and the device removes both lists from `helmOutbox` and says how many were rejected. The capture is written as `received`, `arrivedVia: 'dropBox'`, `seenAt: null`, `sessionId` its own id and `turnIndex` 0, so the sweep sorts it and it lands in "New since you looked"; a turn without `arrivedVia` reads as `'direct'`. `useCaptureOutbox` (mounted by `useHelmSync`) loads the list, flushes on open, when the key arrives or fails and on the `online` event, retries a failed send with a pause that doubles from 15 seconds to 5 minutes while the browser is online, and keeps a sign-out marker (`setPendingDeviceWork`, `@/lib/auth/signOutChecks`: the composed question and a count in localStorage, never the words, so `AuthProvider.logOut` asks even where Helm never loaded; it stops on a "Stay"). The kept list's read-modify-write and the flush take a Web Lock per person (`navigator.locks`), falling back to the in-tab queue; a kept list that can't be read throws, so a save fails with the words still in the field and a send leaves the list alone. The words of a saved thing live in memory for the session that typed them and never in storage. `check:helm-drop-box` seals with the device code and opens with the server code.
- **Where it can't work:** a device that has never opened Helm online has no public key (it couldn't have signed in offline either); plain-HTTP pages and private windows have no `crypto.subtle` or can't keep a device secret (`canKeepDeviceSecrets`) (P-256 isn't in `@noble/ciphers`), so there the sheet says Helm needs a connection to keep it, and the words stay in the field until the tab closes.

### How listeners open what they read

`useHelmSync` starts the data listeners only once the key is ready.
