- **The picker** (`ContactsPicker`, a `Subview` with `SearchInput`) reads through `readConnectorOptions({ connectorId: 'google:contacts' })`: the platform pages `people.connections.list` with `personFields=names,emailAddresses,phoneNumbers,photos,birthdays,organizations` and returns up to 2,000 contacts as `{ resourceName, name, email, phone, hasPhoto, organization, hasBirthday }`, held in memory only, never stored. Already-imported contacts (matched by `importedFrom`) show as such.
- **Import:** `importHelmContacts({ resourceNames })` (≤50 per call; the picker batches) re-fetches the picked people with `people.getBatchGet` on the server (the client's list is never trusted for content), matches each to an existing who by email, then phone, then name through `createOptionMatcher` (a match fills only empty fields and asks nothing), creates the rest as `kind: 'person'`, `tier: 'regular'`, `importedFrom: { connectorId: 'google:contacts', accountId, resourceName, importedAt }`, sealed, in one execution with Undo. A contact's birthday (Google's `{ year?, month, day }`, year often missing) becomes a `birthday` key date with `year: null` when Google gives none and `source.by: 'google:contacts'`; it fills an empty birthday only and is tracked in the who's `importedFields` as `birthday` (round 6e: the date itself is a `keyDates` document). A contact photo is fetched on the server (only from Google's photo hosts, allowlisted), shrunk to 512 px, sealed and stored as the who's photo media. The birthday is written as its own `keyDates` document, and its entry is added to the yearly index in the same transaction (`applyToKeyDateIndex`'s server copy).
- **A one-time copy** (PRODUCT.md light call): nothing updates on its own. A who imported from Contacts has "Update from Google" on its page, which calls `importHelmContacts` for that one resource name and fills or replaces only the fields that came from Google and the person hasn't edited since (`importedFields` map of field → hash at import). Disconnecting Contacts leaves imported who records as they are.

**As built (issue 38):** `importHelmContacts` is one callable with two modes: `{ accountId, resourceNames }` (up to 50) and `{ whoId }` for Update from Google, which reads the account and contact from the who's own `importedFrom`. Both check the live `helm__google:contacts__{accountId}` grant first (`failed-precondition`, reason `notConnected` or `reconnect`). The planner is pure (`nomos/contactImport.ts`): a requested name Google did not return is ignored, a match goes by contact id, then email, then phone (last ten digits), then a sure name through `matchWho`, and fills only empty fields; an unsure name makes a new who. A name match is accepted only when the who has no `importedFrom` and no email or phone the contact lacks, and `importedFrom` matches compare the account as well as the contact id; a who filled or made earlier in the batch carries its `importedFrom` while planning, so two same-name contacts are two people. A new who's id is derived (a keyed HMAC of account and contact id, 20 hex characters), so a concurrent second import's `transaction.create` fails (`ALREADY_EXISTS`, answered as `aborted`). Only name, email, phone and photo are read (`personFields=names,emailAddresses,phoneNumbers,photos`): birthdays and companies wait for issue 43, so no key date is written here. `importedFields` holds a keyed HMAC of each imported value (`name`, `email`, `phone`, `photo`, `photoUrl`), not the value, because that field sits outside the seal. A who write re-checks the sealed nonce in the transaction (`aborted` if it changed). The photo is fetched without credentials from an allowlisted `lh*.googleusercontent.com` host (https, no redirects, 5 MB), shrunk to 512 and 256 px, sealed and stored as a `kept` medium; Undo removes the medium with its who and deletes the stored bytes (a failed delete is logged); an import's Undo keeps a person who was edited or is in use as `kept`, so Activity can say "Partly taken back". The digests (`importedFields` and the derived ids) are keyed by the user's data key, so a key rotation resets them: Update from Google then fills empty fields only, and a re-import of a known contact takes the `importedFrom` match instead. A photo uploaded before a crash that never reached the write is orphaned in Storage until the Issue 52 sweep removes it. The photo count that failed is logged beside the count that came in. The execution is category `import`, so Undo counts people ("Took back 3 people") and accepts an execution with no tasks. `ConnectorOption` gained `detail` and `photoUrl`. Not verified against the live People API; the emulator answers from fixtures.

## 23. Media in the conversation

### Kinds and caps

| Kind | Accepted | Cap | How the assistant reads it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Photo or screenshot | JPEG, PNG, WebP, HEIC (converted on the device) | any size; shrunk on the device to 1600 px (built, issue 52: one sealed 1600 px copy plus a 512 px thumbnail serve the model and the keep; no refusal for size) | an image block |
| PDF | `application/pdf` | 10 MB and 20 pages (PRODUCT.md) | its text layer, extracted on the server, up to 12,000 characters to the model; a scanned PDF with no text layer goes as a document block (Claude reads PDFs natively up to its own page limit; unverified current limits) |
| Voice note | recorded in the composer (`audio/webm` or `audio/mp4`) | 5 minutes, 10 MB | transcribed by `transcribeVoiceNote`, charged to voice listening minutes; the transcript is the capture's text |
| Markdown | `.md`, `text/markdown`, `text/plain` with a `.md` name | 20,000 characters (PRODUCT.md), 100 KB | read as text, no vision path; up to 12,000 characters to the model (the whole file when it starts a topic, below) |

Up to 5 attachments per turn (PRODUCT.md); 2,000 kept media per person (a quiet cap row in Settings). Video is Next.

### Flow

**As built (issue 53).** Step 1 checks the count and each file's kind and bytes when picked (`fitAttachments`), then measures a PDF's pages (unpdf, loaded only when a PDF is picked) and a Markdown file's characters (`checkMeasured`) and refuses past 20 pages or 20,000 characters with a line naming the cap. A recording is made in the composer (`useAudioRecorder`, WebM/Opus or MP4), stops itself at 5 minutes, needs a connection, uploads sealed, and is written out by `transcribeHelmVoiceNote` into the text field before Send, so the person can correct it; the turn is then a voice turn (`sttProvider: 'voiceNote'`). A Markdown file is written straight to `received` with no Storage object. The viewer opens a kept PDF's decrypted copy in a new tab ("Open the PDF"), draws a Markdown file with `MarkdownText` and plays a recording with its transcript under it.

**As built (issue 52).** Photos only. Step 1 shrinks to 1600 px with no size refusal and checks only the count (5) and that the file is an image. A turn of photos alone is an `'attachment'` turn. Step 3's decisions are `keep` or `discard` with a caption and an `attachTo` that is a task or fact (an existing one in the context or the turn's own new task); a photo with no target is let go. "Keep it" on a let-go photo attaches it to the first task or fact the turn changed, else keeps it with no record; it writes from the device, so it needs a connection, and it is not an Undo effect. Step 4 writes a keep in the turn's execution (`executionId` on the medium); a let-go photo is `discarded` with `deleteAfter = now + 24 h` outside any execution. Remove photo (`removeKeptMedia`) takes the photo off its record, and with nothing else holding it writes it `discarded` with `deleteAfter = now + 24 h` instead of deleting it; its Undo is a field-scoped write of `attachedTo`, `status`, `decidedAt` and `deleteAfter`. Deleting a task or forgetting a fact releases the photos kept only on it the same way (a fact's inside its transaction, a task's each in its own write so one already gone never holds back the delete; `planPhotoRelease`, `utils/mediaRelease.ts`), and the delete's Undo puts them back. A photo-only turn never waits in Needs you as unread (`SettleInput.hasMedia`). The server sweep reads each document again and deletes it only while it is still `discarded` and expired, with a `lastUpdateTime` precondition, so a Keep it after the sweep listed the photo wins.

1. The device checks the cap, shrinks a photo, makes a 512 px thumbnail, seals bytes and thumbnail with `encryptBytes` under the data key, uploads to `apps/helm/media/{uid}/{mediaId}` and `{mediaId}.thumb` (`application/octet-stream`), and writes `media/{mediaId}` as `uploading`, then `received`.
2. The capture carries `attachmentIds`; `sortHelmCapture` opens each medium (bytes from Storage through the Admin SDK, opened with the person's key in memory) and hands it to the model as above. A PDF's text layer is extracted with a small server dependency chosen in the PDF issue (`pdfjs-dist` legacy build or `unpdf`; licence checked there).
3. `file_results` carries one decision per medium: `keep` or `discard`, a caption (≤200), `attachTo` aliases (records in the context or `$new` refs from this reply) and, for Markdown, `asNote: true` (with `topicAliases`), `asTopic: true` or a record to attach it to. Code keeps a medium only when it is attached to at least one record or becomes a note or a topic; anything else is `discarded`.
4. One transaction writes the medium's decision with the turn's other effects in the same execution, so Undo reverts a keep.

A voice note's audio is always `discarded` by code unless the person taps Keep the recording (its transcript is already the capture's text). Product's light call; the model is never asked about it.

### Markdown as knowledge

When the assistant keeps a Markdown file as knowledge, code (never the model) writes a note with `source: 'importedFile'`, `sourceMediaId`, title from the first `#` heading or the file name, and body = the file text verbatim, filed like any note (its topics then bring it in). With `asTopic: true` ("make this a topic"), code also creates a topic whose version 1 (reason `imported`) is the file split at its headings into sections, each `writtenBy: 'person'` with the note as its source, so the person's words stay theirs; from then on it grows like any topic (section 24). Kept attached to a record instead, it shows as a file row that opens in `MarkdownText`.

### Discarded media and deletion

A discarded medium stays 24 hours with "Didn't keep it · Keep it" on its line in the conversation (design draws it), then is deleted. That line, PRODUCT.md's Keeping and Deleting and "What the AI sees" all name the same promise: a file the assistant didn't keep is deleted within 24 hours, never "as soon as it's read" (the bytes must outlive the turn so Keep it can work). Deletion: Storage objects first, then the document. Three sweeps do this, so no scheduled job is needed: `sortHelmCapture` deletes up to 20 of the caller's expired media (`status in [uploading, received, discarded]`, `deleteAfter <= now`) at the end of every call, sorted, held or failed; the device deletes expired ones over the photos it has loaded on open (`sweepExpiredMedia` in `mediaActions.ts`, at most 20); and `deleteHelmData` removes the whole Storage prefix (issue 63's Delete all will call the exported sweep). A sweep is best effort: Storage objects first, the document only once they are gone, a failure left for the next one. An `uploading` medium older than 24 hours is deleted the same way.

### Showing sealed media

`useSealedObjectUrl({ id, path, nonce, contentType, open, isEnabled })` (`src/hooks/`, general; `open(bytes, nonce)` is the caller's, so the key stays in the app): downloads the sealed bytes, opens them, returns an object URL and revokes it when it changes and on unmount; the last hundred opened objects are remembered for the signed-in person (the oldest dropped first), `forgetSealedObject(id)` drops one (and its `{id}.…` variants) and the memory clears on a user change. Helm's `useMediaUrl` calls it for a thumbnail (`{mediaId}.thumb`) or the full picture. Runway's receipt image hook moves onto it in the same PR (CLAUDE.md "When a UI primitive is promoted"). Thumbnails load in lists, full images in `ImageViewer`.

## 24. Topics as living documents

- **Reading:** a topic opens on its own route (`/helm/knowledge/topic/{topicId}`) as one formatted document rendered by `MarkdownText` (`src/components/MarkdownText.tsx`, general: `react-markdown` with `skipHtml`, links through `ExternalLinkText`, no raw HTML, no images from outside the app; MIT licence). A parent opens with its overview and a link to each subtopic (drawn by code from the tree), then its own sections. Tapping a section opens `SectionSourcesSheet`: each source as "You said, Nov 3", "Saved answer, Oct 12", "From car-care.md", "General knowledge, checked Oct 14" or "You wrote this", each opening the note, the turn or the file. "History" opens `VersionsSheet`: what changed, when and by whom, newest first; any version can be restored (a new version).
- **How it grows, and what it costs.** Filing a note costs nothing extra: the sorting call that files it may also return `addToTopic { topicAlias, afterSectionId | null, heading | null, markdown (≤2,000), noteIds }` when the note plainly fits a part of the document. Code, not the model, inserts it as a new `nomos` section citing those notes, so the command can't remove or reword anything; it is an automatic `draft` with Undo, a new version (reason `addedFromSource`) and a line in the turn ("Added to Car care: winter tyre size"). A note filed without a block marks the topic `behind`. The **document is rewritten in batches, not on each filing**: when the person opens a `behind` topic (or taps "Bring in 2 new notes"), the device calls `writeHelmTopic({ topicId })`. It claims the topic (`writeClaim`), opens the document and up to 20 pending notes (oldest first; more wait for the next write), and asks the `topicWrite` role (Sonnet-class) for at most 8 section changes (`insert`, `replace` or `merge` of `nomos` sections, and `reorder` or `retitle` of any section, which never touches a `person` section's words; each with its sources and ≤6,000 new characters in all) and a change summary. Code applies them: a change that touches a `person` section, drops a source a kept sentence still needs, or passes the size caps is refused; `version + 1`, `versions/{n}` with reason `addedFromSource` (`rearranged` when it only reordered or retitled; `firstWritten` the first time), `pendingNoteIds` cleared, `docState: 'current'`, one execution with Undo. The screen shows the current document with "Bringing in 2 new notes…" and swaps in the result. Cost: about 8,000–14,000 input tokens (a 30,000-character document plus the notes) and 500–2,000 output, about $0.03–0.07 a write at Sonnet-class list prices (**unverified**, checked at build time); counted on the full route's limits, plus at most 30 topic writes a day per person, past which the topic stays `behind` with "2 new notes will be added tomorrow · Read them now".
- **A parent's overview:** when a parent is opened and any child's `version` differs from `overviewOf`, the `topicOverview` role (Haiku-class) writes ≤1,200 characters from the children's outlines and first sections (about $0.002), saved with the new `overviewOf`; not a version, never touching sections.
- **Revising by asking:** "make it shorter", "rewrite the intro", "move the fragrance part to its own topic". The model returns `reviseTopic { topicAlias, changes (as above, `person` sections allowed), changeSummary, requestQuote }`. **Only when asked:** `requestQuote` must be a verbatim span of this turn's text and the topic the turn's screen focus or named in it, else code refuses it (logged `topic-revise-unasked`) and writes nothing. Code checks the `version` hasn't moved since the turn began (else re-reads and asks the model once more), writes the version with reason `revisedOnRequest` and the quote, in one execution. Without a request, the assistant can only add (`addToTopic`), write and reword its own `nomos` sections from their sources (headings, order, joining sentences, logged in History), and move a topic under an existing parent; the person's words are never removed or reworded.
- **By hand:** `SectionEditSheet` edits one section (heading and Markdown) or adds one; the result is `writtenBy: 'person'` with a `person` source, saved as a version with reason `personEdit`.
- **Nesting:** the person's move, the assistant's `moveTopic` under an existing parent (a `moved` version on the topic), or a yes to `groupTopics` (a `grouped` version on the new parent, `moved` on each child). Depth ≤3 and no cycles are checked on the device and in the server's command check; moving a topic recomputes `depth` for it and its subtopics in one transaction, refused if any would pass 3.
- **Asking while viewing:** the topic is the screen focus, so a question goes to the full model with its whole document (≤30,000 characters) in the context; the answer cites the sections it used.
- **History and Undo:** History lists typed rows (added from a source, your edit, changed on request with the quote, moved, grouped, rearranged, restored). The newest row has Undo, which reverses that change (a move or a group puts the tree back); any older row offers Restore, which writes that version's sections as a new version (reason `restored`).

**As built (issue 50).** `writeHelmTopic` answers `{ status, version, broughtIn, waiting, retry }`, with `retry` `'soon'` on `busy` when the 10-minute allowance is spent (the claim is released, the topic stays `behind`, the day's topic-write count is given back), `'tomorrow'` on `limited` (the 30 a day, the full route's day cap or the project's daily budget) and `null` on every other status. A write that fails before the model is asked gives its day count back; one that fails after keeps it counted. The write's Execution carries no `effects` and no `effectRefs`, so Activity offers no Undo that would plan nothing: the topic's Undo is a restore in History. A topic at version 0 takes the notes already filed under it, at most 50 and in no order (a known limit: the oldest are not guaranteed). Each part of the document a question sees has its own alias (`d1`, `d2`, …), all resolving to the topic as a `topic` source, so an answer can cite a part from a topic's page; when a part and a note disagree the part is newer and wins. A parent's overview has its own 20-second cap and `overviewOf` names only the children it summarised (those with a document).

**As built (issue 51).** A turn can change a topic in four ways, each written in the turn's one transaction by the pure `planTopicCommands` and the shared `writeTopicVersionIn`, and each refused while a `writeHelmTopic` claim is live (the note then files as before and the topic is marked `behind`). `addToTopic { topicAlias, noteRef, afterSectionAlias, heading, markdown }` pairs with a `createNote` in the same reply (`noteRef: "$note1"`, since a new note has no alias), targets only a topic with a document (`version ≥ 1`; a first write stays with `writeHelmTopic`), ≤2,000 characters, one per topic and three per turn; the note is not queued. `reviseTopic { topicAlias, topicChanges, changeSummary, requestQuote }` runs only when the quote is a verbatim span of the turn and the topic is the focus or named in it (`topic-revise-unasked` otherwise); a `person` section may change only when the turn names its heading or asks for the intro (`editablePersonSections`). The topic's version is recorded when the model reads it; when it has moved by the final transaction the server re-reads the document and asks once more (`reviseAgain`), and when it moves again the turn says "changed while this was made · Ask again", so a version is never lost. `moveTopic` and `createParentTopic` are checked by a server copy of the device's tree rules (`topicTree.ts`, guarded by `check:server-copies`); a parent not asked for becomes a `groupTopics` yes/no question ("Group them" / "Not now") answered through `resolveHelmQuestion` on the card, the sheet or by voice ("Don't group" closes it; Skip leaves it waiting; Undo of "Group them" reopens it). The new parent gets no version (version 0 still means "first write pending"); each child with a document gets a `grouped` version, a plain move a `moved` one, and a move on a claimed topic is made without a version. These turn changes carry Execution effects (`version`, placement, `made`) that `planUndo` takes back: a version is restored as a new one when nothing newer was written, a move goes back with its whole subtree or not at all, and a made parent is deleted only while it is untouched. A topic named but not focused is opened with the read-only `get_topic` MCP tool. `writeHelmTopic` batch writes keep empty effects. Editing a note's words or deleting it marks every citing topic `behind` (the delete is one offline-safe batch); a message source now carries its `noteId` so a note made from a turn can be found. Not built: "Move the fragrance part to its own topic" (no command makes a topic from a section; the reply says so in words).

## 25. Procedures and runs

- A **procedure** is a reusable routine ("Packing for a work trip", "Closing the cabin"): a title and steps in optional groups, versioned like a topic document. Commands: `createProcedure` (an automatic `draft` with Undo), `reviseProcedure` (a new version, same rules as `reviseDoc`), `startRun`.
- A **run** is one use of it, attached to a project, a task or a synced trip or event (`RunAttachment`). Starting a run copies the procedure's current steps into the run's own `stepState` and sealed `steps`, with `procedureVersion`, so a run keeps the steps it started with whatever the procedure becomes later (PRODUCT.md). Ticks are dotted-path updates on the run's readable `stepState` map (offline-friendly, no execution). A step added during a run is `isExtra` ("this time only"); "Add to the procedure" on it writes a procedure version with reason `fromRun`.
- A **task with steps** stays a task: its steps live on the task (`steps`, `stepState`) and never become a procedure unless the person says "make this a routine", which copies them into a new procedure.
- Group boxes with partial state are `ChecklistGroup`, moved from Runway to `src/components` in the runs issue with Runway's import (logged in round 3, still holds). `utils/procedureMarkdown.ts` builds the Markdown for Copy.

**As built (issue 54).** A procedure is made three ways: by hand (New procedure on the Procedures view, a full-page form of one row per step), from a task's steps (the task's row "Make these steps a procedure", which becomes "Open {title}" once one exists), or by asking. `createProcedure` and `reviseProcedure` are planned by the pure `planProcedureCommands` and written in the turn's one transaction with `writeProcedureVersionIn` (`procedureStore.ts`), which trims history to 200 versions. A create is kept only when its quote is the turn's own words and asks to make one; a revise only when the procedure is the focus or named and the quote is verbatim, and a revise that would drop steps the turn did not name is refused. Past 60 steps, 12 groups or the effects left in the turn, what was not kept is said on the turn's card. Both carry Execution effects: Undo deletes a made procedure and its first version only while it is still at version 1, and puts a revised one back as a new version when nothing newer was written. The person's edits, restores and deletes write from the device in one transaction (a delete reads the versions from the server and removes the procedure first); version 1 can be restored. `search_procedures` (MCP) reads up to 5 procedures with their steps. Not built: runs, filing under a project, drafting from notes.

**As built (issue 55).** Runs start on the procedure's page (Start a run, then "What is it for?": "Just a run" first, then trips, events from other apps in the next 30 days ("Now" for one under way; a trip's stays, rentals and flights are left out, the trip stands for them), and active projects, 4 at a time with Show more; or from a project or trip, which asks only which procedure, written offline-safe from the device; or by asking, through `startRun` (the quote must be the turn's own words with a start word; one per turn; a second open run for the same procedure and thing says it is already running, checked by query inside the turn; the device opens the running one instead). Steps are ticked by tap (a field-scoped write, no execution) or by asking, through `tickRun` over `rs#` aliases of the undone steps in `<open_runs>` (5 runs, 100 steps, 20 ticks a turn), with an Execution effect so Undo unticks while the tick is unchanged; Undo of a start deletes the run only while nothing was ticked. A step added "just this time" is an extra on that run (needs a connection), and can be added to the procedure as a new version (`fromRun`). The context also lists up to 10 upcoming trips when the person has procedures. Runs show on the procedure, its project, the trip or event's sheet and that event's card in the feed. Not built: a run attached to a task, "Run it again", reminders for a run.
- A run attached to a synced event that has left the calendar keeps its sealed snapshot of the event's title and date ("From Cake tasting, Nov 8, no longer on your calendar").
- The planner (section 11) is unchanged: it plans tasks, not runs; a run's attachment shows on Today when its event is today.

## 26. How Nomos sees you, and writing style

### Preferences, favorites and identity

These are facts (section 2) with `kind` and `topic` (built in issue 41); "How Nomos sees you" (`HowNomosSeesYou`, one route) lists the self who's facts grouped by kind, then topic, each editable (Correct writes a new fact and supersedes the old; Forget deletes with Undo), and the style notes below. Facts about other people stay on their who page.

### Learning the writing style

- **From what the person types to Helm:** `learnHelmStyle` (callable, the `style` role on the quick model) runs on the device's request after every 25 new typed captures (never voice transcripts: speech isn't writing), over the last 50 typed captures' text, opened on the server. It returns at most 12 traits and 6 "avoid" lines, each with how many of the samples showed it (`exampleCount`), and code merges them into the `general` profile: a trait the person set or edited is never replaced; a learned one is replaced when its count drops below 3.
- **From sent mail** (needs the Gmail grant and so the `google-mail-and-drive` gate): "Learn from my sent mail" reads up to 300 sent messages from the last 6 months (PRODUCT.md) live through the platform's Gmail connector (`q: in:sent newer_than:6m`, bodies stripped of quoted replies and signatures by code, each cut to 1,500 characters), sends them to the `style` role in batches of 50, and merges the batches' traits with their counts into `occasion` profiles (work email, personal email) and `audience` profiles only for who records the person already has. **Nothing read is stored**: only the traits, with counts. About 300 × 1,500 characters is roughly 110,000 input tokens on the quick model, a few cents a run (unverified price); counted as 6 calls against the quick route's limits.
- **Where it shows:** under "How Nomos sees you", each note with "Seen in 34 of your messages" and Edit / Remove; a removed note is remembered (sealed `removed`, the last 30, normalised) so learning never brings it back, and Undo forgets it. Used now only to shape the assistant's own free writing (summaries stay code-built); drafting in the person's style is Next.

**As built (issue 56).** The device (`useStyleLearning`) counts typed captures newer than `styleLearnedThroughAt` once a session while online and once its windows have loaded (a server count over the `inputMode`, `createdAt` index), and at 25 calls `learnHelmStyle`, which answers `learned`, `notEnough` (under 10 typed texts) or `busy` (a quick call couldn't be reserved). The reply is checked by code before the merge: a trait over 120 characters, an avoid line over 80, a quoted span over 30 or found in fewer than 3 samples, or any 31-character run shared with a sample is dropped, so no run of more than 30 characters of what the person typed is stored. An empty reply writes nothing; an unreadable profile answers `failed-precondition` and is left as it is. The merge keeps every person-set trait, drops learned ones whose count fell below 3, caps at 12, and keeps the avoid lines once the person has edited them; it runs in a transaction that also moves `styleLearnedThroughAt`. The notes reach the model as `<your_style>` in the full and quick prompts, for its own wording only. Edits, adds and removes on the device are transactions on the sealed notes, with Undo on a remove. Sent mail, occasion and audience profiles are issue 57.

**As built (issue 57).** `learnHelmMailStyle` (callable, no input) finds the person's live `helm` Gmail grant, reads sent mail with `listSentMail` through the router's uncached `withLiveAccess`, and answers `{ status, groups }`: `notEnough` under 10 messages, `busy` when a quick call can't be reserved (one per batch, at most 6, counted before any model call). Code splits mail into work and personal (personal only when every recipient is on a common personal provider), and makes an audience of a who record with 5 or more messages by the address in its sealed words (at most 5, most written first). Each group goes to the `style` role in batches of 50 through the same reply checks as `learnHelmStyle`, batches merge by summing counts per normalised note, and each group is written in one transaction as `mail-work` ("Work email"), `mail-personal` ("Personal email") or `mail-who-{whoId}` (the who's name), `learnedFrom: ['sentMail']`, keeping `createdAt`, `removed` and person-set notes. No `firestore.rules` change: the `style` rule already accepts every scope, `learnedFrom` of up to two entries and a string `audienceWhoId`. Only counts are logged.

**As built (issue 30).** Runway's manifest gains `transactions` (kind `transaction`, `mode: 'live'`, off at connect, `addedInVersion: 2`), so an existing grant has no key for it and it reads as off; the sync run skips live categories. `SourceAdapter.query?(ctx, { category, args })` returns `{ records, matchedCount, totalCents, currency, isPartial, from, to }` (no app words; `from` and `to` are the days it actually read as `YYYY-MM-DD`, or null for a category with no range). Runway's reads `apps/runway/plans/{uid}/purchases` by `date` within the range (default the last 30 days, at most 13 months with the day clamped to the month's last, scan cap 500 newest read as 501 so `isPartial` means more existed, or a purchase would not open: that logs `runway.share.transactionsUnreadable` with a count and no uid), unwraps the plan key once, keeps what Runway's own Spend list shows (a filed credit, a charge with no lines) and drops income-day deposits, pairs (card payments included) and a credit still waiting for a home, and matches the typed payee against the label and the merchant (never the bank's description) with the shared option matcher or by whole words ("Costco" fits "Costco Wholesale"). The category is the envelope's newest sealed version name for a one-line purchase, "Split" for several lines, "Bill" for a charge matched to a bill and "Free to Spend" for none or for an envelope since deleted; the currency is the plan's, else the summary's, else null. `listSiteLiveGrants` and `querySiteLive` (`liveRouter.ts`) find the live `app:*` grants with a live category on and re-check the grant, the category and `status()` on every call; a Google connector, an unknown category or a grant that is off, ended or without access answers `notGranted`. `search_app_records` (`{ app, category, payee?, from?, to? }`) is offered only while `siteGrants` is non-empty (loaded for questions only), logs `your Runway transactions` in the searched line ("Looked in your Runway transactions just now."), fills a missing `to` with the person's local day, returns up to 20 rows, the match count, the total and the `from` and `to` it read (the answer names them as real dates), and an answer that found nothing is still filed: a successful app search with 0 found counts as a source, so "No purchases at Costco between Oct 1 and Oct 31" is kept, and a spend question with no payee asks for every payee; and adds the first 5 rows as quotes whose deep link is the app's own path (`isLiveDeepLink` accepts exactly `/{appId}` for an `app:` connector). A kept row is not drawn as a quote: it carries `record` (`name` the merchant, `detail` the category or "Refund", `amount` as written), and the answer card groups an app's rows under "From Runway" with the name, the detail and day (read in UTC) muted and the amount right-aligned, then one "Open in Runway" link for the group that navigates inside the site (a quote kept before `record` existed is split from its `Runway · Costco` label and `$88.00 · Groceries` text); quotes are blanked by connector and account like Gmail's (`blankQuoted`, key `app:runway|site`). The seed turns `transactions` on for Alex and gives Jamie a grant with only `transactions` on, since Jamie's plan is the one that tracks purchases (ask Jamie what they spent at Costco last month). A live category writes no `categoryState`, so it is never stale on Today, the connection page (no "Update now" or status line when only live categories are on) or the refresh check, and `connection_status` reports it as read when asked. No rules, index or Storage change.

## 27. What keeps the future open

Nothing here is built in the MVP; each line names the MVP field or contract that keeps it a feature rather than a migration.

- **Milestones and a life timeline**: moments are the timeline's first source. One who's timeline is the who page's own read (`whoId ==`, ≤500, sorted on the device). The person's whole life gets its own sealed **moment index**, built in the Next issue, in the yearly index's shape: compact entries grouped by who, one document per decade (`meta/momentIndex-1980`), because 20,000 moments at about 75 bytes each (about 1.5 MB) can't fit in one. That issue builds it once from every moment, read in pages, and from then on every moment write updates it in the same transaction. It does not read moments who by who: 2,000 queries to draw one screen. Dates that come back join it only as their first year when known (a wedding). Facts' `occurredOn` and media's `takenAt` merge in on the device; a `lifeEvents` collection stays reserved (Next) only for events no who owns. No existing record changes shape. A goal's reached milestones (section 28) may join the timeline as one more source merged on the device, never copied into moments or `lifeEvents`.
- **App cards in the conversation:** a turn renders a list of conversation items, a union with a `default` fallback that draws any unknown kind as its title line; a later `card: { appId, kind, ref }` on an answer source or a synced record lets Pinch draw a recipe card or Waypoint a trip card from its own manifest, inside Helm's conversation, without Helm knowing the app's words. The connector contract's sealed `extra` already carries what such a card needs.
- **Other mediums (a browser extension, a watch, a share sheet):** every capture records `medium` (`'web'` now); every callable takes text, attachments and a screen context with a version, so an extension can call `sortHelmCapture` with `medium: 'extension'` and its own `ScreenContext` shape (`v: 2`: the page's URL and selected text) and a share target can post media through the same flow. The MCP server is already a server; exposing it over HTTP with MCP's OAuth (Beyond, "Helm in Claude.ai") reuses its tools unchanged.
- **Orders from other places:** `SharedKind` can gain `order` (a delivery, a purchase) for a mail-reading or store connector, landing in `synced` like any shared kind.
- **Video:** `MediaKind` gains `video`; the media flow, sealing and deletion are the same, with a frame sampler before the model.
- **Briefings and drafts as topics:** an assistant-drafted briefing or plan (Next) is a topic whose sections cite their sources, so it needs no new collection.
- **Drafting in the person's style:** the style profiles exist and are scoped by occasion and audience; an outbound category (Next) uses them.
- **Browse ordered by use:** tiles carry a declared `importance`; a later ordering by the person's own use counts each view's opens on the device (never on the server) and changes only the order inside each size, never the cap (Next).
- **Groups and households:** new `who.kind` values with member links; tiers and roles already apply.
- **Apple contacts and sign-in:** site-wide work (an auth provider, a connector), not Helm's.

## 28. Habits, goals, learning what works, importance and guardrails [Next]


Next Steps, after the MVP (lead, Nov 13). Habits and the goal-planning part live in Helm; a practice app (piano) would be its own app reporting through connected apps and is not planned here (owner, Nov 13). Each piece's "Where does it live?" verdict is in PRODUCT.md. Types are in section 2 (`Habit`, `HabitDay`, `HelmReminderQueue`, `Importance`, `Pattern`, `Project` as a goal). Round 3 moves reminders, pause endings and stage judging onto a scheduled function that reads only readable fields (owner, round 2 answer), adds a per-habit lock-screen name opt-in, and adds importance said in the person's words. Round 2 added a habit's life and its opt-in reminders (owner: "a user can opt into notifications while the habit is building… a process for sunsetting habits once they've formed") and guardrails (owner: "letting the user know that things have reached a point where it would be advisable to pause on something or perhaps unwise to start"). Owner review 1 adds the fixed vocabulary, check-ins said without a window, a goal's why in three versions, learning from what the person says (in the turn, and in a nightly batch), the "Where things live" map, the data the road-trip, learn-later and lead-time research use cases need, and scheduled actions.

### Habits and repeating tasks: the line between them


**The vocabulary is fixed** (lead). "Routine" is not a record type; in copy it means a repeating task or a procedure.

| Word | Record | Examples | Stages | When a time is missed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Task | `tasks` | Shopping in two weeks; buy the cat's refill | No | Overdue until done or moved |
| Repeating task (Issue 70) | `tasks` with `repeat` | Clean the car every 3 weeks or every weekend; the cat's monthly flea medicine | No | The open one stays owed |
| Habit | `habits` + `habitDays` | Practise piano; water through the day; the cat's daily pill (a care habit: no forming) | Yes, except care | It passes |
| Procedure (Issue 54) | `procedures` + runs | The move-out checklist | No | Not timed (Issue 70 can start a run on a cadence) |
| Scheduled action (Issue 115) | `scheduledActions` | Every Sunday, put next week's groceries on the calendar | No | It runs, or says why it couldn't |

The test when a thing is unclear: does it need to become automatic (a habit) or just get done (a repeating task)? A scheduled action is the assistant doing something; the other four are the person's.


| | Habit (this section) | Repeating task (Issue 70) |
|---|---|---|
| What it is | Something done at times of the day, again and again, for its own sake or for a goal: pills, water, practice, a walk | A chore with an outcome that comes round again: pay rent on the 1st, water the plants on Mondays |
| When one is missed | It passes. Nothing is owed; the week reads "5 of 7" | It stays open and becomes overdue until done or moved |
| Where it lives | `habits` + `habitDays`; a line on Today, checked by tap or voice | A `Task` with `repeat`; Tasks, the planner, open counts |
| On the calendar | Never planned into a gap; its windows are times of day, not slots | Planned like any task |
| Next one | Every due day is there from the cadence; nothing is written ahead | Made when this one is done (one open per series) |

- **The assistant picks by that rule**: `createHabit` when the words are about doing something at times of day where a missed time just passes, `createTask` with `repeat` when it is a chore that is still owed once missed. "Take my pills morning and evening" is a habit; "water the plants every Monday" is a repeating task. Converting one into the other ("make it a habit") is Later; until then the reply says what it made, so the person can say "no, a task" and Undo. When the words don't settle it ("the cat's medicine every day" could be either), the model sets `repeatKindUnsure` and code asks once with a code-built `whatKindOfRepeat` pick-one ("Something to make automatic" · "A job to get done" · Skip) holding the command, instead of guessing.
- **One cadence for both.** `Cadence` and its readers (`isDueOn(cadence, day)`, `countDueDays(cadence, from, to)`, `nextDueOn(cadence, after)`) are `src/utils/cadenceUtils.ts`: pure, date-only days in whole UTC days, no app words, with a server copy in `functions/src/vendor/` under `check:server-copies`. Runway's money schedules keep their own shape (month days and amounts), so nothing there moves onto it. Owner review 5: `Cadence` now serves habits only (its `timesPerWeek` is not something a calendar rule can say); repeating tasks and scheduled procedures move to the calendar rule below.
- **Issue 70 changes accordingly:** (`cadence: Cadence` becomes `rule: string` (an RRULE body through `src/utils/repeatRule.ts`) plus `startsOn`, `skipped` and `moved` as on a scheduled action, and `Procedure.schedule` the same shape; the sheets use the shared `RepeatPicker`, so "every other Tuesday" means one thing in Helm; Cadence couldn't say "on the 1st" or "the last Friday" anyway) a repeating task is `Task.repeat: { cadence: Cadence; seriesId: string; time: string | null } | null` (`time` a floating "HH:mm" for a due moment, else a due day). Ticking one creates the next occurrence in the same batch with the id `${seriesId}-${dayKey}`, so two devices ticking offline make one, not two; procedures run on a schedule add `Procedure.schedule: Cadence | null` and start a run on Today's first open of a due day, by the same deterministic id. Habits are not in Issue 70.

### Time: a habit's day and its windows

- **A window is a time of the person's day**, a floating `"HH:mm"` start and end in `HelmProfile.timeZone`, the same kind of value as the profile's `dayStartTime`. It becomes an instant only through `zonedDateTimeToEpoch(dayKey, time, timeZone)` (in `engine/habitDay.ts`), for "is it now", "which window does this check-in belong to" and the evidence buckets. It is never stored as a timestamp, so a window reads the same on every device and survives the clocks changing.
- **A habit's day** is the profile zone's calendar day with a rollover at 03:00 (`HABIT_DAY_ROLLOVER`): a check-in at 01:30 belongs to the day before, so evening pills taken after a late shift count for that evening. `habitDayKey(at, timeZone)` is the one reader, on the device and (copied) on the server. A window may run past midnight (22:00–01:00) but not past the rollover. 03:00 also stays clear of the 02:00–03:00 gap on a spring-forward night.
- **Named parts of the day follow the person's day** (lead, round 1; round 2 here): `dayPartWindows(profile)` gives morning as the first two hours of Your day (`dayStartTime` to +2 h), evening as the last two (`dayEndTime` −2 h to `dayEndTime`), bedtime the hour after `dayEndTime`, and midday and afternoon as the two halves between morning and evening split at 14:00 (or the middle when 14:00 falls outside). Only when the person has never set Your day (`dayStartTime` and `dayEndTime` still the profile's defaults and never edited) does it fall back to the fixed `DAY_PART_WINDOWS` in `constants.ts` (morning 07:00–10:00, midday 11:00–14:00, afternoon 14:00–17:00, evening 18:00–21:00, bedtime 21:00–23:00). The window is written to the habit when it is made (with its `part`), so changing Your day later moves nothing already made; the habit sheet offers "Move to your new evening" when a part-based window no longer matches. "Through the day" is no window. A time the person said ("at 8") comes from the reading's time span, never from the model, and becomes a window of an hour around it unless they gave both ends.
- **A changed time zone** (Settings, "Your day") moves every window with it from the next day on; past `habitDays` keep their keys. Helm has no travel mode; a trip in another zone is Later.
- **Due today** is `isDueOn(cadence, dayDate)` and `startsOn ≤ day ≤ endsOn` and `status == 'active'`; a `timesPerWeek` habit is due every day until the week's count is met, then shows as met. **"This week" is Monday to today** (lead, round 1, overturning rolling 7): `selectHabitWeek` is called with `weekStartsOn: 1` and keeps the parameter, and a `timesPerWeek` count starts again each Monday. A week's "5 of 7" counts only due days up to today, so Wednesday reads "2 of 3", never "2 of 7".

### Check-ins


- **By tap**: Today's habit line and the habit's sheet tick a window, add to a tally, or mark "not today" (`skipped`), with the writes in section 2. A tap on a past window of today records `at` as now; a time picked on the sheet records that time.
- **By voice or typing, without naming the window** ("I took my pills" ticks the matching habit, and the reply shows the to-do checked). The model picks the habit from `<habits>` (names, windows, today's state) and leaves `windowKey` null unless a part of the day was said; when it can't tell which habit, it names up to 4 `candidateAliases`. `resolveCheckIn` (pure, `engine/checkInMatch.ts`, server copy) then decides:
  1. **Which habit.** One alias: that habit. Candidates: keep those due today with an open window (or an open tally). One left: that one. Two or more (two pill habits, both open): nothing is written; the turn carries a code-built `whichHabit` pick-one (each habit's name and its open window's label, "Both", and Skip) holding the check-in, answered with one tap or by voice through `readSpokenAnswer` (lead: the ambiguity rule). None left because every one is done: no write, and a code-built reply says so ("Pills are already ticked for this morning and tonight").
  2. **Which window.** The open window holding the said time (or the capture's time); else the latest open window already started today; else the next open window starting within 2 hours (pills taken a little early count for the morning); a habit with no window ticks `day`. "This morning" or "last night" resolves by its span; "yesterday" writes yesterday's day.
  3. **The write.** An `update` effect on `habitDays/{dayKey}` at `entries.<habitId>.checks.<windowId>` (previous `null`), as milestones are, so the turn's Undo removes exactly that check while it is unchanged.
  4. **The reply shows the to-do checked.** The turn's result line is the habit's own Today line (`HabitResultLine`, drawn from the slice by the effect's ref: the name, the window now ticked, the week's count), not only words; the spoken line is code-built ("Done. Evening pills ticked.").
- **A note with a check-in**: said in the same breath ("took my pills, felt dizzy"), it is `noteSpanId`, copied word for word into the day's sealed notes, and the turn's observations read it at once; typed on the habit's sheet (no turn), it waits for the nightly pass.
- **"How was it?"** (caps lead): after a check-in by voice or typing, the turn may carry one code-built `howWasIt` question ("Good" · "Hard" · a free answer · Skip) when learning is on, `followUpAt` is null for that habit today, no other question is on the turn, and the habit is not care unless a note came with this check-in. Asking stamps `followUpAt` and `followUpState: 'asked'` in the turn's transaction; Skip sets `'skipped'` and nothing asks again that day. The answer is written as a turn (`answersQuestion` set, as a model-written question's answer is), so its observations (`source: 'followUp'`) come from the ordinary turn path. Never after a tap tick, never spoken first.
- **Retired, ended and paused habits** (round 2): a check-in said for one is not written; the reply is code-built ("Your evening walk is retired. Bring it back?" with `bringBack` as the turn's one follow-up), so a formed habit's count never grows behind the person's back.
- **Carers**: a habit with `forWhoId` (Mom's doses) is checked the same way ("gave Mom her evening pills" resolves the who through the matcher, then her habit), and shows the who's name on its line. It is always `isCare`.
- **Cost**: a spoken check-in is a quick-route turn (well under a cent, unverified), inside the existing per-person limits (20 calls in 10 minutes, 150 a day). Eight glasses of water a day said aloud is 240 quick calls a month; the device match below takes most of them off the model.
- **Said check-ins matched on the device, with no model.** A must for health and care habits (`isCare`, or the person's medicine switch), open to every habit. Before a spoken or typed turn goes to the server, the device runs `matchSpokenChoice` with today's habits as the options (each habit's name and window labels as aliases; a who's name for a carer's habit, through the who matcher) and Helm's check-in grammar (`CHECKIN_VERBS` in `constants.ts`: took, take, gave, done, did, had, finished, drank; "my", "the" ignored). `matchSpokenChoice` takes a string, so typed words ("done pills" in the bar) go through the same match as a transcript. A confident match with no extra words goes to `resolveCheckIn` on the device (already pure and shared) and is written by `habitDayWrites` through Firestore's offline cache, with an Execution and Undo like a tap; the reply is the code-built line ("Done. Evening pills ticked.") read by `readAloud`, so it works offline and over the turn limits, and costs nothing. Two matching habits draw the same `whichHabit` pick-one on the device, answered by tap or by `matchSpokenChoice`. Extra words ("took my pills, felt dizzy"), no match, or a retired or ended habit go as a normal turn (offline: the drop box, as before). Offline, a spoken one needs a recognizer that works offline (Issue 17's on-device model); with the browser's cloud recognition there is no transcript and the tap stays. Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-checkin-match` gains the device path (each verb, a carer's "gave Mom her pills", two pill habits, extra words passed to the turn, offline write queued, no model call made).
  **A ticked care habit names its medicine** (product): the reply and the Today/Activity line come from one function, `checkInLine(habit, who, presence)` (`engine/habitDay.ts`, server copy), which for a care habit reads `HabitSealed.medicine` and says it with whose and which window: "Done: Mom's morning metoprolol." ("Noted" stays for something kept but not acted on; with the dose when the habit has two of the same medicine at different doses), so a wrong tick is seen at once; with no medicine said it uses the habit's name. The device path and the server's turn both use it. The one exception: a turn started by the wake phrase on a locked phone or an untouched page says the label ("Mom's morning medicine is ticked") until the first tap, then the line redraws with the name. **Where the name comes from**: `createHabit` gains `medicineSpanId` and `doseSpanId` (verbatim spans of the turn; the dose kept only when it matches `DOSE_PATTERN`), written only when the habit is care (`isCare` or `setCare` on); `setCare { isCare: true }` on a habit with no medicine asks one code-built few-words question, "Which medicine is it?" (skippable; Skip leaves the habit's name in the line), and `updateHabit` with `change: 'medicine'` changes it by voice; the sheet edits both. Sealed like the rest, never in a push (pushes stay fixed words) and never a recognizer hint. Check: `check:helm-checkin-match` gains the named line for the person's own and a carer's care habit, the masked line after a locked-phone wake phrase and its redraw on a tap, and a habit with no medicine using its name.

### Goals

- **A goal is a project with `kind: 'goal'`**, its `why`, its milestones (issue 46's shape) and its regular step: habits whose `projectId` is the goal, or repeating tasks in it. `createProject` gains `kind` and `why`; a habit made in the same turn as its goal links with a `$new` ref.
- **Progress is showing up and milestones reached, never a percentage** (PRODUCT.md, "a project never shown as 62% done"). `selectGoalEvidence` (pure, `engine/goalEvidence.ts`, beside `projectEvidence.ts` and checked by `check:helm-evidence`) builds at most two parts from: this week's check-ins of the regular step against its cadence ("3 of 4 this week"), the weeks of the last 12 with the cadence met ("Played 9 of the last 12 weeks"), the last milestone reached with its day, and the next dated milestone. A goal with no step yet reads "No regular step yet"; product words each part. It needs 12 weeks of `habitDays`, read once when the Projects list or a goal opens and kept in the slice while either is open.
- **Pausing a goal** (lead, round 1: it reuses the project's paused state) sets the project `paused` and pauses its active habits in one write batch, each habit's `history` gaining `paused`; resuming resumes the habits the pause paused (those whose `pausedAt` equals the goal's), never one the person paused on their own. `pausedUntil` on either ends the pause on the first open on or after that day (the daily check). Paused days are left out of "showing up", of a habit's life and of every pattern's evidence, and the batch stamps `habitRemindersChangedAt`, so the planner cancels the paused habits' reminders within 5 minutes (the send hook drops any due sooner); a pause with an end is ended by the planner on its day even when Helm is closed.
- **A goal's milestones are not life milestones** (Issue 89). A reached goal milestone stays on its goal; Issue 89's timeline may show reached goal milestones as one more source merged on the device, like facts' `occurredOn` (section 27), and never copies them into `lifeEvents` or moments.

### Learning, layer 1: noticing



Tick-based detection is pure code over records Helm already keeps and never calls the model. What the person says is a second source (overturning round 1's "never reads what was said"): observations from turns and check-in notes feed `saidStruggling` on the device and the nightly pass's `saidLink` candidates ("Learning from what is said", below). A stated preference still reaches Helm as a fact, through `remember`.

- **Signals.** Habit checks and skips (`habitDays`), Helm's plan slots and what became of them (`planDays`: suggested, kept, Not today, moved; done is a task's `completedAt` inside that day), task completion times, rest days and shift calendars (section 11), and offers answered Not now. Plan slots that move today are deleted from their day (issue 31), which loses the signal, so Issue 69 keeps a moved slot on its old day as `status: 'moved'` with `movedToDayKey` (readers default an unknown status to hidden; Today never shows a moved slot).
- **A day counts only when Helm was in use.** A habit window on a day with no check-in of any habit, no capture and no task ticked is not evidence either way, so a weekend away from the phone never reads as "you skip weekends".
- **Buckets** are a weekday and a part of the day in the profile's zone (`weekdayPart`), or, once shift calendars are marked, the day-part before or after a shift (`shift`), so the nurse's rotation is seen as "evenings before a night shift" instead of a weekday that changes every week.
- **Thresholds** (constants, tuned in the issue): `windowMissed` needs 4 of the last 5 occurrences of that window on that kind of day missed or skipped, within 10 weeks; `doneOutsideWindow` needs 5 of the last 6 done checks of a window outside it on the same side, and suggests the window moved to their median time, same length, on the half hour; `plannedSlotMissed` needs 5 Helm-planned slots in the bucket across 4 different weeks of the last 8, with 80% not done that day, moved or Not today. Only signals after the subject's last relevant edit count (a window's `editedAt`, a habit's cadence change), so changing a time starts its evidence over.
- **A reason, only from records.** When three quarters of the matched days share something Helm can see (a shift that started or ended within 6 hours, a rest day, a calendar busier than 6 hours), the pattern carries a code-built `reasonLine`. Nothing about mood, health or motive is guessed or stored.

### Learning, layer 2 and 3: showing and acting carefully

- **Handling is set by code from the subject**, never by the model: `actAndTell` when the effect touches only Helm's own planning (`plannerAvoid` on slots Helm suggested); `suggest` when it would change something the person set (a habit's window, a task's due time); `askOnly` when the subject is `isCare` (a care habit, or a task the server marked `isCare`), whose effect is `none` and whose Accept only records that the person knows (with a link to edit the habit themself). Tasks gain `isCare: boolean` in the same issue (null-free: a task from before reads `false`; the server sets it on create as it sets a habit's), and the planner never applies a `plannerAvoid` to a care task.
- **Act and tell**: one batch writes the pattern (`accepted`, `acceptedBy: 'auto'`) and an execution (`category: 'plan'`, `actor: 'nomos'`, one effect on the pattern), so the act shows in Activity and on "What I've noticed" with Undo. The planner reads every accepted `plannerAvoid` as a day-part it does not fill (`avoidBuckets` in `buildPlanInput`), and a re-plan never moves a slot already kept.
- **Suggest**: the pattern is written `suggested` and shown once on "What I've noticed" (and wherever UX places the nudge). Accept writes the change as an ordinary execution (a `moveWindow` is an `update` effect on the habit's `windowState.<id>` and its sealed label untouched) and the pattern `accepted`, `acceptedBy: 'person'`, in one write batch.
- **Pacing**: at most one new pattern shown a day and three `suggested` at once; the rest wait as candidates and are recomputed next time. Round 2: the one a day is shared by every family, and Today shows only that one (product, round 1: one suggestion on Today), picked in the order care asks, then guardrails, then habit-life offers, then the rest of noticing; a guardrail raised at the moment of starting is shown on that turn instead and counts as the day's one.
- **Handling for the round-2 kinds**: every habit-life offer and guardrail is `suggest` (never `actAndTell`: each changes something the person set), except that `reminderEase` and every offer about a care habit is `askOnly`-worded (the Accept changes only what the person picked). A care item is never the subject of a guardrail.

### Learning, layer 4: remembering the answer

```
(candidate) ──eligible, actAndTell──► accepted(auto) ──Undo──────────► undone
(candidate) ──eligible, suggest/ask─► suggested ──Accept────────────► accepted(person)
                                      suggested ──Not now───────────► declined
accepted ──"evenings work again"──► retired                (effect stops; evidence starts over)
declined | undone ──past nextEligibleOn, new evidence as strong──► suggested (or accepted(auto))
any ──"stop suggesting that", or the third no──► offReason set (never shown; state kept)
offReason set ──"you can suggest that again" / Reopen──► declined, declineCount 0, nextEligibleOn null
```

- **A no** (Not now, or Undo of an act) adds one to `declineCount`, sets `nextEligibleOn` to today plus the back-off (`PATTERN_BACKOFF_WEEKS = [4, 8]`, by count), sets `evidenceSince` to now and keeps the evidence it was shown with; the third no sets `offReason: 'thirdNo'` (lead, round 1). The wait is shown as a date ("Won't suggest this before Dec 11"). The same back-off covers the habit-life offers and the guardrails (round 2).
- **Coming back** needs both the date and new evidence: only signals after `evidenceSince`, with at least as many matches and at least the same share as the evidence last shown (4 of 5 needs 4 of 5 again, never 3 of 4). For `load` evidence (guardrails, round 2) it is a fresh measurement taken after `nextEligibleOn` that is at least as far over its limit as the one last shown (9 h of work against 6 h free, a ratio of 1.5, needs 1.5 or more again), and for `deadlineWontFit` and `starved` at least one of its `refs` must have changed since (a task added, a date moved), so the same unchanged load is never raised twice.
- **Retire** ("evenings work again", or Retire on the pattern) ends the effect at once, resets `declineCount` to 0 and `nextEligibleOn` to null, and moves `evidenceSince` to now, so the same pattern needs fresh evidence at normal thresholds. A window already moved by an accepted suggestion stays where it is; moving it back is the person's edit.
- **Off for good**: "stop suggesting that" or the third no sets `offReason` and keeps the record, which is what keeps it off; the record's signature is the same thing's forever, so a later detection lands on it and stops. Reopen is the only way back.
- **Undo of a pattern is its own path.** `undoExecution` hands an execution whose effects touch `patterns` to `patternActions.undo`, which writes `undone` with the back-off and stamps `undoneByExecutionId`, instead of deleting the record (a delete would forget the no). An Undo of an accepted `moveWindow` change puts the window back by the ordinary rules and also counts as a no on the pattern.
- **Issue 69 becomes this layer.** PRODUCT.md's "a declined suggestion of the same kind for the same thing is not offered again" is replaced by the back-off: every Not now on an offer (a plan suggestion, a procedure run for an event, a link, a role, a topic grouping) writes or updates an `offerDeclined` pattern by signature, and each offer's code asks `isPatternEligible(signature, today)` before offering. A Not now already stored by an MVP offer reads as one no dated when it was made.
- **Races**: every pattern write is a transaction on its deterministic id that re-reads the state, so two devices noticing the same thing on the same day write it once, and a no on one device is never overwritten by a show on another.

### Learning from what is said: two layers


The owner asked whether this runs on a schedule (batches are cheaper) or at once. Both, split by what each needs: words that arrive in a turn are read in that turn at no extra call; anything that has to look across days runs once a night in a batch. Neither decides anything: the device's daily check weighs every candidate with the same bar, pacing, care rule and back-off as before, and writes the pattern.

**Layer 1: in the turn.**
- `sortHelmCapture` already reads every message. Its `file_results` gains `observations` (section 5): up to 3 a turn, each a quote from the turn's words, 1–3 closed tags, a kind and what it is about. Code keeps one only when the quote is found in the turn's text and the subject resolves; it is sealed and written in the turn's own transaction with `sourceKeys: ['captures/{id}']`. **Another person's health is never kept in a quote**: `maskOthersHealth(quote, whoNames)` (pure, `engine/said.ts`, server copy) finds a quote that names a who other than the self (through the who matcher) together with a health word (`HEALTH_WORDS` plus the closed `OTHERS_HEALTH_WORDS`: panic, anxiety, depressed, diagnosis, surgery, hospital, cancer, pregnant, seizure, attack, symptoms) and writes the observation with its tags and `quote: null`; the capture keeps the person's own words, as it does for every turn. Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-said` ("Maya's having a panic attack" kept as a tag with no quote; "I had a panic attack" kept; a who named with no health word kept). Cost: the 50–150 output tokens it adds, about $0.0001 on the quick route and $0.001 on the full one (section 5's prices); no extra call, no extra latency worth measuring.
- Sources: what the person says anywhere ("don't feel like piano tonight", "I'm tired"), a note said with a check-in, the answer to "How was it?" (a turn), a reason given for a skip ("not today, my back hurts").
- The same transaction sets the profile's `reflectDueAt` to the next 03:00 rollover when it is null and learning is on; the device sets it when it saves a sheet-typed note, a why edit or a learn-later item.

**`saidStruggling`, on the device.** Found by the daily check from the slice's 14 days of observations (`readSaidSignals`, pure, `engine/said.ts`): observations about one habit with a tag in `STRUGGLING_TAGS` (`tired`, `reluctant`, `tooBusy`, `unwell`, `bored`, `stressed`) on 3 or more different days in the last 14 (owner: 3+ times in 2 weeks; distinct days so one long complaint counts once), after the pattern's `evidenceSince`, skipping paused days. Handling is `suggest` with these choices (product words them): **A different time** (`moveWindow` to the window of the habit's own done checks over 8 weeks with the fewest reluctant days, when one stands out; else `talkItThrough: 'differentTime'`), **A smaller version** (`lowerCadence`: one fewer time a week, or the weekday with the most reluctance dropped, never below once a week; or `talkItThrough: 'smaller'` when the smaller step is in the person's words, "10 minutes instead of 30"), **Something new** (`talkItThrough: 'somethingNew'`), and Not now (a no under the back-off; coming back needs 3 new days after it). On a care habit it is `askOnly` with effect `none`: Helm says what it heard and never offers a different time, a smaller dose or something new.

**Both ways**. `saidStruggling` uses `STRUGGLING_TAGS` (tired, reluctant, tooBusy, unwell, bored, stressed); `saidWorking` uses `WORKING_TAGS` (enjoyed, easy, proud, energised, madeProgress) with the same bar (3+ different days in 14), found by the same `readSaidSignals`. Its choices: **Keep it here** (`keepTime` on the window most of those days fell in: while accepted, `doneOutsideWindow` and `saidStruggling`'s different time need twice the evidence for that window), **A bit more** (`raiseCadence`, only when `easy` or `energised` is among the tags and the habit is Holding or Formed; never on care), and Not now. A habit with both kinds in the same 14 days raises neither (mixed signals are not a pattern). On a care habit a working signal is shown only as a statement, ask-only, effect `none`.

**Layer 2: the nightly reflective pass** (`runHelmReflection`, hourly; section 16).
- **Why a batch at night.** Nothing reaches the person before their next open anyway (the assistant never speaks first), the Message Batches API is half price, and most batches end within an hour, all within 24. It passes every question of section 5's test ("What runs in a batch, and what never does"), as registered jobs `reflect` and `prepareResearch`.
- **Who is included.** Private things are read like everything else (owner, review 4). People with `isLearningOn` and a `reflectDueAt` that has passed (their habit day ended at 03:00 with something new in it: an observation, a sheet-typed note, a why edited, a learn-later item saved or a lead-time item answered), at most one request each per night, while the project's daily AI budget has room (an estimate is reserved per request; anyone over waits a night). A person with nothing new is never sent. Because each run takes whoever's day has just ended, people in one zone go together; with today's people that is one batch a night.
- **Submit** (each run, after collecting): query `apps/helm/users where reflectDueAt <= now` (limit 2,000), and for each, 20 at a time: lease `server/{uid}/reflection/state`, unwrap the key, read the observations of the last 14 days (≤300), the notes on those days' `habitDays` (≤14 documents), habits, goals, the patterns not off, and research waiting to be prepared (about 110 reads), and build one request with aliases only: a fixed system prompt (cached for an hour, best effort in a batch), the person's material, and structured output (a JSON schema inside strict mode's limits; structured output works inside a batch, platform.claude.com "Batch processing", read 2026-11-14), read back as `unknown` by `normalizeReflection` and `normalizePreparedResearch`. Write the run document as `creating` with the request ids first, then `submitBatch`, then `submitted` with the batch id. A run still `creating` after 15 minutes (a crash in between) becomes `lost` and its people go back in the queue; a duplicate batch that did get made is collected harmlessly, since writes are idempotent.
- **Requests.** Up to three per person: `reflect` (the Haiku-class model, `claude-haiku-5-5`; link what was said to when things slip, ≤3 candidates; rewrite stale why summaries), and `prepareResearch` for each research item waiting (the Sonnet-class model, `claude-sonnet-5-5`; below). `max_tokens` 1,500 and 3,000. `reflect` carries no tools. `prepareResearch` carries `web_search_20260209` with `max_uses` by depth (Light 3, Thorough 10) when the item needs live facts and the month's allowance has room, and no tools otherwise. A continuation (below) is the same request with the stored partial answer appended.
- **Collect** (each run, first): for each `submitted` run, `retrieve`; once `ended`, read the results. `succeeded`: one transaction per person that skips when `reflections/{dayKey}.reflectionId` already equals this request's (a retry), checks each candidate (the subject resolves; at least 3 observation aliases on at least 3 different days within 14; tags closed; a care subject forced to `suggestion: 'none'`), keeps a why summary only when its `basedOn` still matches the words, keeps research parts only while the item is still `preparing`, seals and writes, and clears `reflectDueAt` only if it still equals the value the request answered (something new since keeps it for the next night). `succeeded` ending `pause_turn` (web search reached its own turn limit): the partial answer is sealed into `server/{uid}/reflection/state.continuation` and the next run sends it back as a continuation, at most 2 times, then gives it up like an expiry. `succeeded` ending `refusal`: one attempt; a batch has no server-side fallback, so nothing is written. `errored`, `expired` (not billed) or `canceled`: the person's `attempts` goes up and `reflectDueAt` stays, so a later run tries again; after 3 it is cleared (`helm.reflect.gaveUp`), and the material is still inside the next night's 14-day window. Each result's cost goes to `aiBudget`. Then the batch is deleted at Anthropic and the run is `collected`.
- **On the device.** The daily check reads unseen reflections and turns each candidate into a `saidLink` pattern only if it is eligible (signature kind, subject and tags; the back-off; the day's one offer; learning on), with its statement and evidence code-built from the tags and `linkedTo` ("Piano tends to slip on days you've said work ran late · You said so on Nov 3, 6 and 10") and the effect from `suggestion` (as `saidStruggling`'s). It writes `whyRefresh` into each goal's sealed why, and sets `seenAt`.
- **What it costs** (prices from platform.claude.com "Batch processing", read 2026-11-13; they are half of section 5's list prices):

| Per person, per night with something new | Tokens | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| `reflect` on the Haiku-class model ($0.05 in, $0.25 out per million in a batch) | about 8,000 in (14 days of observations and notes, habits, goals, patterns, the cached prompt), 600 out | about $0.0006 |
| The same on the Sonnet-class model ($1 in, $5 out), the other option | same | about $0.011 |
| Each research item prepared, Sonnet-class, no search | about 3,000 in, 1,500 out | about $0.011 |
| With live search | Light or Thorough, above | about 11¢ or 35¢, within the 200¢ monthly cap |
| Firestore | about 110 reads, 4 writes | about $0.0001 |

A person active 25 nights a month costs about 1.5 cents a month on the Haiku-class model (about 28 cents on the Sonnet-class one), plus about a cent per learn-later or lead-time item. One more Cloud Scheduler job ($0.10 a month past the free three). At 1,000 people that is about $15 a month on Haiku-class against about $280 on Sonnet-class, which would use most of the project's $10-a-day budget; hence the Haiku-class default, a one-line change in `models.ts`.
- **What changes for privacy, said plainly** (approved; the no-zero-retention fact is stated in the privacy copy). (1) Until now the server opened a person's words only inside a turn they started; this opens some of them (observations, check-in notes, names and why summaries, research questions and answers) once a night with nobody there, and sends them to Anthropic. (2) Batch requests and results sit at Anthropic until deleted, up to 29 days; the collector deletes each batch as soon as its results are stored, usually within the hour. (3) The Batch API is not eligible for zero data retention or HIPAA; Helm claims neither today, for turns or batches, so no current line becomes false. (4) Anthropic's no-training line in "What the AI sees" still holds for the API. What does not change: no capture, Knowledge, fact, who record, synced copy, mail or document is opened; nothing is decided at night; the learning switch turns all of it off. "What the AI sees" gains a fifth block, "Once a night", and its "For each turn" sentence gains "and once a night, what you've said about your habits and goals, when you've said something new".
- **Emulator.** There is no batch in the emulator, so the provider has a fake batch client that answers fixture results; `npm run helm:reflect-tick` runs one submit and one collect against the emulators (`--at <iso>`), and the seed gives Morgan two weeks of observations so a `saidLink` shows on the next open.

### A habit's life: Building, Holding, Formed, Building again, and letting go


Helm cannot know when something has become automatic; nobody can from the outside. It can count how reliably a thing happened, over how long, and how much reminding it took. So "Formed" means only that: nearly every week kept for ten weeks, the last four without a reminder before the tick. The research usually quoted (Lally and others, 2010: a median of about 66 days, ranging from 18 to 254) says it varies too widely for any number to be a rule; the thresholds are product's (round 2, logged), kept in one constant (`HABIT_LIFE`) and worded as Helm's judgment, never as science.

- **Due times** (`readOccurrences(habit, days, from, to)`, pure, `engine/habitLife.ts`): one per window on each due day (one per due day for a habit with no window; for a tally, done when `count ≥ timesPerDay`); a `timesPerWeek` habit has one per week, done when the week's count is met. A due time is **known** when it has a check, or its day's `isInUse` is true (a check of any habit, a capture or a task ticked that day), and **left out** when unknown, skipped ("not today"), paused, before `startsOn` or before the habit's last cadence or window change. Everything here is readable, so the server judges exactly as the device does.
- **A kept week** (`readKeptWeeks`): a Monday-to-Sunday week is judged once it is over. It is **kept** when every known due time but at most one was done, **unkept** otherwise, and **skipped** (neither) when it has fewer than 3 known due times, or the habit was paused for any of it (design: paused weeks are skipped). A `timesPerWeek` week is kept when its count is met. A week judged "skipped" neither keeps nor breaks a run.
- **Stage** (`readHabitStage(habit, keptWeeks)`, pure): walking the judged weeks oldest first from the last start (made, restarted, cadence or windows changed):

| Stage | Enters when | Leaves when |
|---|---|---|
| Building | made, restarted, or its cadence or windows change | the last 3 judged weeks kept → Holding |
| Holding | 3 judged weeks kept in a row | 8 of the last 10 judged weeks kept, the last 4 of them kept with no `reminded` instant before the tick on any due time, and at least 10 weeks since the last start → Formed; 2 unkept in a row → Building again |
| Formed | as above (automatic; the offer to let it go comes a week later) | 2 unkept in a row → Building again; letting it go → retired |
| Building again | 2 unkept judged weeks in a row from Holding or Formed | as Building (3 kept → Holding) |

  "Without a reminder" reads `reminded` beside each check: a week counts as unreminded when no due time in it had a reminder sent before it was ticked (a reminder that went out after the tick, or a habit with reminders off, counts as unreminded). A **health habit** (`isCare`, the person's own) stops at Holding: Formed is never reached, so it is never offered retirement (product, round 3). A **carer's habit** (`forWhoId` set) stores no stage at all (`stage: null`); its kept weeks are still judged, and three in a row raise its one "Fewer reminders?" question.
- **Judged once a week, by whoever comes first.** On the first look at a person in a new week (Monday's 03:00 rollover in their zone), the server's planner reads up to 16 weeks of `habitDays` for each running non-carer habit and judges last week; the device does the same on its first open of the week if `stageWeek` is still older (a person with no reminders on is judged only by the device, since the planner never visits them). The write is a transaction on the habit that writes only when `stageWeek` is older than the week judged, so it lands once: `stage`, `stageAt`, `stageWeek`, a `history` entry (`kind: 'stage'`, `by: 'nomos'`) and, when the server wrote it, `untoldSince`. A stage never changes mid-week.
- **A late check-in re-judges.** A check, skip or tally written for a day in a week already judged (said Tuesday for Sunday, or a tick made offline that syncs late) sets the habit's readable `rejudgeFrom` (that week's Monday key; the earlier one wins) in the same `habitDayWrites` write. Whoever looks next (the device's next open or the planner's next visit) re-walks `readHabitStage` from the stage's last start over the judged weeks, in the same transaction shape as the weekly judgement, writes the stage only when it differs, and clears `rejudgeFrom`. A stage change it causes is told like any other (Activity, the habit's page, `untoldSince` when the server made it). Weeks older than the 16 the judgement reads still count in the history but never re-judge. Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-habits` gains a late tick into a judged week moving Building to Holding, an offline tick synced into last week, a tick older than 16 weeks re-judging nothing, the device and the server re-judging once.
- **Telling it.** The device writes each stage change into Activity and onto the habit's page, sealed, in the same batch that clears `untoldSince` (the server can't seal); a change made while Helm was closed is told on the next open, dated the day it happened ("Since Mon: Holding, kept each of the last 3 weeks"). Nothing about a stage is pushed or spoken first.
- **Slipping is automatic** (round 3: `habitSlipping` is gone). Building again needs no offer: adaptive reminders step back up with it, and the line says so ("Reminders come at the start again"). A formed habit whose reminders were on and faded gets them back on its own, told (product, round 3).

### Reminders: opt-in, adaptive, quiet, and kept going by the server


**Why a scheduled function** (owner, round 2 answer: "keep going from the server… never lapses"). Round 2 had the device write three days ahead, so reminders stopped when Helm went unopened.
