Now the server plans every day, from readable fields only, and the site's existing sender delivers. The device only changes what a reminder depends on and stamps that it did.

**The parts.**

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| `markRemindersChanged` (device) | Inside the same write as any change a reminder depends on, sets `HelmProfile.habitRemindersChangedAt = now`: a reminder turned on, off or its pace changed, a window, cadence or `anyTimeAt` edited, a habit paused, resumed, ended, retired, brought back, restarted or deleted, a goal paused or resumed, the zone, Your day, quiet hours or Pause all changed, and an Undo of any of those. A tick never stamps (the send check reads ticks itself) |
| `planHelmReminders` (scheduled, every 5 minutes) | Finds the people to plan, ends pauses that are due, judges stages on a new week, plans the rest of each person's habit day, writes and cancels central `reminders`, and records it all in `apps/helm/reminderQueue/{uid}` |
| `sendScheduledReminders` (site, every minute, unchanged except the hook) | Claims each due reminder, waits out its second, re-reads it, then asks Helm's hook whether it is still wanted and what it says, sends it to every device on `users/{uid}.fcmTokens`, and lets the hook record that it went |
| Helm's send hook (`sendHook.ts`) | `prepare`: drops what was ticked, skipped, paused or turned off since planning, joins any `lockScreenName`s, or returns null to cancel. `settle`: writes `reminded` on the day |

**Finding whom to plan** (two plain queries per run, both on one field, so no new index; section 16):

1. **Due**: `reminderQueue where lookAt <= now + 5 min, orderBy lookAt, limit 300`. `lookAt` is the person's next 03:00 rollover in their zone (null when no habit of theirs reminds and no pause ends), so each person with reminders is planned once a habit day, just after the rollover, for the whole day ahead.
2. **Changed**: `apps/helm/users where habitRemindersChangedAt >= now − 15 min and <= now + 10 min, limit 300`. A person is skipped when their queue's `plannedFor` equals that stamp (already planned for it), so a stamp is answered once, by the run after it; the 15-minute window rides out a failed run or two, and a stamp more than 10 minutes ahead (a wrong device clock) is ignored and answered at the next rollover. A person with no queue yet (their first reminder) is found by this query and gets one.

Each run takes the union, at most 300 people, plans 20 at a time, and stops starting new people at 200 seconds (timeout 240). More than 300 due at once (a thousand people in one zone at 03:00) is fine: `lookAt` stays in the past and the next runs take the rest in order, long before any morning window. Each person is held by a 2-minute lease on their queue (`leaseUntil`, set in a transaction), so overlapping runs never plan one person twice.

**Planning one person** (`planPerson`, all reads by path from the queue's or profile's uid):

1. Read the profile (none: cancel the queue's pending slots and delete the queue), the habits `where status in ['active', 'paused']`, `habitDays` for the last 14 days (16 weeks on a new week), the queue, and the goals any habit serves (`getAll` by id).
2. End pauses whose `pausedUntil` has come: a habit `paused` → `active` with `history` `resumed` and `untoldSince`; a goal resumed with the habits its pause paused (section 28 "Goals"). Readable fields only; the Activity line is the device's on its next open.
3. On a new week, judge stages (above).
4. Unless `remindersPausedAt` is set or the idle rule holds (below), plan the rest of the habit day: `planHabitDay` (pure, vendored) returns the wanted slots after now + 60 seconds, each a fire instant (via `zonedDateTimeToEpoch`), its `habitId/windowId` refs and its day part.
5. Diff against the queue's slots for this habit day (`diffSlots`): create a reminder for each new slot at a fresh random id with `buildReminderDoc` (fixed words, `subjectRefs`, `appId: 'helm'`, `targetUids` and `createdBy` the person, `link: '/helm?view=today'`), and cancel (`status: 'cancelled'`, in a transaction that checks it is still `pending`) each old one that is no longer wanted or covers different refs and fires more than 60 seconds from now; one closer than that is left to the sender, whose hook re-checks it.
6. Write the queue: `slots`, `dayKey`, `plannedAt`, `plannedFor` (the stamp it answered), `lookAt` (the next rollover, or null), `leaseUntil: null`.

**When a reminder fires** (`planHabitDay`; times rounded down to 5 minutes):

| Pace and stage | Fire time for a window | For a habit with no window |
|---|---|---|
| adaptive, Building or Building again | the window's start | `anyTimeAt`, else the middle of Your day |
| adaptive, Holding | 30 minutes before the window ends (halfway through a window under an hour) | 2 hours before Your day ends |
| adaptive, Formed | none (the habit line says "No reminders: it's holding on its own") | none |
| `start` (pinned, and every care habit until eased) | the window's start | as Building |
| `late` (pinned, a care habit eased, "Keep reminding me" on a Formed habit) | as Holding | as Holding |

- **A window that keeps itself goes quiet.** Under adaptive pace, a window ticked before its fire time on every due day of the last 2 weeks gets no reminder until it misses one (computed from `habitDays` at planning, nothing stored), so "Holding" fades window by window.
- **A tally across the day** (8 glasses) gets one reminder in the middle of Your day if under half are counted by then (the hook checks the count), and, when Holding, only the late one.
- **Care habits** start at `start` and stay there; `reminderEase`'s "Only if I'm late" (or "Only if late") sets `late` and `easedAt`. They never go quiet on their own and never stop because of a stage.
- **Quiet hours** (`HelmProfile.quietHours`, null = outside Your day): a fire time inside them moves to the last minute of the window outside them; a window wholly inside them gets nothing unless `allowQuietHours` is on for that habit. The site's own quiet hours in `pushBudget.ts` apply only to pushes nobody asked for, which these are not.
- **Bundling and the cap.** Fire times within 15 minutes of an earlier one join it; a day has at most 6 (`HABIT_REMINDERS_PER_DAY`), any beyond joining the nearest earlier one.
- **The idle rule** (product's proposal, the owner decides; `HABIT_REMINDERS_IDLE_DAYS = 21` or null). When no `habitDays` of the last 21 has `isInUse`, the planner writes one last fixed-words reminder ("Habit reminders are paused until you're back. Open Helm to pick them up.") at the first slot of the day, sets the profile's readable `habitRemindersIdleAt`, and plans nothing more (`lookAt` the next rollover, so it notices a return). The device's next check of anything or capture clears `habitRemindersIdleAt` and stamps the change, so planning resumes within 5 minutes, and Today says once "Reminders are back on". With the constant null, reminders never lapse.

**Checking at the second of sending** (the hook's `prepare(firestore, reminder, now)`): read the profile, the habits named in `subjectRefs` (`getAll`), and the habit day that holds the fire time. Keep a ref only when the habit is `active`, `reminder.isOn`, the profile has no `remindersPausedAt`, the window still holds the fire time (it wasn't moved), and the window has no check (done or skipped) and, for a tally, the count is under its line. Nothing left: return null, and the sender marks the reminder `cancelled` (logged `reminders.notNeeded`, never an error). Otherwise return the push's title and body for what is left. A hook that throws lets the stored words go (a reminder the person asked for is never silently lost) and logs `helm.reminders.checkFailed`. `settle(firestore, reminder, sentAt)` merges `entries.<habitId>.reminded.<windowId> = sentAt` into that day for each kept ref, which is how Formed knows a week was unreminded.

**What a reminder says.** The stored reminder: `title` the assistant's name (readable, `readAssistantName`), `body` from a template keyed on the day part and the count ("Your morning habits are up", "Time for your evening habit", "A habit is waiting for you" when no part is known; product words them). The push may differ from the stored words only by the hook dropping what is done ("habits" becomes "habit") and by adding names the person opted in.

- **"Show its name on the lock screen"** (`LockScreenNameSwitch`, per habit, off by default; owner, round 2 answer). Before it turns on, the switch says plainly that the name will be kept unsealed so the push can show it, that Google's (and Apple's) push service carries it, and that anyone who sees the lock screen can read it; on, it writes `lockScreenName` (the name, ≤60, nothing else) and a `nameShown` history entry. The hook builds the body from the opted names among the refs left ("Pills", "Pills and Floss", "Pills and 1 more evening habit"), never naming an unopted one. The stored reminder never holds a name, so the name never reaches the site's `reminders` collection, another app's listener or the in-app toast. Rename while on: rewritten in the same update; off, ended, retired, deleted: null in the same write. Offered on health and care habits too, with the same words (product's proposal, owner to confirm). Never a command.
- **Opting in** is per habit, off by default, offered once when the habit is made and on its sheet (`HabitReminderRow`, an `AppToggle` with the pace under it). When no device of the person's can take a push (`readDevicePushState` not on and `users/{uid}.fcmTokens` empty), the row shows the shared `PushOptInRow` (design, round 2; section 16), which asks the browser on its own tap and shows the site's words for a blocked or unsupported browser; the habit's `reminder` is written only once some device is on. Turning on writes `{ isOn: true, pace: isCare ? 'start' : 'adaptive', … }`, a `reminderOn` entry, and the stamp; the sheet says "Starts within a few minutes".
- **What the person sees of the plan** comes from the site's reminders slice, not the queue (which is Admin-only): `selectNextHabitReminder` shows "Next reminder 8:30 PM" on the habit's Reminders row from the pending Helm reminders whose `subjectRefs` name it.
- **Clean-up.** Everything that stops a reminder stamps, and the next run cancels; until then the hook drops it. `deleteHelmData` cancels the queue's pending slots, deletes the queue, then the tree (section 16). Sign-out takes the device's token off the person (`releaseDevicePush`, site-wide).

**What it costs** (prices: Firestore $0.06 per 100,000 reads and $0.18 per 100,000 writes, Cloud Scheduler $0.10 a job a month past the free three; not verified from the sandbox, checked in the issue). A typical person: 5 habits with reminders on, about 6 pushes a day.

| Per person, per day | Reads | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| The day's plan after the rollover, plus about 2 re-plans for changes (profile, habits, 14 days, queue, goals: about 22 each) | about 66 | about 10 (6 reminders, queue writes, a few cancels) |
| The weekly stage read (16 weeks, a seventh of it a day) | about 16 | under 1 |
| The sender: its share of the query, claim, re-check, the hook's profile, habits and day (about 6 a push) | about 36 | about 24 (claim, `sent`, `settle`, pruning) |
| The site's reminders listener, which holds about 14 days of the person's finished reminders and re-reads them on each cold start (about 3 a day) | about 270 | 0 |
| **Total** | **about 390** | **about 35** |

That is about $0.009 a person a month. The fixed part is about $0.11 a month whatever the numbers: one Cloud Scheduler job (free if the project still has a free job left) and the two empty queries of 288 runs a day (576 reads). Function time is a few hundred milliseconds a person a day, inside the free tier at these sizes. Pushes are free.

| People with reminders | A month |
|---|---|
| 1 | about $0.12 |
| 100 | about $1 |
| 1,000 | about $9 (about $3 if the site's reminders listener gained a status and recency filter, a site follow-up that helps every app) |

**The emulator path.** The Functions emulator does not run schedules on its own (to be confirmed in the issue), and `useReminderToasts` already fires due reminders locally against the emulators. So both run bodies are exported, `runHelmReminderPlanning(firestore, now)` and the site sender's `runScheduledReminders(firestore, messaging, now)`, and:

- `npm run helm:reminders-tick` (`scripts/helm/remindersTick.ts`) runs one planning pass and one sending pass against the emulators, with `--at <iso>` to plan as of another moment; the Helm seed turns reminders on for two habits and runs it, so a fresh emulator shows "Next reminder" and the in-app toast. With no `fcmTokens` in the emulator the sender reports 0 devices and marks the reminder `sent`, which is what the toast listens for.
- `check:helm-reminders-server` (`scripts/checks/helmRemindersServer.ts`) runs both bodies against the Firestore emulator with a fixed clock and fake messaging: a day planned at the rollover, a tick before the second cancelling it, a bundled reminder reworded, a stage judged once by the server and then skipped by the device, a pause ending, a stamp answered once, the idle rule, `lockScreenName` reaching only the fake push, and the queue denied to clients.

### Formed, and letting it go


- **Formed is reached on its own** (the weekly judgement); a week later, if the habit is still Formed, the daily check raises `habitFormed` as the day's one offer on Today and on the habit's sheet, its statement and evidence code-built ("Floss seems to be part of your day now · Kept 72 times since Aug 24"). Choices: **Let it go** / "Take it off Today" (effect `retireHabit`) and **Keep counting** / "Keep it on Today", which is a no under the back-off (4 weeks, then 8, then never unless reopened) and changes nothing: the habit stays Formed on Today, its adaptive reminders silent. Never for a health or carer's habit (they never reach Formed).
- **Letting it go** is one batch with one execution (section 9): the habit `status: 'retired'`, `retiredAt`, `formedSummary` (`buildFormedSummary`: done and due since `startsOn`, from a one-shot read of its `habitDays`, at most 2 years of 365 small documents, only at this moment), `reminder.isOn: false`, `lockScreenName: null`, `history` `retired`, the stamp; the pattern `accepted`; and the moment on the self who (`sourceHabitId` set). The next run cancels its reminders. What it leaves: the moment, the read-only page under the Habits list's "Formed" fold, and its `habitDays` entries.
- **Retiring without the offer**: from the habit's sheet or `updateHabit retire`, the same batch with no pattern, only for a Formed habit; refused for a care habit with a code-built line (a care habit is ended, never retired; lead, round 2), which offers ending instead.
- **Undo** of a letting-go restores the earlier fields by D1 (reminders as they were, the stamp re-written), deletes the moment while unchanged, and counts as a no on the pattern. **Bring it back** later is a separate change: `status: 'active'`, `stage: 'formed'` (product, round 3: back as Formed), `retiredAt` and `formedSummary` cleared, reminders off, `history` `broughtBack`, the stamp; the moment stays; the `habitFormed` pattern is `retired`, so it is offered again only after the habit next reaches Formed afresh.

### Importance: what matters most, in the person's words


Owner, round 2 answer: importance is said in natural language, stored, used, not capped at two, and never everything equally high. Product (round 3) words the behaviour; this is how it is kept.

- **Kept on the thing, sealed.** Each project (goals included) and habit carries `sealed.importance: Importance | null` (section 2): a `rank` number, the person's `words`, `saidAt`, an optional `until` and the turn it came from. Ranks are only relative: higher comes first, equal ranks are side by side, any negative rank is "Can wait", null is unplaced. Runway's sealed fractional `orderIndex` is the precedent. Because it is sealed the server never reads it outside a callable that holds the key, so neither the reminder planner nor any scheduled job uses it.
- **Health and care are never placed** (lead, round 3). A habit with `isCare` (and a carer's habit) never carries an entry: `applyImportance` refuses it and the reply is code-built ("Pills is always kept, so it doesn't need a place"), the which-one questions never offer one, and the order view shows them together in one "Always kept" row above the order, not draggable. A task keeps its own Must, Should or Could; the order only breaks ties among tasks equal on those (lead, round 3).
- **One order** (`readImportanceOrder(items, today)`, pure, `engine/importance.ts`): running projects, goals and non-care habits; a habit with no entry of its own sits at its goal's rank (lead, round 3: other habits follow their goal's place); an entry whose `until` has passed reads as unplaced; finished, ended and deleted things are left out (a reopened one comes back with its rank); paused and set-aside things keep their place, marked. The order is the placed ones by rank (ties side by side), then the unplaced by nearest `targetDate` or dated milestone, soonest first, then those with no date, then Can wait. `isAbove(a, b)` is true when a's place is above b's in that order, where among unplaced things only a date at least 7 days nearer counts as above (nothing else separates them).
- **Said, read, placed.** The model reads the words into `setImportance` relations (section 5); `applyImportance(order, command)` turns each into a rank: `most` = one above the current top (alone first); `above`/`below` = the midpoint between the other's rank and its neighbour's; `same` = the other's rank; `matters` = first when nothing is placed, else no write and a `whereDoesItGo` question; `canWait` = below the lowest Can wait; `clear` = null. "The wedding matters most, then work" is `most` for the wedding then `below` the wedding for work, applied in order in one execution. The code, never the model, does the arithmetic, and the reply's order sentence is built from the result.
- **Ties.** Two things may share a place; after applying, `findImportanceTie(order)` returns any place that 3 or more now share (lead, round 3: at the top or anywhere), and the turn carries one `whichComesFirst` pick-one (each of them and Skip) under the reply. The pick moves just above the others, which keep sharing their place ("Which comes first?" at the top; "Which of these comes before the others?" lower down, product's words). Skip leaves the tie; the question waits in Needs you; the tie's members sorted by date stand in meanwhile. The question records the tie's ids, so it is asked again only when a new thing joins the top. Two side by side asks nothing.
- **Renumbering.** When a midpoint would fall within 2^-30 of a neighbour, `renumberRanks` spaces every rank evenly (keeping ties and order) in the same execution; Undo restores the earlier ranks.
- **Shown and changed.** `ImportanceRow` on a project's, goal's and habit's sheet ("Comes first · you said Nov 8"); "What matters most" in How {Name} sees you is `ImportanceSheet`, the order with ties side by side and Can wait at the foot, where a thing moves up, down, beside another, to Can wait or out, each through `applyImportance` as one execution with Undo. The list is a new shared `ReorderList` (`src/components`, no app words: press and hold to drag nearby, with a place sheet for long jumps; design, round 3), since no reorderable list exists in `src/components` today (`PhotoSetPicker`'s hold-and-drag is a photo grid, not a list); it is built in Issue 112 (what matters most) with its Pattern-index line. A finished thing leaving the order is said in the finish line by the device; nothing is rewritten on the others (their ranks still stand).
- **Where it is used.** The planner's tie-break among tasks equal in due date and Must/Should/Could (a task in a higher-placed project first); answers ("what should I work on?") through the `<importance>` context block; and guardrails' `starved` reason and naming rules (below). Never to nag, never spoken first, never pushed.
- **In the prompt.** `<importance>`: up to 30 placed entries in order, each its alias, place words ("first", "beside p3", "can wait"), the person's words and the day, so "what matters most to me?" is answered from the record.

### Guardrails: pause this, or maybe don't start it now


A guardrail is advice, never a block: whatever the person chooses happens, and "Keep going" is always one of the choices. It reasons only from records (counts, dates, lengths, the calendar's busy time, check-ins), shows those reasons, and is a pattern record (one per kind and subject), so it shares the states, the back-off, "stop suggesting that", Reopen, Undo and the pacing with everything else Helm offers. A care item is never its subject.

**Is it a pattern? Yes.** A guardrail needs exactly what a pattern has: a stable identity (kind and subject), a state, a remembered no with a growing wait, evidence shown with it, an effect applied with Undo, and a home on "What I've noticed". A second collection would duplicate all of it and split the one-a-day pacing. What differs is carried by the shape: the `guardrail` family, the `load` evidence, `moment: 'onStart'`, and the `pause` effect. For a guardrail the signature leaves the bucket out (a busy stretch's dates are evidence, not identity), so declining "pause piano for the busy stretch" counts toward the same no next time, whatever the stretch.

**The signals** (pure, `engine/guardrails/`; every threshold a light call in `GUARDRAIL_LIMITS`, tuned in the issue):

| Kind | Computed from | Speaks when | Subject (and `otherId`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tooManyGoals` | `readGoalLoad`: goals with `status: 'active'`; for each, the last day it showed up (a check of one of its habits, a task in it done, a milestone reached) | **On start**: a new goal while 3 or more are already active. **Daily**: 4 or more active and one has not shown up for 21 days | the new goal (`otherId` the quietest active goal), or the quiet goal |
| `deadlineWontFit` | `readDeadlineFit`: for each active project or goal with a `targetDate` or a dated open milestone within 60 days, the work before it (its open, not latent tasks due by then or undated, each `durationMinutes` or 30 like the planner) against `readFreeMinutes` from today to that day; dated items are served earliest date first, so an earlier deadline takes its share first | The work is more than the free time by at least 60 minutes, with 3 or more open tasks in it. **On start**: a new project, goal or dated task makes an existing one stop fitting. **Daily**: one no longer fits | the item that won't fit (`otherId` the new thing that pushed it over), or on start the new thing |
| `starved` | `readStarved` over `readImportanceOrder` (round 3, replacing round 2's `readPriority`): an item is starved when it had no task done, milestone reached, habit check or plan slot kept for 14 days while an item it `isAbove` had 3 or more in the same 14 days; nothing is starved when nothing is placed and no dates separate them | **Daily**, and **on start** when something placed is already starved and the new thing sits below it | the lower item that took the time (`otherId` the starved one) |
| `busyStretch` | `readBusyStretch`: over the next 14 days, a day is full when the calendar's busy time (events not marked free, all-day only when marked busy, rest days as full) is 75% or more of Your day; a stretch is 3 full days in a row or 5 full days in the 14 | **On start**: a new goal, project or habit (not care) starting inside the stretch. **Daily**: once per stretch, a goal whose regular step is due on its full days | the new thing, or the goal (its stretch in the evidence) |

- **The bar** (lead, round 2: design's, logged): `meetsGuardrailBar(reasons)` shows a guardrail only when its subject has **two reasons of different kinds** at threshold at once (say `busyStretch` and `tooManyGoals`), **or one `deadlineWontFit`**. A single task is a subject only for its own deadline that can't fit. Every candidate reason for a subject is computed each time and never stored; when the bar is met, one pattern is written whose `kind` is the strongest reason (a deadline first, then `starved`, `tooManyGoals`, `busyStretch`) and whose `reasons` holds every reason that met its threshold (≤3), so `GuardrailSheet` shows each with its evidence. The signature stays kind and subject, so the same subject answered "Keep going" is one record whatever mix of reasons comes next; coming back needs one of the shown reasons stronger or changed (layer 4).
- **What a guardrail may name** (lead, round 3). The thing it offers to pause is the **lowest-placed** eligible running thing in `readImportanceOrder` (Can wait first, then unplaced with no date, then upward), never a health or care item, never the first thing or anything above the thing that is starved, and never anything with a date or dated milestone within 14 days. If nothing is eligible, no guardrail shows. On start, the new thing is eligible like any other (so "Start it later" is offered only when the new thing is the lowest eligible, else "Pause {the lowest} instead").
- **Free time is the planner's arithmetic.** `readFreeMinutes(day)` is Your day minus busy blocks and the planner's 15-minute buffers, times `planFillPercent`, with rest days at zero: the same function the planner's input uses, so a guardrail never disagrees with the plan. Days past the eager calendar window come from the daily check's one-shot read of `synced where kind == 'event' and startAt` up to 60 days ahead (the existing `synced (kind, startAt)` index; readable `startAt`, `endAt`, `isBusy` and `isAllDay` only, nothing opened); a day with no calendar connected counts Your day times `planFillPercent`, and the evidence line says so ("No calendar connected, so counting your whole day").
- **What each offers**, as the pattern's effect (product words them): on start, **Start it later** (`pause` with `until` the day after the stretch, or open-ended for a goal load), **Pause {the lowest-placed eligible} instead** (`pause` on it), **Keep going** (`none`, a no). Daily, **Pause it** (until a date or open-ended), **Change the date** (opens the project's date; the pattern stays `suggested` until the date moves, then it no longer fits or it does), **Keep going**. A `pause` on a goal pauses its habits (section 28, "Goals"); on a habit it sets `status: 'paused'` and `pausedUntil`. Each Accept is one execution with Undo (section 9).
- **Where it runs.** *On start*: `useStartCheck` runs `checkOnStart(newRecord, slices, freeTimeTable)` on the device when a project, goal, habit or dated task arrives in the slice from a turn's execution or from a form (never for a record another device made, which it tells apart by the execution's `createdAt` within the last minute and the session), and shows at most one guardrail as `GuardrailLine` under that turn's card or in the form's saved toast's place, not spoken (a guardrail is never read aloud unless the person asks "why?"). It needs no read: the slices, plus the free-time table the daily check kept in memory (without one, the eager 7 days and Your day for the rest). *Daily*: `useDailyCheck` runs `checkDaily` after noticing, and its winner competes for the day's one offer.
- **Never moves or stops anything on its own**, never on a health or care item (lead, round 3: nor anything due within 14 days or placed above what is starved), never in the first 7 days after Helm's first habit or goal (nothing to judge from yet), and never while learning is switched off (`isLearningOn`).

### The daily check, and what it costs


- **On the device, at most once a day.** `useDailyCheck` runs after the key and the eager listeners are ready, on the first open of a local day, and writes `HelmProfile.patternsCheckedOn` so a second device that day skips it. In order: write Activity and page lines for every server-made change (`untoldSince` on a habit: a stage, a pace step, a pause ended), sealed, clearing `untoldSince` in the same batch; end any pause still due (when the planner hasn't, for a person without reminders); judge last week's stages if `stageWeek` is older; clear importance entries whose `until` passed, with their page line; weigh unseen reflections and drop observations whose source is gone; noticing, `saidStruggling` included; habit-life offers (`habitFormed` a week after Formed, `reminderEase` at Holding or a carer's third kept week); guardrails. It no longer plans reminders. It reads 8 weeks of `habitDays` and `planDays` by id range (16 weeks on a week's first open), tasks with `completedAt` in those weeks (cap 500), up to 60 days of calendar events' readable fields, and the `patterns` already in the slice.
- **What runs on the server, and what doesn't** (round 3, superseding round 2's "Why not a scheduled function"). The owner's answer moved reminders, pause endings and stage judging to `planHelmReminders`, because each needs only readable fields and each must happen while Helm is closed. Noticing, offers, guardrails, importance and every sealed line stay on the device. Owner review 1 adds one server job that does need the key: the nightly reflective pass, because reading what was said across two weeks is a model's job and a batch halves its price; it writes candidates only, and the device still decides what to show ("Learning from what is said").
- **Cost on the device**: about 700 reads a day per active person, a handful of writes, no model spend. The server's share is in "Reminders" above.

### Private is a class, not only health


The owner asked "are we saying the user indicates healthcare habit, or that the user can just indicate that they want something that is private". Both: **private** is a class any record can carry, and health and care are one way in. Until now only habits carried `isCare` and only habit reminders pushed.

**Which records.** Task (repeating ones too), habit, project and goal, note, topic, fact, who, place, procedure, scheduled action and research. Each gains:

```ts
privacy: 'ordinary' | 'private';            // READABLE: the class in effect, so code without the key honours it
// sealed, beside the record's words:
privateMark: 'health' | 'care' | 'person' | null; // the record's own mark, not one it inherits
isPrivacyCleared: boolean;                  // the person said it needn't be private: code never marks it again
```

Readers default a record from before to `'ordinary'`, `null` and `false`. `isCare` stays as it is (what learning may change); a care thing is always private, but a private thing is not always care.

**Three sources** (product, review 4):
1. **Health and care, set by code.** `readPrivateMark` (pure, `utils/privacy.ts`, server copy under `check:server-copies`) marks `care` when `isCare` is true or a habit or task is for a who other than the self, and `health` when the model's command says `privateMark: 'health'` (medicine, dose, treatment, a symptom, therapy, someone's care), when a `remember` is `sensitive` health, or, as a floor when the model misses it, when the words match `HEALTH_WORDS` (a short closed list in `constants.ts`: pill, medicine, medication, dose, prescription, refill, inhaler, insulin, therapy, treatment) through `createOptionMatcher`. The model can add a mark, never take one off. **Helm never unmarks a health or care thing on its own**: only the person's switch or their own words naming the thing clear it.
   **The person says it is medicine.** `HEALTH_WORDS` holds no drug names (a closed list can't keep up), so a missed medicine ("start my Lexapro") is caught by the person: a habit's sheet (and a task's, for a repeating dose) carries "This is medicine or care", and "this is a medicine" in a turn is `setCare { subjectAlias, isCare }` (≤3 a turn, through `habitWrites`/`taskWrites`, Undo). On, it writes `isCare: true` and `privateMark: 'health'`; off clears both and sets `isPrivacyCleared` (the same one-confirm unmark as any Helm-set mark). **Asked once**: a habit the person marked private with a dose-like count (`DOSE_PATTERN`: a number with mg, mcg, ml, units, tablet, pill, capsule, drop or puff, or a `timesPerDay`/windows count of 2 or more) and not care gets one code-built yesNo "Is this a medicine?" on the turn that made it or the sheet's next open; asking stamps the habit's readable `careAskedAt`, so it never asks again (a one-off flag on the habit, not the back-off: Skip counts as asked). Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-private` gains the switch on and off, `setCare` by voice, the one ask on a dose-like private habit and never twice.
2. **The person.** "Keep this private" (a `setPrivate` command, or `isPrivate: true` on the create command in the same turn), or the Private switch on the thing's sheet.
3. **Inherited.** While a thing is filed under a private project (`projectId`) or is about a private who or place (`whoIds`, `forWhoId`, `subjectIds`, `placeIds`), it is private. It stops being private when it leaves that project or that link goes, unless it has its own mark.

`privacy` = (an own mark and not cleared) or any parent private, worked out by `readEffectivePrivacy(record, parents)` (pure, server copy). **It is known at filing time:** the executor computes it inside the turn's transaction from the command's mark and the parents in the context, so the device's own writes and the executor agree (the lock is the only mark in lists). On the device `deriveRecordPrivacy` is the one deriver (`planCascade`, `readRecordPrivacy` and every create path use it); a cascade takes the changed parent's new facts from the write itself (`withParentFacts`), never the store, is skipped without a parent, is written with `allSettled` and reports a partial failure, and a child whose parent can't be read is never downgraded. `keepInheritedPrivate` runs in chunks of 10 and refuses the delete or forget unless every child is saved. A task's class on the server is its stored `privacy` or its derived one, so a task private only through a parent stays private when completed, and linking a private person or place, or retitling with a health word, recomputes it. A change of link, mark or a parent's class rewrites the children's `privacy` in the same action (write batches of 450; the device reads parents live anyway, so a child whose rewrite hasn't landed still shows as private). An inherited class can't be cleared on the child: the sheet says where it comes from and opens the project or the person.

**What private changes, and what it doesn't.**
- **Never named outside Helm unless the person turns that on.** Outside means a push, the lock screen, a share, a copy, an export, or something written to another service.
- **Pushes: fixed words for everything, private or not.** Every pushable kind (habit reminders now; task reminders, scheduled actions and repeating tasks when they push) uses fixed words by default, and its "Show its name on the lock screen" switch (`lockScreenName`, section 14) is off by default for every thing. On a private thing that switch is the person's opt-in for that thing, and its sheet says the thing is private before it turns on. `planHelmReminders` and the send hook need nothing new: they never read words.
- **Copies and exports leave private things out and say so** (product). `filterPrivate(items)` (pure, `utils/privacy.ts`) runs in every copy and export Helm makes (a list copied, Issue 66's export to Google Docs, any later share): the result says how many were left out ("2 private things left out") with "Include them" for that one copy only, nothing remembered. Copying one private thing from its own page copies it as usual. Every copy still goes through `copyToClipboard`.
- **Proposals that leave Helm carry the flag** (product): a drafted message or document (Issue 65), a calendar proposal (Issue 67), a scheduled action's outbound kind and the two-way calendar sync. The approval row shows "· Private"; an approved event for a private thing is written with Google Calendar's `visibility: 'private'`; the two-way sync, which writes without an approval each time, writes a private thing as "Busy" with no title (light call).
- **Inside Helm, nothing changes.** A private thing shows on every screen as before, is sent to the model in a turn (it needs it to answer), and is spoken aloud as usual when the person spoke, except in an unattended wake-phrase turn (below).
- **A wake-phrase turn nobody is looking at names no private thing (owner).** When a turn starts by the wake phrase while the phone is locked or the page untouched, private things are answered without their names ("Your morning medicine is ticked", "You have one private thing at 3") until the person taps the phone; then normal. **How the device knows**: each turn carries `presence: { isHidden: boolean; isUntouched: boolean }`, `isHidden` from `document.visibilityState` (a locked phone hides the page) and `isUntouched` true until the first `pointerdown` or `keydown` since the page opened; the turn is unattended when it began by the wake phrase and either is true. **Code masks, not the model**: for an unattended turn `buildContext` sends each private record under a code-built label (`readMaskedLabel`: neutral, "a private task this morning" from its window, never an inferred kind such as medicine; "a private thing" otherwise, with its time), so the model never sees the name, and `maskPrivateNames(reply, names)` (pure, server copy) replaces any private name still in the reply text (the person's own words can carry one) before it is spoken, shown or sealed. The result cards draw from the slice and show the label while the session is unattended; the first touch ends it for the session and the cards show names, while the spoken words stay as they were said. The device's own check-in match uses the same label. The mask is threaded into the MCP scope, so `search_tasks`, `get_calendar`, `search_synced`, `get_topic` and `search_procedures` return private records as labels and push their words into the names; the already-made lines, earlier turns (a turn that touched something private reads "Something private was said/done.") and a topic path under a private ancestor are masked too, and an open run's class is read from its procedure's own `privacy`. On an unattended turn no `setPrivate` release is applied, and a turn never clears a mark code set (it says to change it on its page); `setPrivate` of either direction needs the person's words quoted, and on a new task it makes the task private at creation. Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-private` gains a hidden page, an untouched page, a touch ending it, a private name in the person's words masked, and an ordinary thing named as usual.
- **Derived records are private when what they rest on is.** Patterns, observations, reflections, seen-as lines, Activity lines (executions), moments, timeline entries and Today's lines quote or summarise their sources, so each derived document carries the readable `privacy` too, worked out by code: `readDerivedPrivacy(sourceKeys, classOf)` (pure, server copy) is `'private'` when any source is private. A capture counts as a private source when its turn filed or touched a private thing (`captures/{id}.touchesPrivate`, readable, set by the executor); an observation also reads its `subject`; a goal's why summary lives on the goal and has its class; Today's lines are selectors and read the subject's class. Each derived record's writer sets it at write time; when a source's class changes, the same rewrite pass that rewrites children also rewrites the derived records found by `sourceKeys array-contains` (the cascade's single-field query). A derived record has no mark of its own and no switch: to change it, change its source. `filterPrivate` covers every derived kind (copying "What I've noticed", Activity, the seen-as page, a timeline), and `check:helm-private` gains each derived kind made private by one private source, a source going private rewriting its derived records, a source going ordinary releasing them, and no copy quoting a private source.
- **The nightly batch reads it like the rest** (owner, review 4). Private is not a batch question; section 5's test asks only about data-handling terms, and today every call runs under the same standard terms.

**Commands** (through `normalizeNomosReply`, like every command): `setPrivate { subjectAlias, isPrivate: boolean }` for any `t#`, `h#`, `p#`, `n#`, `tp#`, `f#`, `w#`, `pl#`, `pr#`, `sa#` or `r#` in the context; at most 5 a turn; an `update` effect with Undo, written by the thing's own write builder. `isPrivate: false` is kept only when the turn's intent is a request and the words name the thing; on an inherited class it is refused with a code-built line ("It's private because Mom is private. Change it on her page?"). Create commands (`createTask`, `createHabit`, `createProject`, `createNote`, `remember`, `upsertWho`, `upsertPlace`, `createProcedure`, `scheduleAction`, `saveForLater`) gain `privateMark: 'health' | 'care' | null` and `isPrivate: boolean`. Showing a name outside is never a single command: "Include them" stays on the copy, and the lock-screen name (owner) is a two-step confirm, code-enforced: "show my pills' name on the lock screen" makes the model propose `askShowName { subjectAlias }`, which writes nothing; code builds a yesNo question that speaks and shows the switch's own warning (the name is kept unsealed, the push service carries it, anyone who sees the lock screen can read it); only a tapped Yes, or a spoken yes matched on the device by `matchSpokenChoice`'s yes words, runs `habitWrites.setLockScreenName` on the device (which holds the key and the name). An answer the device can't match goes to the model as usual but can't turn it on: code asks again. Hiding a name by voice (`hideName { subjectAlias }`) is one step, with Undo. Check: `check:helm-private` gains a voice request writing nothing until yes, a model-read yes refused, a spoken yes and a tap each turning it on, hide in one step.

**Rules.** `privacy` is a readable field under the owner wildcard, and the beta tier checks ownership only, so no rule changes (`firestore-rules.md`: a new field needs no rules change). Rules can't stop the owner flipping their own flag, and needn't: it only changes what their own device and their own pushes show.

**Checks.** `check:helm-private`: each source (care, a health word the model missed, the person, a private project, a private who); leaving the project; a cleared mark not re-marked by code; a health mark never cleared by code; `filterPrivate` counts and "Include them" for one copy; one thing copied from its page; a calendar proposal carrying `visibility: 'private'`; `setPrivate` refused on an inherited class. `check:helm-copy` gains: every copy or export in `src/apps/helm/` goes through `filterPrivate`.

**As built in Issue 103.** The class ships for the kinds that exist today: task, project, who, place, note, topic, fact, procedure, key date, execution and capture (habits, goals, scheduled actions and research arrive with their own issues and add `privacy` there). The sealed `privateMark` and `isPrivacyCleared` are required `T | null` keys in the types; every listener defaults them for a record from before (null and false), so it reads as ordinary, and records a turn seals on the server carry them only where it sets a mark (a task or a fact), which the same defaults read. `utils/privacy.ts` is the pure core with a server copy (`functions/src/apps/helm/privacy.ts`, `check:server-copies`); `utils/privacyParents.ts` finds each record's parents and plans the cascade and `planKeepOnLeaving`; `store/actions/privacyActions.ts` is the one writer (`setPrivate`, `keepInheritedPrivate`) and re-seals the marks in a transaction, so a switch on the device needs a connection and says so. `deleteProject`, `forgetWho` and `forgetPlace` call `keepInheritedPrivate` first, so each child that inherited the class gets its own mark: `readLinkedChildren` queries the database for every task, fact, note and topic linked to the parent (not only those the screen loaded) before the parent goes, on the device and not the server, and `setPrivate` on a parent rewrites its children's class the same way. A child's class change is its own write per child in batches of 450, not one transaction with the parent's delete. On the server `setPrivate` is a `NomosCommand` for tasks (`t#`, at most the turn's `MAX_COMMANDS`, `isPrivate: false` kept only with the person's quoted words saying private or public, refused on an inherited class with the code-built line, which the turn reports with its other not-kept lines); a task or fact made by a turn carries the health mark when its words match `HEALTH_WORDS` (a fact also when it was kept as sensitive) and is stored private from the start when it is filed under or about a private project, person or place (`planWrites` reads each parent's mark from the people and projects the turn already read); a turn's execution carries `privacy` and its capture `touchesPrivate` when it touched a private task, made a private record, or gave an answer that repeats the words of a private record read into its context (an exact match of its words, so a paraphrase is not caught). The unattended turn is `presence` on the capture (`startedByWake`, `isHidden`, `isUntouched`): `nomos/unattended.ts` gives the model the label of a private open or on-screen task and `maskContextRecords` covers every other record that reaches the context (facts, the person's own facts, key dates and upcoming dates through their person, projects, topics, notes, procedures with the open one and its runs, and synced copies), each by a fixed label such as "a private note", and `maskPrivateNames` runs over the reply line and answer text before they are kept or spoken. Not built here: patterns, observations, reflections, seen-as lines and habits (the kinds don't exist yet; each adds `readDerivedPrivacy` on arrival); `setPrivate` by voice for kinds other than tasks (the device switch covers every kind; the voice command is a task command because notes, topics, facts and procedures undo through versions and projects, people and places have no alias in the context, so it needs its own alias books and per-kind Undo); `presence` carries a third field, `startedByWake`, beside the page's hidden and untouched flags. Copies: Tasks' "Copy list" goes through `filterPrivate` with an "Include them" action on its toast; a note's or a procedure's own copy is `filterPrivateOwnPage`; `check:helm-copy` fails a file that copies without `filterPrivate`.

### Where things live


The owner's concern: "data in too many places and the agents don't know where to look", and "central state: no risk of things being overridden or forgotten or falling between the cracks". This table is the map, and the assistant's tool contract. Its one source is `WHERE_THINGS_LIVE` in `src/apps/helm/whereThingsLive.ts`; this table mirrors it.

| Thing | Its home | Its one writer | Derived records that cite it | The tool that reads it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Habit (care habits included) | `habits/{id}` | `habitWrites`, from the habit sheet or `createHabit`/`updateHabit`; `stage`, `stageWeek`, `untoldSince` by `judgeStage` (device or planner) | observations and patterns (subject), reminders (`subjectRefs`), a formed habit's moment (`sourceHabitId`) | `<habits>`, `search_habits` |
| Check-in (tick, skip, tally) | `habitDays/{dayKey}.entries.<habitId>` | `habitDayWrites`, from a tap or `checkIn`; `reminded` only by the sender's hook; `isInUse` by the day's first use | stage and goal evidence (computed, never stored), pattern evidence | `<habits>` (today), `search_habits` |
| Check-in note | `habitDays/{dayKey}.sealed.notes` | `habitDayWrites.note`, from the sheet or `checkIn.noteSpanId` | observations (`checkInNote` at night, or `turn` at once) | `search_habits` (its range's notes) |
| "How was it?" and its answer | asked: `followUpAt`/`followUpState` on the day; answer: a capture with `answersQuestion` | the turn's executor; `resolveHelmQuestion` | observations (`followUp`) | `search_said` |
| What was said | `observations/{id}` | derived: `sortHelmCapture` (a turn), `runHelmReflection` (notes) | patterns (`said` evidence), reflections | `<said>`, `search_said` |
| Pattern, guardrail or habit-life offer, and the person's answer | `patterns/{id}` | `patternWrites`, from `patternActions` or `setPatternState`; evidence and lines only by the detector that found it | none; the answer is the person's and survives a rebuild of the evidence | `<patterns>`, `search_patterns` |
| Nightly candidates | `reflections/{dayKey}` | derived: `runHelmReflection` only | patterns made from them | none (the device weighs them) |
| How Nomos sees you: tendencies, what works, rhythm, how they talk | `seenAs/{lineId}` | derived: `runHelmReflection`; the correction and removal by `seenAsActions` (the person's, kept through rebuilds) | none | `<how_you_seem>`, the page |
| Personality: traits, honesty, clash winners | `HelmProfile.personality` | `profileActions.setPersonality` (the personality sheet) | the preview in `style/general` (`previewHash`) | `<personality>` |
| Where someone lives and will be away | the who record's sealed `home` and `away` (the self who for the person) | `whoWrites`, from the who page or `upsertWho` with a city span | none; whereabouts are computed | `<whereabouts>`, `search_who` |
| Where the person is right now | `HelmProfile.currentPlace` (said, lapses after 3 h); with phone location on, `nearNow` in the self who's sealed words (the city, and the point only at Precise; lapses after 6 h) | the device | none | `<whereabouts>` |
| Whether a turn acted or was noted | `captures/{id}.disposition` | the executor (`readActNow`) | none | Activity, the nightly pass |
| How an appointment meets | `tasks/{id}.meets` and its `placeIds` | `taskWrites` (person or turn); the calendar fills a null | none | `<open_tasks>`, `get_calendar` |
| Goal | `projects/{id}`, `kind: 'goal'` | `projectWrites`, from the goal sheet or `createProject`/`updateProject` | guardrails, research (`projectId`); evidence computed | `<projects>`, `search_projects` |
| A goal's why, the person's words | `projects/{id}` sealed `why.verbatim` | `goalWhyWrites`, from `whySpanId` or the sheet | the tidied version, summary and tags (`basedOn`) | `<projects>` (the shown version), `search_projects` (all three) |
| A goal's why, tidied; its summary and tags | `why.cleaned`; `why.summary`, `why.tags` | derived: the turn that brought the words; a stale summary by the nightly pass | none | as above |
| Importance | sealed on the project or habit | `applyImportance`, from `setImportance` or the order sheet | guardrails' `starved`, the planner's tie-break (computed) | `<importance>` |
| Task | `tasks/{id}` | `taskWrites`, from Tasks or `createTask`/`updateTask`/`completeTask` | plan slots, guardrails' fit | `<open_tasks>`, `search_tasks` |
| Repeating task (Issue 70) | `tasks/{id}` with `repeat` | `taskWrites`; the tick makes the next (`${seriesId}-${dayKey}`) | the next occurrence | `search_tasks` |
| Procedure and its runs | `procedures/{id}` + `versions`; `procedureRuns/{id}` | `procedureWrites` | runs | `search_procedures`, `get_doc` |
| Scheduled action | `scheduledActions/{id}` | `scheduledActionWrites`, from `scheduleAction` or its sheet | its runs (captures with `scheduledActionId` and their executions); the server queue (computed) | `list_scheduled_actions` |
| Learn later; research ahead of a project | `research/{id}` | `researchWrites`, from `saveForLater`, `offerResearch` and its answers; prepared parts by `runHelmReflection` | the topic the person keeps (`resultTopicId`, which cites it) | `<research>`, `search_research`; on the device, Knowledge › Learn later and Search |
| Knowledge | `notes/{id}`, `topics/{id}` + `versions` | as built (issues 48–51) | topic documents cite their notes | `search_knowledge`, `get_doc` |
| People, places, key dates, facts | `who`, `places`, `keyDates`, `facts` | as built | `meta/keyDateIndex`, rewritten in the same transaction | `search_who`, `search_memory` |
| Helm's plan for a day | `planDays/{dayKey}` | the device planner | none | `get_calendar` (slots) |
| What the assistant did | `executions/{id}` | the executor; Undo | none | Activity |
| Habit reminders | central `reminders`, `reminderQueue/{uid}` | `planHelmReminders` only | none | `<habits>` (reminder state) |
| Calendar events | Google Calendar; a sealed read cache in `synced` | the platform's sink only | none | `get_calendar` |
| Another app's planned things (meals, pickups, trips, bills) | that app; a synced category's sealed cache in `synced`, a live one never stored | that app; the sink writes the cache | none | `search_synced`, `search_app_records` |
| Going Once's things (drafts, not ready) | Going Once, read live | Going Once | none | `search_app_records` |
| Mail and Drive | Google, read live; only an answer's quotes are kept | Google | a fact a question noted cites its quote | `search_mail`, `read_mail`, `search_documents`, `read_document` |

**The rules.**
- **One home per fact.** Something that seems to need a second copy gets a derived record or a selector instead.
- **One writer per field.** A field is written only through its write builder (`habitWrites`, `habitDayWrites`, `goalWhyWrites`, `patternWrites`, `researchWrites` and the like), a pure function that returns the writes; the device's actions and the server's executor both call it (the server's copy under `check:server-copies`), so a tap and a sentence make the same change. A field a server job owns (`reminded`, `stage`) names that job in the table.
- **Derived records say what they rest on.** Each carries `sourceKeys` (`'<collection>/<id>'`, with `#<path>` for part of a document), is never edited by hand and can be rebuilt from its sources. A pattern is derived in its evidence and lines, but its state is the person's answer, so a rebuild keeps the answer.
- **A delete takes what cites it.** `cascadeFor(sourceKey)` (`engine/cascade.ts`, server copy) reads `DERIVED_FROM` and, in the same write batch as the delete, deletes the observations that rest only on it, rebuilds a pattern's `said` evidence without it (a pattern left under its threshold that nobody answered is deleted; an answered one keeps its answer and the evidence left), and deletes unseen reflections that cite it. A delete made offline is finished by the daily check, which drops observations whose source is gone (`getAll`, at most 300). Deleting a habit also stamps the reminder change; a formed habit's moment is the person's once written, so it stays with `sourceHabitId` set to null. `deleteHelmData` takes the whole tree.
- **Nothing is left orphaned.** `readDeleteImpact(record)` (pure, `engine/cascade.ts`, server copy) lists what a delete would strand, and the confirm names it, code-built. **Deleting a goal, or turning it into a project** (`kind` → `'project'`): its habits (`projectId`) are named with "Keep them" (the default: each becomes a standalone habit, `projectId` set to null, and leaves the goal's importance place: a habit that followed its goal in the order becomes unplaced, `importance` null, rather than inheriting a place nobody gave it) or "Delete them" (each habit's ordinary delete, reminders cancelled through `habitRemindersChangedAt`); the same choice is a pick-one when the turn asks for it by voice. **Forgetting a who**: the care habits for them (`forWhoId`) are named and ended (`status: 'ended'`, reminder off, the reminder change stamped) in the same action, with Undo restoring both. **A link that goes by deletion keeps its privacy**: when a private project, who or place is deleted or forgotten, every child that was private only through it gets the last class as its own mark (`privateMark: 'care'` from a care who, `'person'` otherwise) in the same write batch, so nothing silently stops being private; moving a thing out of a private project by the person still clears it, as before. Decider: code. Checks: `check:helm-where` (a goal deleted with Keep and with Delete, a goal turned into a project, a who forgotten ending their care habits and cancelling reminders, Undo) and `check:helm-private` (an orphan keeping its mark).
- **Other apps are read through their connection.** Nothing an app owns becomes a Helm record. Live categories are never stored beyond the quotes an answer keeps. Synced categories (calendar events, another app's planned things) keep sealed copies in `synced` as a read cache with one writer (the sink): never edited, never the home of a fact, deleted with the grant. Everything new in this area is live or rides on an existing synced category. (Owner review 2 settled it: the caches stay, one writer, never a home.)
- **A new record type is not done without a row.** Owner review 2 adds: nor is a learned result. Anything Helm works out about the person (a pattern, a candidate, a stage, a profile line, a summary) is a synced record; computing on the device is fine, keeping the answer only there is not ("Nothing learned lives on one device"). `check:helm-where` fails when a collection in `types.ts` or in the rules' wildcard list has no row in `WHERE_THINGS_LIVE`, when a derived collection lacks `sourceKeys`, or when `DERIVED_FROM` misses one of its sources.
- **The map is the assistant's tool contract.** Its compact form (thing → tool) is the `<where_things_live>` block of the cached system prompt, and each tool's description names the things it reads, so the model knows where to look.

**The tight leash.** The owner: "instruct agents on where to look and how … keep a very tight leash on how information is organized, how it's accessed, what's our access, and then how the agent knows about it. And it can not only read from it, but also … make changes to it." The table above says where each thing lives; this one says who may read it, how the assistant reads and changes it, what grant it needs and what deleting it takes. Both are generated from the same rows.

Class: **sealed** (words sealed; the server opens them only with the key), **readable** (fields the server reads without the key), **private-capable** (carries `privacy`, section 28 "Private is a class"). Readers: **device**; **server** jobs without the key (the reminder planner and send hook); the model roles **turn** (`interpret`), **quick** and **night** (the `reflect` and `prepareResearch` batch jobs).

| Thing | Class | Read by | To answer about it: look in | To change it: command → writer | Grant | Deleting it takes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Habit | sealed words, readable schedule; private-capable (care always private) | device, server, turn, quick, night | `<habits>`, `search_habits` | `createHabit`, `updateHabit`, `setImportance`, `setPrivate` → `habitWrites` | none | its pending reminders (cancelled), observations resting only on it, unanswered patterns; a formed habit's moment stays |
| Check-in and its note | readable ticks, sealed note; private with its habit | device, server (ticks), turn, quick, night (notes) | `<habits>`, `search_habits` | `checkIn` → `habitDayWrites` | none | observations citing the note |
| What was said | sealed; private with its subject | device, turn, quick, night | `<said>`, `search_said` | none: derived, written by the executor in the turn and by the night | none | pattern evidence rebuilt, unseen reflections citing it |
| Pattern and the person's answer | sealed lines, readable state | device, turn, quick, night | `<patterns>`, `search_patterns` | `setPatternState` → `patternWrites` | none | nothing; the answer is kept |
| Nightly candidates | sealed | device | none | none: written by the night | none | nothing |
| How Nomos sees you | sealed | device, turn, quick, night | `<how_you_seem>` | a correction said in a turn → `seenAsActions` | none | nothing; a removal is kept |
| Personality | readable setting | device, turn, quick | `<personality>` | none: the sheet → `profileActions.setPersonality` | none | the preview |
| Goal, its why and milestones | sealed; private-capable, inherited by its tasks and habits | device, turn, quick, night (names, why summary) | `<projects>`, `search_projects` | `createProject`, `updateProject`, `addMilestone`, `setImportance`, `setPrivate` → `projectWrites`, `goalWhyWrites` | none | its guardrail patterns and research items; its tasks and habits stay, unfiled |
| Task, repeating task | sealed; private-capable | device, turn, quick | `<open_tasks>`, `search_tasks` | `createTask`, `updateTask`, `completeTask`, `planIt`, `link`, `setPrivate` → `taskWrites` | none | its plan slots, its reminders when tasks push |
| Who and place | sealed; private-capable, inherited by what is about them | device, turn, quick | `search_who`, `<whereabouts>` | `upsertWho`, `upsertPlace`, `setKeyDate`, `removeKeyDate`, `setCurrentPlace`, `setPrivate` → `whoWrites`, `placeWrites`, `keyDateWrites` | Contacts, only to import | its key dates and their index entries, facts about it |
| Fact | sealed; private-capable | device, turn, quick | context facts, `search_memory` | `remember`, `setPrivate` → `factWrites` | none | nothing |
| Note and topic | sealed; private-capable | device, turn, quick (`topicWrite` on open) | `search_knowledge`, `get_doc` | `createNote`, `addToTopic`, `reviseTopic`, `moveTopic`, `createParentTopic`, `setPrivate` → `noteWrites`, `topicWrite` | none | a topic's versions; a deleted note leaves its topic `behind` for the next write |
| Procedure and its runs | sealed; private-capable | device, turn, quick | `search_procedures`, `get_doc` | `createProcedure`, `reviseProcedure`, `startRun`, `setPrivate` → `procedureWrites` | none | its versions and runs |
| Scheduled action | sealed instruction, readable cadence; private-capable | device, its runner, turn | `list_scheduled_actions` | `scheduleAction`, `setPrivate` → `scheduledActionWrites` | what the action names | its queue entry; its runs stay in Activity |
| Research | sealed; private-capable, inherits its project | device, turn, quick, night | `<research>`, `search_research`; the person finds it in Knowledge › Learn later and Search | `saveForLater`, `offerResearch`, `setPrivate` → `researchWrites` | none | nothing; a kept topic stays and its `resultTopicId` link is cleared |
| Importance | sealed | device, turn, quick | `<importance>` | `setImportance` → `applyImportance` | none | nothing |
| Turns (captures) | sealed | device, turn (the session's last 6) | the session context | none: the device writes them | none | their observations, answer quotes, media |
| What the assistant did | sealed | device, turn (the session) | Activity | none: the executor, Undo | none | never deleted by the person (rule) |
| Habit reminders | readable, fixed words | server | `<habits>` (reminder state) | `updateHabit` `remindOn`/`remindOff` → `habitWrites` stamp → `planHelmReminders` | none | cancelled with their habit |
| Calendar events | synced cache, sealed | device, turn, quick | `get_calendar` | none (Issue 67: approved writes) | Calendar | the copies, with the grant |
| Another app's synced category | synced cache, sealed | device, turn, quick | `search_synced` | none | that category | the copies, with the grant |
| Another app's live category (Going Once's things, Runway's transactions) | live, never stored | turn, quick once its tool joins | `search_app_records` | none | that category | nothing kept |
| Mail and Drive | live; only an answer's quotes kept | turn, quick once their tools join | `search_mail`, `read_mail`, `search_documents`, `read_document` | none | Gmail, Drive | the quotes, with their capture |

The night reads nothing from turns, Knowledge, facts, who records, places, media, synced copies or live categories (the approved line, section 14); private things are read like the rest (owner, review 4).

**`WHERE_THINGS_LIVE` is the one code source.** `src/apps/helm/whereThingsLive.ts`, with a server copy under `check:server-copies`, holds one row per thing:

```ts
interface WhereRow {
  thing: string;                          // the words the prompt uses
  home: HelmCollection | 'synced' | 'live' | 'reminders';
  classes: ('sealed' | 'readable' | 'privateCapable')[];
  readers: ('device' | 'server' | 'turn' | 'quick' | 'night')[];
  contextBlock: string | null;            // '<habits>'
  tools: HelmToolName[];                  // the read path
  commands: NomosCommandKind[];           // the model's write path
  writer: string;                         // the write builder every command and tap goes through
  jobFields: { field: string; job: string }[]; // fields a server job owns (stage, reminded)
  grant: { source: string; category: string } | null;
  batchJobs: BatchJobId[];                // night jobs allowed to read it
  derivedFrom: string[];                  // homes it cites (sourceKeys)
  deleteTakes: string[];                  // what cascadeFor deletes or rebuilds
  isSynced: boolean;                      // a learned result that must sync ("Nothing learned lives on one device")
}
```

From these rows, and nowhere else, come:
- **The prompt's `<where_things_live>` block**, one line per row the person can reach, in a fixed form: "To answer about habits, look in `<habits>`, or call `search_habits`. To change a habit, use `createHabit`, `updateHabit` or `setPrivate`." A row whose grant isn't live is left out.
- **The tool list:** a tool is offered only when a row names it and its grant is live (today's filter, now driven by the rows).
- **The command → writer table** the executor dispatches through (`COMMAND_WRITERS`), so a sentence and a tap make the same change.
- **Batch eligibility:** a job's input builder declares the homes it reads; each must list that job in `batchJobs`.
- **Delete cascades:** `DERIVED_FROM` and `cascadeFor` are built from `derivedFrom` and `deleteTakes`.

**`check:helm-where` fails** when a tool in `mcp/buildTools.ts`, a command kind in `commands.ts`, a collection in `types.ts` or the rules' wildcard, a `BATCH_JOBS` entry Helm submits, a context block, or a category Helm's sink or live router accepts has no row; when a row names a tool, command, writer or job that doesn't exist; when a job reads a home whose row doesn't list it; when a derived home lacks `sourceKeys`; and when a learned result's row isn't `isSynced`.

**The growth rule.** A new record type, tool, command, context block, connected category or batch job isn't done until its row exists, and the row is reviewed with the PR: the technical reviewer reads the diff of `whereThingsLive.ts` as part of the review (`docs/conventions/agents.md` gains the line in the docs PR).

**As built (Issue 102).** The MVP's things, one row each: every collection in the rules, every tool in `mcp/buildTools.ts`, every command kind and `closeConversation`, every block `buildContext.ts` writes (the turn's own blocks are `TURN_BLOCKS`) and every category of a connector manifest. The row is the shape above with these differences: `home` is a `HelmHome` (the collections, plus `profile`, `live` and `connections`), `writer` is a module (`actions/<file>` on the device, `server/<file>`, `platform/<file>`), `taps` lists the device actions that call it so the check proves a sentence and a tap share a writer, `sourceKeys` is the field that holds a derived record's keys, and `grant` is one `connector/category`. **Generated:** the prompt's `<where_things_live>` block (`renderWhereBlock`, in the system prompt after `SYSTEM_PROMPT`; a connected row is listed only when `isRowGranted` holds: a synced row when the turn holds copies from its source, which stay searchable after a connection ends, and a live read (Mail, Drive, an app's live category) when its source or `source/category` pair is granted that turn), `COMMAND_WRITERS` and `DERIVED_FROM`. **Checked, not generated:** the tool list, the executor's command lists and the deletes, which `check:helm-where` proves equal the rows (rewriting them from the rows is left to the issue that adds the first derived record and `cascadeFor`). A snapshot of the block, with every source and with none, is `scripts/checks/fixtures/helmWhere.snapshot.txt` (`npm run check:helm-where -- --write` rewrites it). No rules change.

**Read path and write path, said once.** To answer about X, the model looks in X's context block, then calls X's tool; it never guesses a home. To change X, it emits one of X's commands; code resolves the aliases, `normalizeNomosReply` checks the shape, the policy decides act or note, and X's writer makes the change in the turn's transaction with Undo. The model can't write a home that has no command (derived records, answers to patterns, settings changed only on a sheet), and it can't read a home that has no tool or block.


### The use cases: what the data needs


**The road trip** ("I'm considering hitting the road this weekend. What would I need to get done before leaving Sunday?").
1. A question, so the full route. The context already holds open tasks, projects with dates (the wedding, the move) and today's and tomorrow's events; the model calls `get_calendar` for Friday to Monday, `search_tasks` for what is due by Sunday, and, with Going Once's `things` category on, `search_app_records`.
2. **The warning is code's, not a guess.** The server runs the vendored `readFreeMinutes` and `readDeadlineFit` (the guardrails' arithmetic, which the planner shares) over the open work due by Sunday against the free time before it, and passes one `<fit>` line ("About 7 h of tasks due by Sunday, about 4 h free before then; the seating chart is due Saturday"). The model words the answer around it; work that can't fit is said plainly ("I'd hold off").
3. **Going Once.** "Your sale still has 3 drafts and 2 things with no price yet. Is this the final list?": from the live `things` category (title and state only). Helm can't see things never entered, which is why it asks.
4. **The meeting with the couple** (Priya Park and Sam Whitaker, whose wedding O officiates Sat Dec 12 at Cascade Orchard, Leavenworth; the call is Monday Nov 16, on video; O's own wedding with Maya at Ridgeline Barn is a separate project). Owner review 2: the context marks an event `nearby` when `readWhereabouts` puts the person within 50 km of the event's city that day (below); a calendar event's place text is matched to a city by name through `createOptionMatcher` against the cities Helm knows (homes, away, trips, places), with no geocoding service. The model asks a pick-one ("Keep it on video" · "Meet in person, you'll be nearby" · "Move it"). Until Issue 67 can change the calendar, a pick makes a task ("Tell Priya and Sam we'll meet in person") and the reply says the calendar is unchanged.
5. **The status of the questions for them.** Kept as a task with steps ("Questions for Priya and Sam", each step a question, linked to the meeting's event): a step ticked is asked; Issue 68's preparation before an event asks the day before "Do these feel ready?" with the open ones listed. Questions sitting in a Drive document are read live, and Helm offers once to make them a task with steps, which is then their home (the document stays the person's).
6. Keeping the what-if itself is Issue 73's scenario; this answer doesn't need it.

**Learn later** ("What's the difference between AWD and 4WD?").
- Answered now, as any question; or "save it for later": `saveForLater` writes a `learnLater` research record (`preparing`) and sets `reflectDueAt`.
- **Saved with no question first (lead).** A wish with no question ("one day I want to learn how engines work") is `saveForLater` at once, with no answer and no ask; the reply says it's saved and where. **Same subject merges**: before writing, code compares the new subject with open `learnLater` items (`createOptionMatcher` over their sealed `subject` labels, on the device's copy in the context); a match adds the words to that item's sealed `moreAsked` (≤5) instead of a new record, else one record each. **Learning about vs practising**: learning about a subject is `saveForLater`; a skill to practise ("learn piano") is a goal offer; when the model can't tell, it asks one code-built pickOne ("Read up on it later" · "Make it a goal you practise" · Something else) and writes neither. **Code check**: a turn proposing `saveForLater` and a goal `createProject` (`kind: 'goal'`) on the same words (overlapping spans) has both refused by the normalizer and the pickOne asked instead. **Where it's found**: Knowledge › Learn later (the eager `research` slice, `mode == 'learnLater'`), Search (its subject and question, on the device), and by asking (`<research>`, `search_research`). Decider: code for the merge and the refusal, the model for which of the two it is. Check: `check:helm-sort` fixtures for a wish saved with no question, two wishes on one subject merging, two subjects making two items, "learn piano" offered as a goal, an unclear wish asking the pickOne, and `saveForLater` with a goal create on the same words refused.
- **A Not now can mean three things (lead).** A tapped Not now on a learn-later offer is `timing`. A Not now in words goes to the model, which sets `declineLearn { subjectAlias, reason }` with `reason` from the closed `LEARN_DECLINE_REASONS` (`timing` · `lostInterest` · `changedMind`; an unclear one is `timing`); code applies the effect, through `researchWrites`, with Undo. **timing**: the item goes back on the list (`ready`), and the offer's outcome (Show me or Not now, with the kind of evening `findLearnSlot` saw: weekday or weekend, free gap length, after a busy day or not) is recorded on a `learnEvening` pattern that `findLearnSlot` reads to prefer the evenings the person says yes on. **lostInterest**: the item is removed (Undo restores it) and an `offerDeclined` pattern is written on the subject's signature (`learnLater:` + the normalised subject, the same signature mechanism as every pattern) with `offReason: 'lostInterest'`, so it is never offered again and never named as related; it reopens only when the person raises the subject themselves (a `saveForLater` or question on a matching subject clears `offReason`). **changedMind**: the item's question is edited from the person's words (`moreAsked` replaced, the item kept). **Three in a row**: `notNowStreak` (on the item, reset by Show me) reaching 3 asks one code-built pickOne instead of offering it again: "Still want to learn how engines work?" · Keep it · Remove it (Remove is `lostInterest`). Decider: code for a tap, the streak and every effect; the model only picks the reason from words. Check: `check:helm-sort` fixtures for each reason in words and an unclear one read as timing; `check:helm-research` for each effect, the third Not now asking, Remove writing the back-off, a lostInterest subject never offered or related until raised, and `findLearnSlot` preferring the yes evenings.
- **Related things meet (lead).** Saved subjects are in `<research>` (aliases, subjects). The model may add `relatedLearn { aliasA, aliasB }` ("That's close to the engines thing you saved. Learn them together?"). Code keeps it only when: it is the turn's only one; neither subject carries a `lostInterest` back-off; the pair's signature (`relatedLearn:` + the two ids, sorted) has no line in the last 4 weeks (written as a pattern with `nextEligibleOn` 4 weeks out); and not when the turn is spoken (`isSpokenTurn`) and the person is mid-task (a procedure run in progress or a question open in the session). **Together** merges the two into one item (the newer folded into the older's `moreAsked`, its record deleted) with Undo; Not now writes the pair's 4-week line. Decider: the model proposes, code gates and merges. Check: `check:helm-sort` fixtures for a related line kept, a second one in a turn dropped, a pair within 4 weeks dropped, a lostInterest subject dropped, a spoken mid-task turn dropped, and Together merging with Undo.
- That night the pass adds a `prepareResearch` request: an overview (a summary, up to 5 sections, a comparison table when the question compares) and a video search query (≤100 characters). The collector seals it, calls `searchVideos` (embeddable, 3 to 20 minutes, strict safe search, the top 2 by YouTube's relevance), and sets `ready`.
- **Back at a good time.** `findLearnSlot` (pure, `engine/research.ts`) on the device: a day outside a busy stretch, with a gap of 30 minutes or more in the last third of Your day. The item then competes for Today's one offer, after care asks, guardrails and habit-life offers (product orders it): "Remember that thing you wanted to learn? AWD vs 4WD". Not now is a no under the back-off (an `offerDeclined` pattern on the record).
- **Opened**: `ResearchOverview`, labelled "Not from your records", with up to 2 videos through the shared `VideoEmbed` (Pinch's `HowToVideo` moves to `src/components` in that issue: tap to load, nothing plays on its own). Keep it files it as a note or topic (`resultTopicId`); Done closes it. Videos over 30 days old are searched again when it is opened (one call) or dropped, as YouTube's terms require.
- **The video source** is the YouTube Data API v3 (section 16): free, 100 searches a day per project by default and shared by the site, so one search per record; without its key the overview shows alone.

**Research depth and live web search** (owner: yes, with a per-person monthly cap).
- **The source**: Anthropic's web search server tool (`web_search_20260209`, with its dynamic filtering) on the `prepareResearch` request, through `functions/src/ai/` (no app words). Only the question's words, the up-front answers and the project's name go out as the search; never records. Price, checked on platform.claude.com ("Web search tool", read 2026-11-13): $10 per 1,000 searches, plus the result text counted as input tokens; a failed search isn't billed. Server tools run inside a Message Batch (platform.claude.com "Batch processing", read 2026-11-14), so an item with search is part of the nightly batch at batch token prices; a search can end the result with `pause_turn`, continued in the next run at most 2 times, then given up. The costs below are at-once prices: they stay the reservation, since research asked for "now" runs at once, and a batched item settles at its real, lower cost (about 7¢ Light, 22¢ Thorough; searches aren't discounted). The time budgets bind at-once calls; in a batch the bounds are `max_uses`, `max_tokens` and the continuation cap.
- **Depth** maps to a budget, in `RESEARCH_DEPTHS` (`constants.ts`):

| Depth | Searches (`max_uses`) | Model | Time budget | Output | Cost, about |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Light (default) | 3 | Sonnet-class (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) | 2 min | summary and up to 3 sections, sources | 11¢ (3¢ searches, about 30,000 in and 2,000 out) |
| Thorough | 10 | Sonnet-class | 8 min | summary, up to 5 sections, a comparison table, sources | 35¢ (10¢ searches, about 100,000 in and 4,000 out) |

- **The ask.** Each research request carries its depth. With `researchDepth` null, Helm asks once per request, a pickOne answerable by voice ("Quick look or thorough? A thorough one reads more widely and uses more of this month's research": depth never promises a time), with "Always quick" and "Always thorough" setting the default; a skipped ask is Light. A turn can say it ("look into it properly").
- **The cap**: `RESEARCH_MONTHLY_CAP_CENTS = 200` per person (about 18 light or 5 thorough items a month, at most $2 a person; at 1,000 people that is up to $2,000 a month if everyone used all of it, so the project's daily AI budget also applies).
