Kept in `apps/helm/server/{uid}/research/usage` (`month`, `cents`, `searches`; Admin only). A request is reserved at its depth's estimate before it is sent and settled at the real cost; **past the cap** (decider: code, `readResearchAllowance(usage, depth, today)` in `engine/research.ts`, server copy): Thorough is locked, shown in the ask with its reset date ("Thorough looks are back on Dec 1"); a Quick look still answers at once from general knowledge with no `web_search` tool, its overview labelled "Not from the web" (the "Not from your records" label stays beside it); its reply offers "Look on Dec 1", and only a yes writes the research record's `rerunOn` (date-only, the 1st of next month), which the hourly submit picks up that day as an ordinary live Quick item, reserved then like any other. Nothing re-runs without that yes. A thorough request whose estimate would pass the cap is offered as Quick. A time budget exceeded ends the call with what it has.
- **Learn later** uses search only when the question needs current facts (the model's `needsLiveFacts` on `saveForLater`); "AWD vs 4WD" doesn't, and costs about a cent as before.

**Research ahead of a dated project** ("we move in March").
- When a project gets a date, the model may add `offerResearch` ("apartments near the new job", 60 days ahead; at most 3 per project), or the person asks for it. `computeOfferOn` sets `offerOn` = the date minus `leadDays` (date-only arithmetic in code), rewritten when the date moves.
- On or after `offerOn`, the daily check offers it in Today's one offer slot ("Your move is 8 weeks out. Want me to look into apartments near Oakland?"), under the back-off.
- Yes opens a turn that asks the questions up front as model-written `ask` questions (budget, areas, must-haves; at most 5), status `asking`; the answers are turns, cited in `sourceKeys` and kept in `answers`. Then `preparing`: the nightly pass prepares it ("I'll have it for you in the morning"), or "now" runs it as a full turn. The result is a topic under the project (Issue 65's drafted topic, sections citing sources).
- **Limit.** The model knows areas in general, not today's listings. Real listings need a live web source: the owner said yes, with a monthly cap per person ("Research depth and live web search", below); a lead-time item asks its depth with its up-front questions. No apartments app is assumed: it is a research topic in Helm; an apartments app built later would report through connected apps.

**The gaps, and where each lands.**

| Gap | What it needs | Lands in |
|---|---|---|
| Going Once's full state | The audit below: every thing (drafts and not ready included) with its price, and buyers' questions; off at connect. Issue 22 is built, so the widening is its own issue | Issue 124 |
| A meeting's format and place, and "you'll be nearby" | The Calendar adapter adds `placeLabel` and `format` (from conference data and known meeting hosts; never a link, description or attendee); `transformVersion` bumped, so the next sync rewrites copies; the context sends both for events in the question's range (yes); "nearby" by `readWhereabouts` and distance between cities | Issue 123 (with Helm's own appointments' `meets`); "nearby" in Issue 107 |
| The status of questions for someone | A task with steps linked to the event; the "ready?" question before it | Issue 121, on Issue 68 |
| "Can I go?" without a guess | `readFreeMinutes` and `readDeadlineFit` vendored for the server, a `<fit>` block for date-range questions | Issue 122 (days away), the first issue that answers one |
| A learn-later queue | `research` (`learnLater`), the nightly preparer, `findLearnSlot`, the overview, shared `searchVideos` and `VideoEmbed` | Issue 119 |
| Research ahead of a dated project | `research` (`leadTime`), `computeOfferOn`, up-front questions, the result topic | Issue 120, after 65 and 74 |
| Changing the meeting itself | Calendar writes, approved one by one | Issue 67 |
| Helm's plan kept in step with a calendar the person already looks at | Two-way sync with one chosen Google Calendar (below) | Issue 127 |
| Every other app's read gaps | The full-state audit below | Issue 124 |

### Scheduled actions


Something the assistant does for the person at a set time (lead: "every Sunday, put next week's groceries on the calendar"). It is not Issue 70: a repeating task or a scheduled procedure is the person's own job coming round again, and nothing acts; a scheduled action is the assistant doing work while the person is away.

- **Made** by `scheduleAction` in a turn ("every Sunday at 6, make my shopping task from Pinch's list") or on its sheet: a repeat (a calendar rule, "Repeats like a calendar" below; was a cadence), a floating time, and either held commands (`kind: 'commands'`: run by the executor with no model, like a code-built question's) or the person's instruction (`kind: 'prompt'`: a model turn, for work that needs judgment, such as a Friday briefing once Issue 65 drafts topics). `allowed` lists the command kinds a run may write, checked at create and at every run; `nextRunAt` is computed by code through `zonedDateTimeToEpoch`. Any change stamps `scheduledActionsChangedAt`.
- **Run** by `runHelmScheduledActions` (every 5 minutes): the reminder planner's shape (an Admin queue `actionQueue/{uid}` with one indexed `lookAt`, plus the recent-change query, a lease per person). Each due action writes a capture with the deterministic id `sa_{actionId}_{dueAt}` (`arrivedVia: 'scheduled'`, `scheduledActionId`), so a retried run makes one, then goes through the same normalizer, policy and one transaction as any turn, narrowed to `allowed` and with no live mail or document tool unless the action names one. The result is an Execution with Undo, an Activity line ("Done for you · Sun 6:00 PM") and, on the next open, a line on Today. Nothing is pushed; the assistant still never speaks first. A run missed while the function was down runs once if it is less than 6 hours late, else it is `skipped` and says so.
- **On Issues 67 and 93.** Until Issue 67, only Helm-internal kinds are allowed (make or update tasks, plan a day, refresh a research topic, start a procedure run). With 67, an outbound kind (put an event on the calendar) is allowed, and each run's outbound part waits in 67's approvals queue as a code-built `approveAction` question in Needs you ("Ready: next week's groceries on your calendar · Add it"); `needsApproval` stays true. When Issue 93 grants acting without asking for that kind, the run acts and tells, with Undo.
- **Cost.** A `commands` action costs no model call; a `prompt` action is a full turn (1 to 2 cents), counted on the person's limits and the project's budget. Firestore: a few reads a run.
- **Privacy.** A run unwraps the key with no one present, like the nightly pass, but only because the person asked for that action; "What the AI sees" lists each `prompt` action under "On a schedule".
- **Seen, changed and failed.** **The list**: Browse › Scheduled (`ScheduledActionsList`, from the eager `scheduledActions` slice), one row each with its next run. **Cap**: 50 per person (`HELM_CAPS.scheduledActions`, the listener's cap); `scheduleAction` past it is refused with a code-built line naming the cap and the list. **Its sheet** (`ScheduledActionSheet`): Pause, Change (cadence, time, what it does) and End, through `scheduledActionWrites`, each stamping `scheduledActionsChangedAt`. **Each run leaves one outcome line**, on Today on the next open (`selectScheduledOutcomes`: runs since the last open, from `lastOutcome` and its execution) and in Activity. **A run that can't go** is decided by code before any model call (`readRunBlocker`: a connection the action needs is missing or expired, an `allowed` kind no longer granted): `lastOutcome: 'failed'` with `lastFailReason: 'notConnected' | 'grantExpired' | 'notAllowed' | 'runFailed' | null`, and a code-built line: "Sunday's grocery list didn't run: Pinch isn't connected. Reconnect · Skip this week". Reconnect opens the connection and then offers Run it now, which runs once only while it is still before the action's next scheduled run; Skip this week clears it until that next run, whatever the cadence (daily, weekly, monthly). A failed run is never retried on its own. **Over the person's limits** (the turn limit or the project's daily budget), the run waits (`lastOutcome: 'waiting'`) and runs on the next open, when the device's open stamps `scheduledActionsChangedAt` and the queue picks it up within 5 minutes (the person is there, so the 6-hour rule doesn't apply to it). A run missed because the function was down keeps the existing rule: under 6 hours late it runs, later it is `skipped` and its outcome line says so. **Never pushed**: a scheduled action never sends a push on its own; its outcome shows on the next open. Decider: code. Check: `check:helm-scheduled` gains each blocker's line, Skip this week, a waiting run run on the next open, and no push sent for any outcome.

#### Repeats like a calendar


Owner: "similar to how Google Calendar does it… I do wonder how the schedules work under the hood. Maybe use a cron job." Lead decision A, as built under the hood:

- **The rule is a calendar rule.** `repeat` holds an RFC 5545 RRULE body, the standard Google Calendar stores, beside `startsOn` (its DTSTART, a day key), the floating `time`, `timeZone`, `skipped` (EXDATE) and `moved` (one-off overrides). The rule is **date-only**: it produces days, and each day becomes an instant only through `zonedDateTimeToEpoch(day, time, zone)` (`src/utils/timezoneUtils.ts`, server copy `functions/src/notifications/zonedTime.ts`), so 8:00 AM stays 8:00 AM on both sides of a clock change and no rule ever carries a time or a TZID.
- **The closed subset** `parseRepeatRule` accepts (anything else is `null` and refused): `FREQ` DAILY, WEEKLY, MONTHLY or YEARLY; `INTERVAL` 1–365 for days, 1–52 for weeks, 1–12 for months, 1–10 for years (design's Custom limits); `WKST` set from the locale's first day of the week (`Intl.Locale` week info), Sunday when the browser doesn't say, which decides how "every 2 weeks on Sunday and Monday" pairs its days; `BYDAY` for weekly (one to seven days; "Every weekday" is `FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO,TU,WE,TH,FR`, as Google writes it); for monthly either `BYMONTHDAY=1..31` ("on the 23rd"), `BYMONTHDAY=-1` ("on the last day") or `BYDAY` with one ordinal weekday, `1MO`–`4MO` or `-1MO` ("the fourth Monday", "the last Monday"); yearly on `startsOn`'s month and day; and at most one of `UNTIL=YYYYMMDD` (a date, since DTSTART is a date) or `COUNT=1..999`. No HOURLY, MINUTELY, SECONDLY, BYHOUR, BYSETPOS, BYWEEKNO or several ordinals. RFC behaviour is kept where Google's matches it: "the 31st" skips months without one (`describeRepeat` adds "(months without one are skipped)"), Feb 29 yearly comes only in leap years, and COUNT counts occurrences, so a skipped run still counts toward "After 10 times".
- **Library or our own: our own, checked against `rrule`.** There is no `cadenceUtils` in `src/` today: it is this plan's own unbuilt habit helper, and its shape can't say "on the 1st", "the last Friday", a yearly date, every N weeks or an end, so it is not extended (habits keep it). `rrule` (npm 2.8.1, BSD-3-Clause, one dependency, `tslib`) is the reference implementation, but its last release was Nov 2023, its time-zone path (TZID with its "UTC-pretend" `Date`s) has long-standing clock-change issues, and we would still need our own subset check, describer and presets around it; `rrule-temporal` (MIT, active) needs a Temporal polyfill in every browser that lacks Temporal. So `src/utils/repeatRule.ts` is a small pure iterator over whole UTC days for the subset above (about 250 lines with the describer), and `rrule` comes in only as a **devDependency oracle**: `check:repeat-rule` generates a few thousand rules in the subset (every frequency, interval, weekday set, ordinal including -1, day 29–31 and -1, both ends, leap years) and compares our first 50 occurrences with `rrule`'s in its date-only UTC mode, where it is reliable. Nothing from `rrule` ships to browsers or to Functions. BSD-3 needs no attribution for a dev-only tool.
- **The helpers** (`src/utils/repeatRule.ts`, no app words, server copy): `parseRepeatRule(text)` and `formatRepeatRule(rule)`; `repeatFromParts(parts, startsOn)` builds a rule from the model's structured fields or the picker; `checkRepeatRule(rule, startsOn, today)` returns the first broken cap or null; `listOccurrences(rule, startsOn, { fromDay, limit, skipped, moved })` and `nextOccurrence(...)`; `repeatPresets(startsOn)` gives Google's presets worded from the first date (Every day, Every weekday, Every week on Monday, Every month on the 23rd / the fourth Monday / the last Monday / the last day, Every year on Nov 23); `describeRepeat(rule, startsOn, { time, timeFormat })` gives the one words line ("Every 2 weeks on Tuesday and Thursday at 8:00 AM, until Mar 1"), used by the picker, the sheet, the list row and the turn's reply alike (one function, so a reply never disagrees with its sheet). The shared `RepeatPicker` (design names and draws it) sits on these; its Custom screen is an in-place view of the same sheet, so `FormSheet` gains a `viewKey` view swap like `DetailSheet`'s (a `BackLink` plus `useBackStep`, so the phone's Back returns to the presets), a shared component change made in Issue 106 (the repeat rule) with every `FormSheet` consumer checked. The picker is what Issue 70 and later apps (Waypoint, Runway, Nine Lives reminders) use.
- **Caps** (`checkRepeatRule`, at create, at every change and at every run): never more often than daily (the subset has nothing finer, and a move can't put two runs on one day); `INTERVAL` within its frequency's range (1–365 days, 1–52 weeks, 1–12 months, 1–10 years); `COUNT` ≤999; `UNTIL` on or after `startsOn`; at least one occurrence after today ("That never comes round" otherwise); at most 50 actions counted as `status != 'ended'` (a finished action leaves the count); `skipped` ≤100 and `moved` ≤20 future entries. `firestore.rules` checks no fields in Helm's beta tier, so the caps are code's on the device and on the server: a run whose stored rule fails them is not run (`lastFailReason: 'ruleInvalid'`, its line says to change it).
- **Said, not typed.** "Every other Tuesday at 8", "the last Friday of every month", "weekdays at 7 until the end of March": the model fills `scheduleAction.repeat` (frequency, interval, weekdays, which monthly kind, ordinal, end) and attaches the time and dates by span id (dates still come only from code); `repeatFromParts` builds the rule, `checkRepeatRule` validates it, and a rule that fails becomes a code-built question naming what couldn't be set, never a guess. "Every 2 hours" or "at 8 and at 6" is not a rule the code will take: the model answers with an `ask` (yesNo) offering separate daily actions, each made only on the yes, and a sub-daily rule that reaches code is refused anyway (`check:helm-scheduled`).
- **When it runs: no cron per action.** `runHelmScheduledActions` (this plan's every-5-minute function, beside the reminder planner; not yet built) reads `actionQueue` where `lookAt <= now` (single-field index; `lookAt` is the earliest `nextRunAt` among the person's actions, kept by every write that changes one), then that person's `scheduledActions` where `nextRunAt <= now` (single-field index, no composite needed, since paused-with-no-end and ended actions hold `nextRunAt: null`). A run is therefore 0–5 minutes after its time; its outcome line shows `lastRunDueAt`, the set time, never when the function got to it (except a waiting run, which is dated when it ran). The server never trusts a stored `nextRunAt`: on pickup it recomputes the due occurrence from the rule, and if that isn't due it rewrites `nextRunAt` and moves on.
- **One run per occurrence.** The run's capture id becomes `sa_{actionId}_{occurrenceKey}`, the occurrence's **original** day key (it was `dueAt`), so a moved run, a retry and a changed time all map to one capture; since a rule is at most daily, a day key is unique per action. After the run it writes `lastRunDueAt`, prunes past `skipped`/`moved` entries and sets the next `nextRunAt`/`nextRunKey`; the run that was the rule's last sets `status: 'ended'`, `endedBy: 'finished'` and one Activity line ("Finished after its 10th run: …"), and the list drops it. Missed runs keep the 6-hour rule; when several occurrences were missed (a daily action over a 2-day outage), only the newest under 6 hours runs and the rest are one `skipped` line.
- **Skip this one** adds the occurrence's day key to `skipped` (Undo removes it); it replaces the earlier "Skip this week" on the failure line, which now skips exactly the next run whatever the rule. **Move this one** writes `moved[key] = { day, time }`: the new day must be today or later, not a day that already ran, and no later than the day before the run after it (product), so the order of runs never changes and a day never holds two; Undo deletes the entry. **Just this one** (Change opened from a run) is a move: it changes that run's day and time only. **This and following** (the default from a run, and the only scope from the action's sheet): from the next run, the record is rewritten in place (`startsOn` = that run's day or the new first day, the new rule, `COUNT` reduced by the occurrences already past, skips and moves kept only where they still fall on a run of the new rule, the rest dropped, and the change's confirm line counts them); from a later run, the action splits like a calendar series: the old record gains `UNTIL` = the day before, and a new one starts there (`splitFromId`), both in one batch, the new one counting toward the 50. Past runs are never rewritten: their captures, executions and Activity lines keep their own text. A changed time on a day that already ran doesn't run again that day (its capture id exists).
- **Clocks and away zones.** A floating time in the action's zone: the profile's home zone when `timeZone` is null (moving home moves them, and the Settings write recomputes each `nextRunAt` and stamps `scheduledActionsChangedAt`), or the zone the person named. A time that doesn't exist on a spring-forward night runs the same distance past the gap (02:30 → 03:30), and one that happens twice on a fall-back night runs at its first occurrence, once (the day key). "Use Lisbon time" (`zoneSpans`) never moves an action (the lead's call stands); only "Pause what runs at home" does, and it pauses **by span** (design): the yes writes a readable `homeRunsPaused: { from, to }[]` on the profile (date-only, inclusive, ≤10, cleared with the away span), and `nextOccurrence` skips every day inside a span for every action, so one made during the time away, or whose runs fall inside it, is paused too without being named; the per-action `pausedFrom`/`pausedUntil` stays for a pause the person sets on one action. When the device's zone differs from the action's, the list and sheet show the zone's abbreviation after the time (`formatTimezoneAbbreviation`).
- **Cost.** Iterating a rule is microseconds; a run reads 2 documents more than before (the queue doc and the person's due actions). Nothing new is billed.

### Nothing learned lives on one device


Owner review 2: a new phone loses nothing. Each learned result or setting, where it lived and where it lives now:

| What | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| A pattern found but waiting under pacing | recomputed each day, never stored | `patterns/{id}`, state `candidate`, sealed lines |
| `saidStruggling`, `saidWorking`, tick-only patterns | found on the device, stored once shown | found on the device, stored as soon as found (as above) |
| A habit's stage, pace, pause | the habit (synced) | unchanged |
| Observations, reflections, profile lines, why summaries | synced | unchanged; `seenAs` is new and synced |
| The style notes and the personality preview | `style/*` (synced) | unchanged; preview added |
| The natural voice switch | the browser | `prefersNaturalVoice` on the profile; each device downloads the voice (a device file) when true |
| Hands-free, wake word, voice mode, tone, theme, personality | the profile | unchanged (the theme's localStorage copy is only a first-paint cache) |
| Phone location's last city (and point, at Precise) | n/a | `nearNow` on the self who (sealed), lapsing after 6 h |
| What stays on a device by its nature | | Trust this browser's key, a downloaded voice or recognition model, the outbox, mute for the session, form drafts |

`check:helm-where` gains a list of the slice's learned fields and fails when one has no Firestore home.

### "How Nomos sees you", the profile behind it


The page (section 26) now shows, in this order: the `seenAs` lines grouped by kind ("What works for you", "What gets in the way", "Your rhythm", "How you talk"), each with its `basis` line and a tap that opens its sources (an observation's date and quote, a pattern's statement and evidence, a style note); the facts (unchanged home: `facts`); the style notes (unchanged home: `style`); the personality (below). Every line has Correct and Remove; facts and style notes keep their own Correct and Forget. Lines come from the nightly pass (schema in section 2); with learning off, no line is written and the page says so, and "Forget what you've noticed" deletes the `seenAs` lines with the observations.

### Personality


- **Picked** on the personality sheet: up to 5 traits from `PERSONALITY_TRAITS` and an honesty level (Tactful, Straight, Unvarnished), or said ("be more blunt with me": `setPersonality` command, a request, so it acts).
- **Clashes** by code: `findTraitClashes(traits)` (pure) over `TRAIT_CLASHES` in `constants.ts`: blunt–gentle, brief–detailed, playful–formal, casual–formal, dry–warm, and honesty Unvarnished with gentle. A clash warns on the sheet ("Blunt and gentle pull apart. Which wins when they do?") and asks for a winner (`clashWinner`); both may be kept. A turn that sets a clashing pair asks the same as a `pickOne` question, answerable by voice.
- **How replies will read**: `previewHelmPersonality` (callable, the quick model, one call, ≤3 replies of ≤240 characters to three fixed prompts in `constants.ts`: a reminder, a pushback, a piece of news) when the person stops changing the sheet for 1.5 s and `previewHash` differs; counted on the quick route's limits (about $0.0005 a call); offline or failed, each trait's fixed sample sentence shows instead.
- **To the model**: each trait's prompt line, the honesty line and the winners, as `<personality>` in the cached system prompt (it changes rarely, so it stays in the cached prefix per person). Honesty shapes pushback and guardrail wording only within the product's rules: care items stay ask-only and nothing turns unkind (Unvarnished means plain, not harsh; the prompt says so).

### Where the person is, and where they will be


- **Stated**: the self who's `home` and `away` (and each who's), from the who page (`CitySelect`) or a turn ("I'm in Lisbon Dec 2 to 9": `upsertWho` on the self with an `away` city span and two date spans, dates by code). A city named in a turn is matched to a `City` through the shared city search on the device (`@/lib/cities`) when the person confirms it in the result card; the server keeps the name until then.
- **`readWhereabouts(dayKey)`** (pure, `engine/whereabouts.ts`, server copy): the first that holds for the day: a stated `away` covering it → a Waypoint trip covering it (the `trips` category's city) → a calendar event that day marked away (all-day or over 6 hours, with a place matched to a known city) → `currentPlace` today (a place with a city) → home. Returns the city and which source said so; the context sends it as names.
- **Nearby**: two cities within 50 km by great-circle distance on their coordinates (`NEARBY_KM`), or a closeness the person stated; a place with no city is nearby only by a name match. PRODUCT now says the same.
- **Phone location (owner: Off, City only or Precise, per person, Off by default).** Off asks the browser for nothing. With either on, the device reads a position at most once an hour while Helm is open (City only asks the browser for low accuracy) and finds the nearest known city (homes, away, trips, places with cities) within 50 km. **City only** writes just that city's name and the time as `nearNow`; coordinates never leave the device. **Precise** also keeps the point, rounded to 3 decimals (about 110 m), in the same sealed `nearNow`, so "nearby" can use the distance to a known place ("you're about 2 km from the pharmacy"); it is overwritten by each reading, kept no more than 6 hours (the device clears a lapsed one, the daily check too), never kept as a history, and the model gets only code's distances to places the turn names, never the point. Turning it down to City only or Off clears the point (and the city) in the same write. Unknown surroundings read as "away from home" (more than 50 km from home), with no city named. No reverse-geocoding service. The settings line says what each keeps and for how long.
- **Offered by the assistant, never asked up front.** Each turn the device sends `abilities: { location: { setting: 'off' | 'city' | 'precise', permission: 'granted' | 'prompt' | 'denied' | 'unsupported' } }` (the Permissions API's `geolocation` state, read on the device), and the context carries it as `<abilities>`. The terminal tool's input gains `offerAbility: { ability: 'location', level: 'city' | 'precise' } | null`. Code keeps the offer only when the level is above the setting, the permission isn't `unsupported`, and no Not now for that ability and level is under 4 weeks old (`abilityOffers` on the profile, readable, dates only); `denied` turns it into the settings line. The device draws an `AbilityOfferRow` under the answer; Turn on calls `getCurrentPosition` inside the tap and writes `phoneLocation` only on success. The model can never change the setting: there is no command for it. The same field and row serve any later ability the browser gates behind a tap (reminders, the microphone), each a new `ability` value.
- **Away in another time zone.** Not a travel mode: only a stated away span or a Waypoint trip (the `trips` category's city and dates trigger the same offer when the trip syncs in). When a turn writes the self's `away` with a city whose zone (the `City`'s `timeZone` from `@/lib/cities`) has a different offset from the profile's on any of those dates, code (`readAwayZoneOffer`, pure, `engine/whereabouts.ts`) adds one offer row under the reply, drawn like `AbilityOfferRow`, offered once per span (`zoneOffered` on the away entry): **"Use Lisbon time Dec 2–9"** writes `zoneSpans: { zone, from, to }[]` (readable on the profile, date-only, inclusive, ≤10) with Undo; **Later:** "Pause what runs at home (groceries on Sunday)" names the scheduled actions whose next run falls in the span and writes each a `pausedFrom`/`pausedUntil` (date-only), resumed by code the day after; it is not built with this issue and waits for Issue 115, which adds scheduled actions. A Waypoint trip's zone offer is also Later: it waits for the sink to keep the trip's zone (today it keeps the city and dates only), so only a stated away span offers a zone. `readZoneFor(dayKey, profile)` (pure, server copy) returns the span's zone or the profile's, and these clocks read it: the reminder planner, the 03:00 rollover, the Today planner, and `reflectDueAt` (scheduled actions do not: they keep home time and follow only their `pausedFrom`/`pausedUntil`; lead), so the nightly pass takes the person after 03:00 Lisbon time and their results are in before a Lisbon morning (the hourly submit already groups people by the hour their day ends). A switch day's rollover is at 03:00 in the new zone, and day keys only move forward, so no day is counted twice or lost. Writing or undoing a span stamps `habitRemindersChangedAt`; the pause row stamps `scheduledActionsChangedAt`. Decider: code (the model only writes the away span). Checks: `check:helm-whereabouts` (the offer only when offsets differ, once per span), `check:helm-habits` (Lisbon Dec 2–9: reminders at Lisbon hours, both switch days with no double or lost day), `check:helm-scheduled` (a run paused in the span and resumed; a zone span leaving `nextRunAt` on home time); a Waypoint trip in another zone offering once.

### Hands-free, for the whole area


Owner review 2: assume the person's hands are busy. What exists (section 20, issues 12 and 16): the follow-up window (8 s after a spoken reply, `isHandsFreeOn`), barge-in through the speech gate, the wake phrase on device recognition (`isWakeWordOn`, off by default), "Undo that". What is new:

- **Every question has a spoken form.** Each `QuestionShape` gains a code-built `spokenPrompt` (sealed with the question): pickOne reads up to 3 options with "or" ("Which one: morning pills, evening pills, or both?"), more than 3 says "Say its name, or 'list them'"; yesNo ends on the question; pickSeveral adds "say 'that's all' when done". Questions the model writes through `ask` get it by the same builder.
- **Answers matched on the device first.** `matchSpokenChoice(options, transcript)` (new, general, `src/lib/speech/spokenChoice.ts`, no app words; `check:pinch-voice`'s shared rule covers it): ordinals ("the first one", "the second"), "both", "all of them", "neither", "none", "skip", yes/no words, and option labels through `createOptionMatcher` with the option's aliases (Helm passes "morning" and "evening" for windows). A confident match answers through the same `resolveHelmQuestion` as a tap, with no model call; no match, or a match with extra words ("the evening one, and remind me tomorrow"), goes as a turn with `answersQuestion` set and the model reads it. `whichHabit`, `whatKindOfRepeat`, the clash question and every pattern's choices are pickOne, so all are answerable.
- **A reply that asks listens.** After a spoken reply whose turn left a question open, the same 8 s window opens (lead: 8 s, not 12), and the answer is tied to that question (a typed turn gets a written reply, so it never opens one). A message typed while a spoken conversation is open doesn't close its open question: it goes as its own turn, and the question stays answerable by tap or by the wake phrase for its 2 minutes (`OPEN_QUESTION_MS`). A typed turn with speech off opens nothing (the phone doesn't start listening to someone reading).
- **Barge-in on a question.** Speaking over a question being read stops it, and what was said is matched against that question first.
- **The wake phrase answers an open question.** The session keeps `openQuestion` (the newest unanswered question of its last turn, device state rebuilt from the capture) for 2 minutes (`OPEN_QUESTION_MS`); "Hey Nomos, the evening one" is matched against it before anything else. Needs `isWakeWordOn`; when hands-free is used and wake is off, Helm offers it once, saying that Chrome's recognition sends audio to Google unless the on-device model is downloaded (a product promise stays: off until turned on).
- **Taps stay**: every spoken question shows its buttons; nothing waits on voice.
- **Cost**: matching on the device costs nothing; an unmatched answer is a normal turn.
- **Ending it by saying so**: a closing phrase drops the open question and closes the microphone; the rules, the order against a question's own answers and the new `close` cue are section 20, "Ending a conversation by saying so".

### Connected apps: what Helm reads against what the person sees there


Owner review 2: a connected app is read as the full state the person sees in it. Audit of every share (section 19's table, the apps' `types.ts`). New categories are **off at connect** and **live** (read when asked, never stored) unless noted; each lands in Issue 124. What stays out everywhere: another member's private items, credentials and account numbers, and a buyer's or contact's email and phone (shown by the app, never sent to the model; Helm links to the app to reply).

| App | Shares now | Seen there, not read by Helm | The change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Going Once | `pickups`, `sales` (counts); drafts and withdrawn skipped; no price, buyer or note | Drafts, things with no price or photo, asking and floor price, condition, notes, status history, buyers' questions (`inquiries`: thing, name, message, New or Handled) | `things` (every thing, drafts included: title, sale, state, asking and floor price, condition, notes, last status change); `questions` (thing, buyer's first name, message, status, when; no email or phone); `sales` gains draft and not-ready counts. Issue 22 |
| A-List | `plannedShowings`, `savedTheaters`, `seenMovies` (rated only) | Watchlist, every seen movie, tickets bought (or not yet) for a planned showing, membership savings | `watchlist` (title, added, release); `seenMovies` all seen; `plannedShowings` gains `hasTicket`; `savings` (this year's total) |
| Nine Lives | `cats`, `vetVisits`, `dueCare`, `clinics`, `conditions` | Past visits and their notes, weights, symptoms, medications and preventives given, litter log, expenses, diet, insurance | `visits` (past, with reason and notes), `weights`, `symptoms`, `doses` (given), `expenses` (totals by month and cat); litter logs and insurance stay out (low use, high volume) |
| Pinch | `plannedMeals`, `shoppingList`, `useSoon` | Recipes and their ingredients, ideas, collections, pantry containers and packages, what was cooked when | `recipes` (title, ingredients, time, collection), `pantry` (containers and packages with dates), `cooked` (recipe, when) |
| Runway | `moneySummary`; off: `upcomingBills`, `horizon`, `transactions` | Envelopes and what's left in each, goals, account balances, card payments due | `envelopes` (name, left this period), `balances` (account name and balance, no numbers), `cardsDue` (card name, amount, due); both money categories off at connect |
| Waypoint | `trips`, `itinerary`, `myTasks` | Who owes whom on a trip, ideas, announcements, the whole Before the Road list, stays' check-in details | `balances` (what the person owes and is owed per trip), `ideas`, `announcements`, `checklist` (every item, assigned or not); stays gain check-in and check-out times |
| Worth the Wait | `boxCounts` (off) | Revealed items (unrevealed ones the person can't see either) | `revealedItems` (box, item name, revealed when); unrevealed stay counts |
| Google Calendar | `events` (title, time, calendar), plus place and format | Description, attendees | None now: descriptions are the main prompt-injection surface and attendees are other people's addresses; who is coming is read from Helm's own `who` links. Two-way sync is Issue 127 (below) |
| Gmail, Drive, Contacts | live search and read | nothing material | None |

A full-state share is still read only when the person asks something that needs it (or a context line names it), each live call is listed in the answer's `searched`, and `check:adapters` gains a row per new category proving the left-out fields are absent.

### Two-way sync with one chosen Google Calendar (Issue 127)


Today Helm reads the calendar (Issue 20) and, with Issue 67, writes one approved event at a time. The owner wants Helm's plan where they already look. Issue 127: the person picks one calendar (or lets Helm make one, "Helm"); Helm writes its plan slots, habit windows on Today and appointments (`meets`) there as events with an extended property `helmRef`, and reads changes back on the existing sync: a moved or deleted event moves or removes the slot (a habit window's move is offered as a pattern, never applied silently), and an event the person adds there is an ordinary calendar event. Needs the `calendar.events` scope on that one calendar's grant (sensitive, not restricted), the sink's `transformVersion`, conflict rule "the newer edit wins, with an Activity line", and Issue 67's write path.

### Your day: aims, plan and wind down (Issue 128)

The day card (morning and wind-down) is two views of one `DayAims` document; the server keeps it right when the app is closed.

- **The device does what carries no risk of a gap.** It draws the cards, files the person's answer through the normal turn (`createTask` or a link to an existing task; one Undo), writes the aims into the day's sealed payload, writes the tap's own result (Keep, Tomorrow, Later, Drop it, a reason), and stamps `dayAimsChangedAt` (a `HelmProfile` readable field, written in the same update) so the server looks soon. Everything below runs on `runHelmDayPlan` and never waits for the app to open ("Work that must happen on time happens on the server", `cloud-functions.md`).
- **The job** (`functions/src/apps/helm/dayPlan/`, a scheduled function that binds the keyring and no model key, by the exception in `cloud-functions.md` "A scheduled function for an app whose data is sealed holds no key, unless…": aims are sealed, and it opens only the stated fields: each aim's `taskId`, `order` and `windDown` results, never a task's words). It finds people through `dayQueue/{uid}` where `lookAt <= now`, plus the recent-change query, a lease per person, and recomputes what is due from the day documents, never trusting a stored `lookAt`. Four duties:
  - **Rollover.** At the day's end in the profile's zone, for each aim whose task is not `completed` and whose `windDown` choice is not `later` or `dropped`, it writes the next day's `DayAims` with the aim marked `broughtFrom` (an aim already sent to Tomorrow arrives brought); a task with no choice is listed in "From yesterday" by the device from `completedAt`, never moved. `rolledAt` makes it once. A missed rollover runs on the next pass, whatever the delay.
  - **Wind-down ready.** At the day's end minus 2 hours it sets `windDownState: 'ready'` (only when the day has aims, and not on a rest day with none kept), so the first open in that window shows the card. The card also shows if the device's clock is already past it, so a late pass never delays it.
  - **Overlap re-check.** On a calendar sync's change (the sync stamps the person's `dayQueue.lookAt` to now) it compares each kept aim's slot with the stored calendar copies; a kept aim that now overlaps gets `overlapsEventRef` on its `PlanDay` slot, readable, and the device words it "Now overlaps Brunch with Maya's parents" with Another time. It never moves a kept slot.
  - **Re-plan what no longer fits.** The vendored planner runs for the aims only and the unkept ones: a `suggested` slot that now collides with an event or a lost buffer is re-placed; a kept one is left as it is and offered another time as a new `suggested` slot, never replaced silently. It writes slots by dotted path, as the device does, so two writers never overwrite a whole map, and a re-plan that changes a slot the person just decided is dropped (the slot's `decidedAt` wins).
  After each duty it sets the next `lookAt`: the earliest of the day's end, the wind-down time, and the next day's start.
- **No push.** Both cards wait for the next open; nothing in the job sends a notification (it has no reminder hook).
- **The reason feeds Issue 113, never computed while the person waits.** The nightly reflective job (`BATCH_JOBS`, `whenWantedNow`) gains one read in the app's map: the last 14 days of `windDown` results with their reasons (opened in the batch's own read list, `private` honoured, a care task's reasons never read). Its candidate rules are unchanged (a pattern needs 2 days, a care candidate is ask-only, the back-off applies); "deep work slips after afternoon meetings" is a `plannerAvoid` pattern from it, and the next morning's `buildPlanInput` reads it through `avoidBuckets`. The card itself never computes a pattern. A tapped reason is a signal; a said one is read by code through the same fixed list.
- **Google Calendar, through Issue 127.** Only after the person has chosen the one calendar: a kept aim's slot is written as an event with `helmRef` by the existing sink (Issue 127's write path, one calendar's `calendar.events` grant); ticking the task marks the event done (title prefixed, `transparency` unchanged); Tomorrow moves it to tomorrow's slot or removes it when the day has no slot; Drop it removes it; a private task is "Busy" with no title. These go through the same sink as Issue 127's other writes, with its conflict rule and Activity line. Before a calendar is chosen the sink has no target and nothing is written.
- **Shown on Helm's own views.** Today and the strip draw aims as kept tasks; a past day keeps its `PlanDay` slots and the aims' `windDown`, shown as built with done read from `completedAt`.
- **Caps.** At most 12 aims a day (`HELM_CAPS.dayAims`; a longer list is asked "That's a lot for one day: which come first?"), 30 days of `DayAims` kept after their day then deleted by the weekly retention (`readPrunable`), a said reason's span id only. `firestore.rules` checks no fields in the beta tier; the caps are code's. Rules: `dayAims` is under the owner wildcard for `users/{uid}`; `dayQueue` has no rule match, proven as owner, another user and signed out.
- **Checks.** `check:helm-day-plan` (`scripts/checks/helmDayPlan.ts`, fixed clock): aims first and unplaced words ("doesn't fit"), a break after each task matching its effort (5/15/30) and the 15-minute buffer in every plan, an unknown effort planned as moderate, never more than the fill cap; a guessed length from the right past tasks and the fallback; rollover once across retries and a 2-day outage; wind-down `ready` at end minus 2 hours in two zones and across both clock changes; a kept aim never moved by the re-check; the job opening nothing but the stated fields and sending no push; no Google write before a calendar is chosen, and a private aim written as "Busy". `check:helm-where` gains rows for `dayAims` and `dayQueue`; `check:server-copies` covers the vendored planner; `check:helm-copy` covers the card's lines.

### Related things, grouped overnight (Issue 130)

The nightly reflective batch (Issue 113: `BATCH_JOBS`, the same run and key handling as `reflect`) gains one job, `relate`, so a person's later "moving" finds a project already built. No data access is added: it reads only what Helm already holds (tasks, topics, notes, projects, who and place records) and the connected categories the sink has already synced into Helm (Issue 124). Which of Going Once's and Runway's records are readable is that area's own decision (another PR adds it): when Issue 130 is built, read that PR to see what is readable and ask its session for any reasonable gap; this plan adds no adapter or category.

- **The batch test** (`BATCH_JOBS.relate`): nobody waits; the result waits for the next open; it is idempotent (a grouping's id is a hash of its sorted source keys, so a retry or a second arrival writes one); built on the server; writes only inside Helm (a `groupings` record or an Helm link, never another app's record or a calendar); standard batch terms. `whenWantedNow: 'answerAtOnce'`: "anything related to my move?" is answered at once by the turn from the groupings and links already stored, plus a live `search_app_records`.
- **Reads** (a row in the app's batch-read map and in `WHERE_THINGS_LIVE`): per source only its title, kind, app, dates and a short readable gist; never a note's body or a private thing's words. A `privacy` record is left out of the request unless Issue 103's rules allow it, and a care record never enters one. The job's input is capped (400 candidate sources a night, the newest and the most linked first).
- **Writes.** Results are validated by code: every source key must exist in what was sent, a grouping needs at least 2 sources from at least 2 places (kinds or apps), and a source can belong to one grouping only. Then code decides the path, never the model:
  - **Certain** (every source already links to, or names, one existing project or topic and the model's confidence is at least the bar, `GROUPING_CONFIDENCE`, and the evidence rests on a shared who, project, dated event or a title match code verifies): the new links are written by the normal link writer in one transaction, with an Activity line (`createdBy: 'nomos'`, Undo) and a next-open quiet line.
  - **Everything else** (a new project or group, a new parent topic, or a link under the bar) writes a `Grouping` in `state: 'proposed'` and nothing else; the third home card offers it. A new parent topic is always proposed.
- **The record.**
```ts
interface Grouping {                   // id = hash(sorted source keys)
  parent: { kind: 'project' | 'topic'; id: string | null; proposedTitle: string | null }; // id null until made
  sources: { key: string; app: string | null; title: string }[];  // key: 'tasks/{id}', 'topics/{id}', or a synced id; ≥3
  state: 'proposed' | 'filed' | 'groupedByYou' | 'notRelated' | 'notNow';
  sourceKeys: string[];                // every source's key, for the delete cascade ("Every fact has one home")
  proposedAt: number; answeredAt: number | null; nextOfferAfter: number | null;
  privacy: PrivacyClass;               // from the sources: any private source makes it private
}
```
  A grouping is derived and never edited by hand; accepting it makes the project or topic and writes each link once, through its home's writer, so the facts stay where they live. The grouping holds titles only to draw the card and cites each source; deleting a source removes it from the grouping (below 3 sources the grouping is dropped).
- **The offer is a learned offer.** `nextOfferAfter` follows `product.md`'s back-off exception (a Not now waits 4 weeks, then 8, a third ends it; Not related is final until new evidence, a source added since, arrives). At most one `proposed` grouping is offered per open (the card reads the oldest unanswered whose `nextOfferAfter` has passed); the rest stay `proposed`. A private grouping shows as "A private item" and is never named. With learning off, nothing is proposed.
- **Readers and the device.** A `groupings` listener (≤200 live) feeds the third card through `selectGroupingOffer`; Group them, Not related and Not now are one transaction each, with Undo, and work offline. The device never computes a grouping.
- **Checks.** `check:helm-grouping` (fixed fixtures): the id is stable across a retry and a second arrival; a source not in the request is refused; fewer than 2 sources or one place only is refused; certain links are quiet and carry an Undo, everything else only proposes, and a new parent topic is never made unasked; a private or care source never reaches the request and a private grouping is never named; at most one offer per open and the back-off steps; "Not related" stays after a new night's run with no new evidence; no write outside Helm. `check:batch-jobs` covers the registry entry; `check:helm-where` gains rows for `groupings` and the `relate` read.

### What the assistant gains


- **Context**: `<habits>` (alias `h#`, name, windows with labels, cadence words, today's state per window, the who for a carer's habit, the goal, the stage word and the reminder state with its pace and next time, from readable fields, so "how's my walk going?" and "are you still reminding me?" need no long read; ≤30 running habits, retired ones only by name for `bringBack`); `<patterns>` (alias `pt#`, statement, state, evidence line, family, and a guardrail's reasons; ≤20 not off); `<importance>` (≤30 placed entries in order with the person's words and day, and the "Always kept" items by alias only).
- **Commands** (section 5): `createHabit`, `updateHabit` (`remindOn` with a pace, `remindOff`, `retire`, `bringBack`, a pause with an end), `checkIn`, `setPatternState` (`option`), `setImportance`, and `createProject` gains `kind` and `why`. No command creates a pattern, sets its evidence, writes a reason, judges a stage, turns on a lock-screen name, computes a rank or places a health or care item.
- **Tools**: `search_habits` (a habit's checks for a range, as counts by week and window, ≤12 weeks, with its kept weeks and stage history; a retired habit's `formedSummary`) and `search_patterns` (off and retired included; guardrails and habit-life offers too).
- **Answers the stage and the order make possible**: "is my floss formed yet?" (the stage and the weeks behind it, worded as Helm's judgment), "what matters most to me?" (the order in a sentence), "why did you suggest pausing Italian?" (the guardrail's reasons and that Italian was lowest-placed).
- **Owner review 1.** Context: `<said>` (alias `o#`, the newest 30 observations of 14 days: subject alias, tags, the day; quotes only through the tool), `<research>` (`r#`, title, status, `offerOn`), `<where_things_live>` (the map's thing → tool lines), and a `<fit>` line for a question about a date range. Commands: `saveForLater`, `offerResearch`, `scheduleAction`; `checkIn` with `candidateAliases` and `noteSpanId`; the why's three fields. Tools: `search_said` (observations for a subject or range with their quotes, ≤40), `search_research`, `list_scheduled_actions`. Answers it makes possible: "what did I say about piano last week?", "what did I want to learn?", "what are you doing for me on Sundays?".
- **Owner review 2**. Context: `<personality>`, `<how_you_seem>`, `<whereabouts>`; `file_results.intent`. Commands: `setPersonality`, `correctSeenAs`, `removeSeenAs`, `upsertWho` with `home`/`away`, `createTask`/`updateTask` with `meetsHow`. Tools: the new live categories above through `search_app_records`. Answers: "how do you see me?", "where will I be next Friday?", "is anyone asking about the couch?".

### What Helm keeps, and for how long


Retention by kind, in code (`readPrunable`, pure, `engine/retention.ts`), run by the device's daily check once a week, at most 400 deletes a run (a year's backlog clears in a few weeks):

| Kind | Kept | Then |
|---|---|---|
| Observations | 26 weeks (`OBSERVATION_KEEP_WEEKS`) | deleted, unless a live pattern's evidence cites it |
| Patterns, live (`isLive`: not off, not retired) | while live | the listener reads them (cap 500); past 500 the oldest live by `lastEvidenceAt` is retired with its answer kept |
| Patterns stopped or "never again" (`offReason` set) | the signature, forever | lines and evidence cleared, `isLive` false; read one-shot by the daily check (≤5,000, oldest by `offAt` dropped past that, so a very old "no" could be offered once more) |
| Reflections | 14 days after `seenAt` | deleted |
| Seen-as lines | ≤40 shown or corrected; ≤200 removed (the tombstones that keep a line from returning) | the oldest derived `shown` line goes first; a correction is never pruned |
| Activity (executions) | 13 months; the list shows 90 days, older on "Show earlier" | deleted (Undo is long gone) |

Decider: code. Check: new `check:helm-retention` (a 27-week observation cited by a live pattern kept and one not cited deleted, a stopped pattern cut to its signature and still blocking, the live cap of 500, seen-as caps, executions at 13 months).

### Checks


`check:helm-habits` (fixed clocks: the rollover, a window past midnight, both DST nights in two zones, each cadence, a tally from two devices, Monday-to-today weeks, day parts from Your day and the fixed fallback; round 3: kept, unkept and skipped weeks with `isInUse`, each stage step at product's thresholds, Formed held back by a `reminded` before the tick and by the 10-week floor, a paused week skipped, a health habit stopping at Holding, a carer's habit with no stage, the device and the server judging one week once), `check:helm-reminders` (pure: each pace and stage's fire time, a window going quiet after 2 self-kept weeks, a tally's middle-of-day line, quiet hours moving or dropping a time, bundling within 15 minutes, the cap of 6, a zone change, both DST nights, the diff leaving a slot within 60 seconds alone, the idle rule on and off, templates reading only readable values), `check:helm-reminders-server` (both run bodies on the Firestore emulator, listed under "Reminders"), `check:helm-importance` (each relation's rank, "matters most, then work" in order, two sharing a place asking nothing, three asking, a pick, Skip, a renumbering keeping ties and order, `until` passing, a health habit refused, a habit following its goal, dates ordering the unplaced), `check:helm-patterns` (O's Tuesday evenings, N's rotating nights through the `shift` bucket, C's care habit staying ask-only, a no and its back-off, evidence counted only after the no, the third no, stop, retire and reopen, a day with no use left out; the formed offer a week after Formed and "Keep counting" as a no, `reminderEase` for a health and a carer's habit), `check:helm-guardrails` (one reason not shown, two kinds shown, one deadline shown, a task only on its own deadline, `reasons` listing each, the lowest-placed eligible named, nothing due within 14 days, health and care never named, nothing eligible showing nothing, `starved` read from the order, load evidence coming back only stronger or changed, nothing in the first 7 days, the switch off), `check:helm-copy` (no sealed field in a reminder template; `lockScreenName` only in the hook's push), `check:server-copies` (the Helm vendor files), new rows in `check:helm-evidence` (goals), `check:helm-undo` (a voice check-in, a pattern Undo, a letting-go's Undo with its moment and stamp, a guardrail's pause of a goal and its habits, a `setImportance` and a renumbering) and `check:helm-rules` (the three collections, `apps/helm/reminderQueue/{uid}` denied to the owner, another user and signed out, and the central `reminders` rule unchanged: the owner may cancel their own Helm reminder, another user can't read, cancel or address one to them).
